# Requirements Document

## Introduction

RuleTrace is a competition rulebook compiler. RuleTrace transforms competition regulations into structured, typed, traceable, executable rules. RuleTrace then evaluates competition data against those rules and produces reproducible decisions. Each decision carries an explanation and references to the original source clauses.

The first version answers questions of these kinds: Is a participant eligible under a stated condition? How is a score calculated? How are ties broken, and is a tie left explicit when no criterion separates participants? How are participants ranked? Does a published result equal the result computed from the rules? The first version answers only questions that its Rule kinds can express (Requirement 14).

RuleTrace never invents a rule and never treats an ambiguous rule as having a definite interpretation. The compiler is the product. This specification excludes any dashboard, chatbot, or hosted service, with the single exception of the local RuleTrace_Laboratory (Requirements 36 and 37; UD-38). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

RuleTrace also contains an optional, first-class, AI-assisted rule proposal pipeline (Requirements 18, 23, and 28 to 30). The pipeline is disabled by default, lives in Auxiliary_Crates outside the deterministic core, and runs only in the separate executable ruletrace-ai, and sends clause text to an AI provider only when the user supplies an explicit TOML configuration file and gives a per-invocation consent that names the documents sent. The pipeline emits untrusted Proposals. Every Proposal passes strict parsing, provenance verification, and the same deterministic semantic validation as a manually authored Rule, and a human Reviewer must explicitly approve it through the review command group of the deterministic ruletrace executable, which records the approval in a separate Approval_Ledger. AI never approves, compiles, or executes a rule. The compiler works completely without AI credentials, network access, or provider libraries. In short, AI helps understand the rules, and RuleTrace verifies and executes them.

### Pipeline

RuleTrace separates seven responsibilities, each with its own declared typed inputs and typed outputs. Data flows only along the typed values listed in the following table. A stage consumes every typed input that the table lists for it, and a listed input may be the output of any earlier stage or an explicit caller-supplied value. No stage reads or writes undocumented shared state, a mutable global, or an implicit environmental input such as an environment variable, the working directory, the locale, the clock, or a random source. Requirement 24 makes these obligations testable.

1. Source ingestion preserves the original document structure.
2. Extraction produces candidate clauses with source locations.
3. The Rule IR holds rules in a typed, versioned form. The review state of each Rule_Revision is derived from a separate Approval_Ledger and is never stored in the Rulebook.
4. Semantic validation checks types, missing values, conflicts, unsupported operations, and incomplete interpretations.
5. Compilation produces a deterministic executable representation, only from approved rules, and not while any Rule_Revision is in an Unresolved_Approval_State.
6. Execution evaluates the compiled rules over competition data. Execution produces Outcomes and Decision_Traces that hold only Locations and document hashes. Execution reads no file and no source text.
7. Explanation resolves, verifies, and renders each Decision_Trace against Source_Models that the caller has already loaded into memory. Explanation runs in a component separate from the Rules_Engine. This component is the provenance component of RuleTrace and is called the Explainer.

| Stage | Responsibility | Typed inputs | Typed outputs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Source ingestion | Source_Document bytes, document identifier, effective size limit | Source_Model, or Diagnostics |
| 2 | Extraction | Source_Model, extraction mode | Candidate_Clauses, Diagnostics |
| 3 | Rule_IR and Approval_Ledger | For parsing a Rulebook: Rule_IR document bytes, effective size limit, effective nesting depth limit. For parsing an Approval_Ledger: ledger bytes, effective size limit. For an Approval_Status operation (review command group): Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Review_Decision, Source_Models | Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, or Diagnostics |
| 4 | Semantic validation | Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Source_Models, optional Candidate_Clauses | Validation_Result |
| 5 | Compilation | Validation_Result | Compiled_Rulebook, or Diagnostics |
| 6 | Execution | Compiled_Rulebook, Competition_Data, Questions, optional Published_Result | Outcomes, Decision_Traces, Diagnostics |
| 7 | Explanation | Decision_Trace, pre-loaded Source_Models | Verification status, rendered Decision_Trace, Diagnostics |

The optional AI-assisted proposal pipeline adds two Auxiliary stages outside the seven stages. AI proposal generation consumes Candidate_Clauses, Source_Models, the type declarations of a Rulebook, and an AI_Configuration, and produces Proposals or Diagnostics. Review is performed by the review command group of the ruletrace executable. Review consumes a Proposal_Store entry, a Review_Decision, a Rulebook, and an Approval_Ledger, and produces an appended Approval_Ledger, a Rulebook that adopts the approved Rule_Revision, and an updated Proposal_Store reference. Requirement 24 states the complete contract of every stage.

### Demonstration applications (first-version capability) and future full applications (not first-version capability)

The first version contains two thin demonstration Workflow_Modules (also named Application_Layers), Prize_Check and Bracket_Check (Requirements 31 and 32), which prove that one Rules_Engine powers two different workflows and which the RuleTrace_Laboratory presents as the selectable Prize workflow and Tournament standings workflow (Requirement 36 criterion 18). Each Application_Layer maps its own input format to Competition_Data, calls the shared Rules_Engine through the library interface, and renders a report. Each Application_Layer contains input mapping and report layout only, and no eligibility, scoring, ranking, or tie-breaking logic (Requirement 15 criteria 5 and 6). Prize_Check demonstrates eligibility, weighted scoring, ranking with Tie_Groups, and a published-result consistency check. Bracket_Check demonstrates standings (ranking with ordered tie-breaking criteria), qualification as defined by the Qualification_Predicate of Requirement 14 criterion 11, Tie_Groups, and a published-standings consistency check. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

The RuleTrace_Laboratory (Requirements 36 and 37; UD-38) is one integrated local application, served by the executable ruletrace-lab on the loopback interface only. The Laboratory is a functional interface to RuleTrace and not a substitute for it: it orchestrates the executables ruletrace and ruletrace-ai as subprocesses, displays Proposals, findings, approval history, Outcomes, and proof, and decides, approves, and computes nothing itself. The Laboratory holds no AI credential and has no path that writes the Approval_Ledger. An approval needs an explicit human action, a Reviewer identity, and confirmation of the exact Rule_Revision and Rule_Content_Hash. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

The future full applications Prize Check and Bracket Check are not first-version capability. Prize allocation, slot-based qualification (the allocation of a limited number of places, slots, or stages), seeding, bracket generation, tournament progression, and multi-stage competitions remain outside the First_Version_Support_Boundary and need new Rule kinds, which need a revision of this specification (Requirement 15). The first-version Application_Layers are not the full applications and are not to be presented as such (Requirement 34). A reproducible end-to-end demonstration (Requirement 33) shows the complete flow from a real public rulebook to a verified decision.

### Rule language decision

The first version uses a constrained, declarative rule language serialized as a versioned data format. The language is preferred over arbitrary executable code because every construct can be typed, validated, bounded, and traced to a source clause. A general-purpose language is excluded unless a later decision records a compelling reason (see Unresolved Decisions).

### Scope

In scope for the first version:

- A Cargo workspace of Rust crates covering the seven pipeline stages, with three runtime executables: the deterministic ruletrace (the CLI), which has four pipeline commands and the review command group, the separate optional ruletrace-ai (Requirement 27), and the local RuleTrace_Laboratory ruletrace-lab (Requirements 27, 36, and 37; UD-38), plus the library packages prize-check and bracket-check (Requirement 31), which have no binary target. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- Two thin demonstration Workflow_Modules (Application_Layers), Prize_Check and Bracket_Check, limited to input mapping, Question construction, and report presentation over the shared Rules_Engine (Requirements 15, 31, and 32). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- The local RuleTrace_Laboratory ruletrace-lab: one integrated local application with a loopback browser interface in front of ruletrace and ruletrace-ai, with the Prize workflow and the Tournament standings workflow, an offline demo mode, and the local security requirements of Requirement 37 (UD-38). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- A documented, reproducible end-to-end demonstration over real public competition documents, with committed fixtures, a recorded provider response, a runbook, a proof report, and a demonstration acceptance test (Requirement 33), and honest-claims rules for all demonstration material (Requirement 34).
- Ingestion of plain-text and Markdown Source_Documents with source spans, and library-level extraction of Candidate_Clauses, also available through the ingest command.
- Structured Rule_IR input in a versioned data format, referencing source spans, with Rule revisions, Origin metadata, a derived review state, and a separate append-only Approval_Ledger (Requirements 25 and 26).
- Semantic validation including the decidable subset of static conflict detection (Requirement 6), atomic compilation, deterministic execution, and decision traces.
- Rule kinds in the First_Version_Support_Boundary (Requirement 14).
- An optional, disabled-by-default, AI-assisted rule proposal pipeline in Auxiliary_Crates outside the deterministic core (Requirements 18 and 23): a provider-independent AI_Adapter, at least one real AI_Provider in a separate AI_Integration_Crate, a deterministic Mock_Provider for automated tests, Clause_Context with document identifiers, spans, and hashes, a structured Proposal_Schema, strict parsing and schema validation, provenance verification, deterministic semantic validation, human review through the review command group of ruletrace, rejection, revision handling, per-invocation transmission consent, a deterministic retry policy, and explicit failure behavior.
- A Proposal_Store of Proposals, and the review command group of the ruletrace executable (list, approve, reject), which is the only code path that appends to the Approval_Ledger. This supersedes the earlier separate Review_Tool (UD-22).
- Explicit typed pipeline contracts and an automatically verified crate dependency graph (Requirement 24).
- Automated tests, formatting, linting, and continuous integration on a defined platform matrix.

Out of scope for the first version:

- Automatic approval of any rule by AI, compilation or execution of any rule by AI, and any AI component inside the deterministic core (the Core_Crates).
- Automatic extraction of rules from free text, including heuristic or statistical extraction, other than the AI proposal pipeline of Requirement 23. The AI proposal pipeline proposes rules only from Candidate_Clauses of plain-text or Markdown Source_Documents, and every Proposal needs human approval before it can become executable.
- PDF, HTML, spreadsheet, or image ingestion.
- The full Prize Check and Bracket Check applications, their user interfaces (other than the single local RuleTrace_Laboratory of Requirements 36 and 37), and their domain-specific workflows beyond the thin Application_Layers of Requirements 31 and 32; and any prize allocation, slot-based qualification, seeding, bracket generation, progression, or multi-stage competition logic in any crate, including the Application_Layers. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- PDF ingestion for the demonstration: a demonstration document that is published as PDF is converted to plain text or Markdown manually by the user, and the conversion is recorded (Requirement 33 criterion 5).
- Dashboards, chatbots, web services operated by RuleTrace, hosted application services, cloud dashboards, user accounts and remote authentication services, hosted databases, general-purpose workflow services, databases, microservices, and any replacement of the compiler or the Rules_Engine by user-interface logic. The single local RuleTrace_Laboratory (Requirements 36 and 37) is the only user interface in scope. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- Support for arbitrary or universal rulebooks.
- Currency conversion, time-zone handling, and localization.
- Training, fine-tuning, or hosting of AI models, and any AI provider integration beyond the one real integration crate for the Responses protocol that Requirement 23 criterion 29 requires (UD-17, UD-37). Its first Provider_Profile is the Meta Model API. The OpenAI Responses API is the second profile of the same crate. Chat Completions, Modal and custom endpoints are not integrated.

Deferred beyond the first version:

- Authentication of Reviewer identity or of Approval_Records (see R-8).
- Additional real AI_Providers beyond the first.

### Proposed vertical slice

The smallest meaningful end-to-end slice takes a Source_Document, a structured Rule_IR file, an Approval_Ledger file, and a Competition_Data file, and runs the four CLI commands in order. The slice validates the rules, compiles them, executes them, and writes an auditable Decision_Trace that resolves to source spans in an ingested Source_Document. Requirement 16 defines the slice, including a second slice in which the Rules are proposed through the Mock_Provider by ruletrace-ai and approved by a Reviewer through ruletrace review.

## Glossary

- **RuleTrace**: The complete compiler and execution system described by this document.
- **Source_Document**: A rulebook text file supplied as input, in plain-text or Markdown format.
- **Location**: The single authoritative type that says where something is. A Location is exactly one of five variants: Source_Span, Document_Location, Input_Location, Cli_Location, or No_Location. Rules, Diagnostics, and Decision_Trace entries carry Locations. No other location type exists. The term SourceRef is retired and means a Location.
- **Source_Span**: The Location variant that references a contiguous range of an ingested Source_Document, with the document identifier, the start byte offset (inclusive), the end byte offset (exclusive), and the 1-based line and column of the start. A Source_Span exists only when it was derived from a Source_Model. It is a variant of Location and not a separate type.
- **Document_Location**: The Location variant that references a whole Source_Document by document identifier, for use where no valid span exists.
- **Input_Location**: The Location variant that references a non-Source_Document input (a Rule_IR file, a Competition_Data file, a Source_Model file, or a Candidate_Clause list), by input identifier, with an optional 1-based line and column and an optional field name.
- **Cli_Location**: The Location variant that references a command-line argument by its name or its position.
- **No_Location**: The Location variant that states that no location exists, with a mandatory reason code from the code catalogue.
- **Ingestor**: The component that reads a Source_Document and produces a Source_Model.
- **Source_Model**: The structured representation of a Source_Document that preserves headings, numbering, paragraphs, list items, and Source_Spans, and records the content hash of the document.
- **Candidate_Clause**: A portion of a Source_Model proposed as the origin of a rule, together with its Source_Span. A Candidate_Clause is not a Rule and has no Approval_Status.
- **Extractor**: The component that produces Candidate_Clauses from a Source_Model.
- **Rule_IR**: The typed, versioned intermediate representation of rules and their review state.
- **Rulebook**: A named, versioned collection of Rule_IR rules, type declarations, and references to Source_Documents. Each reference declares the document identifier and the SHA-256 content hash of the document.
- **Rule**: A single declarative element of a Rulebook, with an identifier, a Revision_Number, a kind, a Target, typed operands, a derived Approval_Status, an Origin, and one or more Source_Spans. Wherever this specification requires a Diagnostic or a Decision_Trace entry to identify a Rule, the identification consists of the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number.
- **Rule_Revision**: One version of a Rule, identified by the pair of the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number. Each Rule_Revision has its own Approval_Status and its own Approval_Records.
- **Revision_Number**: A positive integer that starts at 1 for the first Rule_Revision of a Rule identifier and increases by exactly 1 for each further Rule_Revision, with no gap and no reuse.
- **Effective_Revision**: For each Rule identifier, the Rule_Revision with the highest Revision_Number among the Rule_Revisions whose derived Approval_Status is Approved (Requirement 25 criterion 11). The Compiler compiles only Effective_Revisions. An Approved Rule_Revision that is not the Effective_Revision is superseded. Superseded is derived and is not an Approval_Status.
- **Origin**: The provenance metadata of a Rule_Revision. The Origin is exactly one of Manual or AI_Proposal. The Origin is not part of Rule_Content and has no effect on validation, compilation, execution, or any executable artifact.
- **Rule_Content**: The part of a Rule_Revision that Requirement 25 criterion 4 defines as the content that an Approval_Record approves.
- **Rule_Content_Hash**: The SHA-256 hash of the canonical serialization of Rule_Content, encoded as 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.
- **Target**: The declared name that a Rule defines, such as an eligibility name, a derived value name, a score name, a ranking name, or the input field that a default value Rule covers.
- **IR_Version**: The version identifier of the Rule_IR format.
- **Approval_Status**: The review state of a Rule_Revision, derived from the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger and never stored in a Rulebook, a Proposal_Store entry, or a Compiled_Rulebook (Requirement 26 criterion 10). The values are Proposed, Approved, and Rejected. The only operations are the append of an approval record (Proposed to Approved) and the append of a rejection record (Proposed to Rejected) (Requirement 5). Approval_Status is the single canonical lifecycle field, and no independent flag such as is_approved or is_rejected exists (Requirement 22 criterion 17).
- **Approval_Record**: A Ledger_Record of kind approval in the Approval_Ledger. It names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Reviewer, the approval time as a value supplied by the caller, the Rule_Content_Hash of the exact Rule_Content approved, the IR_Version, and the Approval_Binding of document hashes. An Approval_Record is non-matching for a Rule_Revision when any of the bound values differs from the current value of that Rule_Revision or of the Rulebook (Requirement 26 criterion 8).
- **Approval_Ledger**: The separate, append-only, canonically serialized file of Ledger_Records, ordered by sequence number and protected by a hash chain (Requirement 26). It is not part of the Rulebook and is supplied to validation as an explicit typed input.
- **Ledger_Record**: One entry of the Approval_Ledger, either an Approval_Record or a rejection record. A rejection record names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Rule_Content_Hash, the Reviewer, a caller-supplied time, and a reason text.
- **Approval_Binding**: The sorted list of pairs of document identifier and SHA-256 document hash, for every Source_Document referenced by a Source_Span of the approved Rule_Revision, recorded in an Approval_Record together with the IR_Version.
- **Unresolved_Approval_State**: The condition of a Rule_Revision whose derived Approval_Status is Proposed and for which no Rule_Revision of the same Rule identifier with a higher Revision_Number has the derived Approval_Status Approved or Rejected. It is reported as a Diagnostic of Severity Error with the code APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED (Requirement 26 criterion 11).
- **Warning_Diagnostic**: A Diagnostic with Severity Warning. A Warning_Diagnostic never changes compilability, the derived flags of Requirement 22, or an exit code.
- **Diagnostic**: A structured message with a stable code, a severity, a message, a primary Location, and an ordered list of related Locations that may be empty.
- **Severity**: The Diagnostic level. The values are Error, Warning, Ambiguity, and Unsupported.
- **Blocking_Diagnostic**: A Diagnostic with Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported. A Diagnostic of an Unresolved_Approval_State (APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED) and the Diagnostic APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES have Severity Error and are therefore Blocking_Diagnostics.
- **Diagnostic_State**: The single canonical diagnostic state of a validation or compilation result, defined as the ordered list of Diagnostics, each with a Severity. The flags has_errors and no_errors and the validation passed or failed result are derived from the Diagnostic_State only.
- **Validator**: The component that performs semantic validation of a Rulebook against caller-supplied Source_Models.
- **Validation_Result**: The Rulebook together with the Approval_Ledger as supplied and the Diagnostic_State produced by the Validator.
- **Compiler**: The component that translates a Validation_Result with no Blocking_Diagnostic into a Compiled_Rulebook.
- **Compiled_Rulebook**: The deterministic executable representation of the Effective_Revisions of a validated Rulebook. A Compiled_Rulebook owns all of its state.
- **Rules_Engine**: The application-neutral component that executes a Compiled_Rulebook over Competition_Data. The Rules_Engine performs no file, network, clock, random, or environment access and receives no Source_Model.
- **Explainer**: The component of stage 7, in a crate separate from the Rules_Engine, that resolves, verifies, and renders a Decision_Trace against caller-supplied, pre-loaded Source_Models. The Explainer is the provenance component of RuleTrace.
- **Competition_Data**: The typed input records (participants, attributes, scores, results) that the Rules_Engine evaluates.
- **Outcome**: The result of evaluating one question. The values are Decided (with a value) and Indeterminate (with reasons).
- **Question**: A single item to be decided that is supplied to the Rules_Engine with Competition_Data, such as the eligibility of one participant or the ranking of a participant set. The Rules_Engine returns one Outcome per Question.
- **Tie_Group**: A set of two or more participants that are equal on the ranking key and on every declared tie-breaking criterion, returned as one Decided value in which every member has the same rank position. A Tie_Group is a set and carries no internal order.
- **Rank_Position**: For a participant in a ranking, the value 1 plus the number of participants that are strictly ahead of that participant.
- **External_Ranking_Value**: A ranking value that an outside authority issues (for example a pre-event seeding list that a competition uses as a late tie-breaker) and that Competition_Data supplies as an explicit input field together with its ranking authority, its edition or effective date, and its provenance. An External_Ranking_Value is not a Rank_Position and is never read by the rank reference operation of Requirement 14 criterion 12.
- **Published_Result**: A previously published decision or ranking supplied for comparison with a computed Outcome.
- **Core_Crate**: A crate that implements a pipeline stage, the Rule_IR, the Rules_Engine, or the shared Diagnostic, Location, and Exact_Number types, as opposed to an Auxiliary_Crate, an application crate, or a test-only crate. The deterministic core consists of the Core_Crates. The ruletrace binary crate (the CLI, including the review command group) is a Core_Crate that performs file input and output and no other pipeline function.
- **Auxiliary_Crate**: A crate outside the deterministic core that supports the optional AI proposal pipeline. The Auxiliary_Crates are the AI_Adapter crate, each AI_Integration_Crate, the Mock_Provider crate, and the AI_Tool crate. A Core_Crate has no dependency on any Auxiliary_Crate. The review library crate (the Approval_Status operations, the Approval_Ledger append function, and the review listing) is a Core_Crate.
- **AI_Crate**: An Auxiliary_Crate that is the AI_Adapter crate, an AI_Integration_Crate, the Mock_Provider crate, or the AI_Tool crate.
- **Pipeline_Contract**: The declaration of all typed inputs and typed outputs of one stage, as listed in the Pipeline table of the Introduction and in Requirement 24.
- **Decision_Trace**: The inspectable record of an evaluation, listing applicable Rules, Locations, input values, calculation steps, and the Outcome. A Decision_Trace holds Locations and document hashes and holds no Source_Document text.
- **Exact_Number**: A numeric value that is exact, has no floating-point representation, and has one canonical serialization. The internal representation is selected under UD-3, which is preferred as bounded rational and pending design documentation: Option D is a bounded decimal, and Option R is a bounded rational with decimal Rule_IR literals. The literal syntax and the literal limits are identical under both options (Requirement 8 criterion 27).
- **Exact_Number_Limits**: The representable range of an Exact_Number under the selected UD-3 option. Option D: a coefficient of at most 38 decimal digits and a scale from 0 to 28. Option R-128: a numerator magnitude of at most 2^127 - 1 and a denominator from 1 to 2^127 - 1. Option R-Big: a numerator magnitude and a denominator each of at most 2^256 - 1. Exactly one limit applies after the design documents the complete comparison of Requirement 8 criterion 19 and the user confirms UD-3 and the sub-option.
- **Missing_Value**: The explicit absence of a required input value in Competition_Data.
- **Conflict**: A situation in which two or more Rules apply to the same subject and produce incompatible results, and no declared precedence resolves them. Requirement 6 defines the Conflict classes that the first version detects statically.
- **Precedence_Declaration**: An explicit Rule_IR element, which is not a Rule kind, that totally orders two or more Rules that define the same Target, with its own Source_Span.
- **AI_Boundary**: The optional boundary between the Core_Crates and any AI provider. The AI_Boundary consists of the AI_Adapter interface, the Proposal_Schema, and the trust rules of Requirement 18.
- **AI_Adapter**: The provider-independent typed interface, defined in an Auxiliary_Crate, through which the AI_Tool sends a Provider_Request to an AI_Provider and receives a Provider_Response or a Provider_Failure.
- **AI_Provider**: A concrete implementation of the AI_Adapter. An AI_Provider is either a real provider in an AI_Integration_Crate or the Mock_Provider.
- **AI_Integration_Crate**: An Auxiliary_Crate that implements the AI_Adapter for one real provider protocol and contains all network, credential, and provider-library code. The first is `rt-ai-responses`, which implements the OpenAI Responses protocol shared by the Meta and OpenAI Provider_Profiles (UD-37).
- **Provider_Profile**: The documented constants and data of one provider identifier in the AI_Tool crate (not in an AI_Integration_Crate): the official endpoint host, the protocol, the default Capabilities, the accepted reasoning effort values, the `max_output_tokens` constant, and the documentation URLs. A Provider_Profile holds no network or credential code (Requirement 35).
- **Capability**: A named property of a provider and model, such as strict JSON Schema structured output, with the value yes, no, or unknown. An unknown required Capability fails closed (Requirement 35 criterion 3).
- **Provider_Identity**: The provider identifier, the protocol, and the adapter version of the AI_Provider that produced a Provider_Response.
- **Reported_Metadata**: The model name, response identifier, and token usage that a provider reports in its response. It is recorded for audit and is never authoritative (Requirement 35 criterion 15).
- **Model_Catalogue**: The list of model identifiers that a provider returns for the `models` command of ruletrace-ai (Requirement 35 criterion 18).
- **Mock_Provider**: A deterministic AI_Provider, in its own Auxiliary_Crate, that returns scripted Provider_Responses and scripted Provider_Failures, and uses no network, clock, or random source.
- **AI_Tool**: The separate executable ruletrace-ai and its library, in an Auxiliary_Crate, that builds a Provider_Request, invokes an AI_Provider, and runs the proposal checks of Requirement 23. The AI_Tool is not the CLI, has no dependency on any crate that can write the Approval_Ledger, and writes only the Proposal_Store and the Transmission_Log.
- **Review_Tool**: Retired by UD-22. Superseded by Review_Group.
- **Review_Group**: The review command group of the ruletrace executable, with exactly the subcommands list, approve, and reject. The Review_Group is the only executable code path that appends to the Approval_Ledger, and it has no dependency on any AI_Crate or network library.
- **ruletrace**: The deterministic executable. It has the four pipeline commands and the Review_Group (Requirement 27).
- **ruletrace-ai**: The separate, optional executable that is the AI_Tool (Requirement 27).
- **Transmission_Consent**: A per-invocation argument of ruletrace-ai that names each Source_Document whose text may be sent to the AI_Provider in that invocation (Requirement 28 criterion 7).
- **Transmission_Log**: The append-only file of ruletrace-ai that records each transmission without document text, response text, or credential (Requirement 28 criterion 8).
- **Request_Identifier**: The SHA-256 hash of the canonical bytes of a Provider_Request, encoded as 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters (Requirement 29 criterion 7).
- **Transient_Failure**: A Provider_Failure that the retry policy of Requirement 29 criterion 2 may retry: a timeout, a connection refusal, a connection reset or other unavailability, an HTTP 429 response, or an HTTP 5xx response.
- **Protocol_Failure**: A failure of the transport, the response schema, or the provenance verification of a Provider_Response, which rejects the complete batch (Requirement 30 criterion 1).
- **Run_Summary**: The deterministic text that a ruletrace-ai propose run writes to standard output after it has built at least one Provider_Request. It states the run as complete or incomplete and lists, in ascending order of start byte offset, the Candidate_Clause identifiers (document identifier, start byte offset, end byte offset) in three sets: processed (the Candidate_Clauses of every Provider_Request that completed steps 1 to 11, including a batch that returned AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED), stored (the processed Candidate_Clauses whose batch this run wrote to the Proposal_Store, so that stored is a subset of processed), and unprocessed (every other requested Candidate_Clause) (Requirement 23 criterion 36).
- **Semantic_Outcome**: The result of deterministic semantic validation of a well-formed, provenance-verified Proposal, such as an Ambiguity, Unsupported, or Error Diagnostic. A Semantic_Outcome is not a Protocol_Failure (Requirement 30 criterion 2).
- **AI_Configuration**: The explicit, versioned TOML configuration file that enables ruletrace-ai and names the AI_Provider, the model, the endpoint, the name of the credential environment variable, the limits, and the transmission settings (Requirement 28). No AI_Configuration means AI is disabled.
- **Clause_Context**: The typed input that the AI_Tool supplies to an AI_Provider for a task. The Clause_Context contains, for each supplied Candidate_Clause, the document identifier, the document SHA-256 content hash, the start and end byte offsets, the 1-based start line and column, and the exact clause text, together with the vocabulary of the First_Version_Support_Boundary.
- **Provider_Request**: The serialized request that the AI_Tool sends through the AI_Adapter. A Provider_Request consists of a Clause_Context, the task kind, and the limits from the AI_Configuration.
- **Provider_Response**: The raw bytes that an AI_Provider returns for a Provider_Request, which are untrusted until they pass the checks of Requirement 23.
- **Provider_Failure**: A typed failure returned by an AI_Provider instead of a Provider_Response. The Provider_Failure kinds are unavailable, timeout, rate-limited, authentication-failed, TLS-failed, and other provider error.
- **Proposal_Schema**: The versioned, typed data schema that defines the only accepted structure of a Provider_Response (Requirement 23 criterion 4).
- **Proposal_Store**: The set of files, in a versioned data format and without a database, that holds Proposals, their Origin metadata, their checking Diagnostics, and a reference to the Ledger_Record of each review decision. The Proposal_Store holds no approval flag and no stored Approval_Status.
- **Review_Decision**: A typed value that names a Rule_Revision, a Reviewer, a caller-supplied time, the Rule_Content_Hash that the Reviewer saw, and the decision (approve or reject) with a reason text for a rejection. A Review_Decision yields a Ledger_Record only through the Review_Group.
- **Pending_Proposal**: A Proposal in the Proposal_Store that yields a Rule_Revision to which no Ledger_Record refers and that no review approve has adopted into the Rulebook. A Pending_Proposal is not a Rule_Revision of the Rulebook, has no entry in the Rulebook, and is therefore different from a Proposed Rule_Revision inside the Rulebook (Requirement 26 criteria 28 and 29).
- **Proposal**: A typed suggestion produced from a Provider_Response, such as a candidate rule, relationship, conflict, or test. A Proposal is untrusted until a Reviewer approves the Rule_Revision that it yields, and only a structured rule suggestion can yield a Rule_Revision.
- **Reviewer**: A human who approves, edits, or rejects Rule_Revisions. An AI_Provider, a model, a proposer identity, and any other automated component can never be a Reviewer.
- **CLI**: The ruletrace executable, with exactly the four pipeline commands of Requirement 17 criterion 1 and the Review_Group. The CLI contains no command or option that invokes an AI_Provider, and the only commands that append to the Approval_Ledger are review approve and review reject.
- **Prize_Check**: The first-version thin demonstration Workflow_Module (Application_Layer) of Requirement 31, presented by the RuleTrace_Laboratory as the Prize workflow, which demonstrates eligibility, weighted scoring, ranking with Tie_Groups, and a published-result consistency check through the shared Rules_Engine, and performs no prize allocation. The full Prize Check application remains a future application. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Bracket_Check**: The first-version thin demonstration Workflow_Module (Application_Layer) of Requirements 31 and 32, presented by the RuleTrace_Laboratory as the Tournament standings workflow, which demonstrates standings, Qualification_Predicates, Tie_Groups, and a published-standings consistency check through the shared Rules_Engine, and performs no seeding, bracket generation, progression, or multi-stage logic. The full Bracket Check application remains a future application. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Application_Layer**: A Workflow_Module; the two terms are synonyms and Workflow_Module is preferred. A library crate without a binary target that contains only the input mapping of its own Application_Input_Format to Competition_Data, the construction of Questions, and report layout, and that calls the shared Rules_Engine through the library interface (Requirement 31). Prize_Check and Bracket_Check are the only Application_Layers of the first version. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Application_Input_Format**: The file format in which an Application_Layer reads its participants, scores, and published result, either CSV or JSON as the design fixes, which an Application_Layer converts to Competition_Data and to a Published_Result.
- **Demonstration_Executable**: A small executable that wraps one Application_Layer for file input and report output at its binary edge. Superseded by UD-38: no executable of the first version is a Demonstration_Executable. The packages prize-check and bracket-check are library-only Workflow_Modules, and the term remains defined only so that the historical text of UD-33 is readable. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Workflow_Module**: A library crate that contains only the input mapping of an Application_Input_Format to Competition_Data, the construction of Questions, and the presentation of a report, with no binary target and no business logic (Requirement 31). The Workflow_Modules are prize-check and bracket-check. The RuleTrace_Laboratory invokes them. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **RuleTrace_Laboratory** (also the Laboratory): The one integrated local application of Requirements 36 and 37, served by the executable ruletrace-lab on the loopback interface. It orchestrates ruletrace and ruletrace-ai as subprocesses and displays their results. It holds no AI credential, writes no Approval_Ledger record, and makes no decision, approval, or computation of its own (UD-38). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Laboratory_Session**: The period between the exchange of a Launch_Secret for a session cookie and the invalidation of that cookie, with its own temporary directory and its own CSRF token (Requirement 37). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Launch_Secret**: A random value of at least 128 bits that ruletrace-lab generates at each start and gives to the user only through the terminal, and that the user exchanges once for a session cookie (Requirement 37 criteria 5 and 6). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Hand_Off_Artifact**: A file that one subprocess of the RuleTrace_Laboratory produces and the next subprocess consumes, whose SHA-256 hash the Laboratory records and verifies before use (Requirement 36 criterion 9). A valid hash does not make the content trusted. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Runtime_Executable**: An executable that the RuleTrace delivers to a user: ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, and ruletrace-lab. A development or test utility that the workspace builds (for example rt-xtask) is not a Runtime_Executable (Requirement 27 criterion 1). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **Qualification_Predicate**: A Rule of kind eligibility predicate that states whether a participant meets a stated condition and that allocates no place, slot, or stage and fixes no number of qualifiers (Requirement 14 criterion 11).
- **Standings**: The Outcome of a ranking Rule over a participant set, with each participant's Rank_Position, each Tie_Group, and each Indeterminate position, as the Rules_Engine returns it.
- **Demo_Corpus**: The committed set of Source_Documents, manifest entries, Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Competition_Data, Published_Results, Recorded_Provider_Script, Coverage_Statement, and golden files that the end-to-end demonstration uses (Requirement 33).
- **Coverage_Statement**: The committed list of every Candidate_Clause of the demonstration documents with exactly one classification: Rule approved, Proposal rejected, Unsupported, Ambiguity, or Not a rule (Requirement 33 criterion 6).
- **Recorded_Provider_Script**: A committed Mock_Provider script whose Provider_Responses are the unmodified bytes that a real AI_Provider returned for the Provider_Requests of the demonstration, each keyed by its Request_Identifier (Requirement 33 criterion 8).
- **Demo_Runbook**: The committed document that lists the commands and expected results of the offline demonstration and of the optional live-provider step (Requirement 33 criterion 12).
- **Proof_Report**: The deterministic report that shows, for each decision, the Rule, the source clause text from the Explainer, the input values read, and the calculation steps (Requirement 33 criterion 14).
- **First_Version_Support_Boundary**: The list in Requirement 14 of the Rule kinds, operations, and types that the first version supports.
- **Fixture**: A stored input file with an expected output used for regression testing.
- **Diagnostic_Catalogue**: The single code catalogue file that lists every Diagnostic code (identifier of the form RT-family-number and mnemonic) with its Severity, meaning, and exit code.

## Requirements

### Requirement 1: Source Ingestion

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want rulebook documents ingested without losing structure, so that every rule can point back to its original clause.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Source_Document that is valid UTF-8 and is plain text or Markdown is provided, THE Ingestor SHALL produce a Source_Model containing every heading, numbering label, paragraph, and list item of the document, ordered by ascending start offset in the original document.
2. THE Ingestor SHALL assign each element of the Source_Model a Source_Span consisting of the document identifier, a start byte offset (inclusive), an end byte offset (exclusive), a start line number, and a start column number, where line and column numbers are 1-based and columns count Unicode scalar values.
3. WHEN a Source_Document is ingested, THE Ingestor SHALL record in the Source_Model the SHA-256 content hash of the document bytes, encoded as 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.
4. FOR ALL valid Source_Documents, extracting the bytes of each Source_Span from the original document SHALL reproduce the text of the corresponding Source_Model element byte-for-byte.
5. IF a Source_Document is not valid UTF-8, THEN THE Ingestor SHALL return an Error Diagnostic whose primary Location is the Document_Location of the document and whose message states the byte offset of the first invalid sequence, and THE Ingestor SHALL NOT produce a Source_Model for that document.
6. IF a Source_Document has a format that is not plain text or Markdown, THEN THE Ingestor SHALL return an Unsupported Diagnostic that names the unsupported format and has the Document_Location of the document as its primary Location, and THE Ingestor SHALL NOT produce a Source_Model for that document.
7. WHEN the same Source_Document bytes and document identifier are ingested twice, in any order and in the same or different processes, THE Ingestor SHALL produce Source_Models that are byte-for-byte identical when serialized.
8. WHEN a Source_Document is empty (zero bytes), THE Ingestor SHALL produce a Source_Model with zero elements and the content hash of zero bytes, and THE Ingestor SHALL NOT return an Error Diagnostic.
9. IF a Source_Document exceeds 16 MiB (16,777,216 bytes), THEN THE Ingestor SHALL return an Error Diagnostic that has the Document_Location of the document as its primary Location and names the size limit, and THE Ingestor SHALL NOT produce a Source_Model for that document.
10. THE document identifier SHALL be an opaque UTF-8 string supplied by the caller, and WHEN the CLI derives a document identifier from a path argument, THE CLI SHALL use the path text as given with each backslash replaced by a forward slash, so that the identifier does not depend on the operating system.

### Requirement 2: Clause Extraction Interface

**User Story:** As a reviewer, I want candidate clauses listed with their locations, so that I can decide which clauses become rules.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a valid Source_Model is provided, THE Extractor SHALL produce an ordered list of Candidate_Clauses, each containing a Source_Span (document identifier, start offset, end offset) and the exact source text of that span, with the list ordered by ascending start offset.
2. THE Extractor SHALL create no Rule and SHALL assign no Approval_Status to any Candidate_Clause, because a Candidate_Clause is not a Rule.
3. WHEN creation of a Rule from a Candidate_Clause is requested, THE Rule_IR library SHALL create a Rule_Revision with Revision_Number 1, Approval_Status Proposed, Origin Manual, the Source_Span of that Candidate_Clause as one of its Source_Spans, and no Approval_Record, and SHALL NOT require an Approval_Record for that creation.
4. WHERE the first-version extraction mode is used, THE Extractor SHALL produce exactly one Candidate_Clause for each numbered clause or list item of the Source_Model, with the Source_Span covering that clause or list item, and SHALL NOT interpret, classify, or alter the clause content.
5. WHEN a Rulebook is validated and a list of Candidate_Clauses is supplied, THE Validator SHALL emit one Warning Diagnostic for each Candidate_Clause that no Rule in the Rulebook references, and each Warning Diagnostic SHALL have the Source_Span of that Candidate_Clause as its primary Location.
6. IF the provided Source_Model contains no numbered clause or list item, THEN THE Extractor SHALL produce zero Candidate_Clauses, regardless of the extraction mode in use.
7. IF the provided Source_Model is invalid or lacks Source_Span information, THEN THE Extractor SHALL produce zero Candidate_Clauses and return an Error Diagnostic indicating the invalid Source_Model.
8. IF the provided Source_Model contains no numbered clause or list item, THEN THE Extractor SHALL return a Warning Diagnostic indicating that no clauses were found, with the Document_Location of the whole document as its primary Location, regardless of the extraction mode in use.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL expose extraction as a library function of the Extractor and SHALL expose it on the CLI only as the --candidates option of the ingest command, which writes the Candidate_Clause list in addition to the Source_Model, and THE CLI SHALL provide no command named extract.

### Requirement 3: Typed Versioned Rule IR

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want rules in a typed, versioned representation, so that rules can be validated and replayed reliably.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Rule_IR SHALL declare exactly one IR_Version in every Rulebook, as a single field of the Rulebook, and THE Rulebook SHALL NOT allow any Rule to declare its own IR_Version.
2. THE Rule_IR SHALL represent each Rule as a declarative data structure composed only of the construct kinds, operations, and types listed in the First_Version_Support_Boundary of Requirement 14, and SHALL provide no other construct kind that the Rule_IR parser accepts as a Rule.
3. THE Rule_IR SHALL assign each Rule a stable identifier, and SHALL assign each Rule_Revision of that Rule a Revision_Number that is a positive integer, so that the pair of identifier and Revision_Number is unique within the Rulebook, and the pair has the same value after the Rulebook is serialized and parsed again.
4. THE Rule_IR SHALL require each Rule to carry at least one Location of the Source_Span variant, where the Source_Span names a document identifier declared in the Rulebook and has a start byte offset and an end byte offset that satisfy 0 ≤ start < end ≤ the byte length of that document, and SHALL NOT accept any other Location variant in place of that Source_Span.
5. THE Rule_IR SHALL declare an explicit type for every field, parameter, and expression result, where each declared type is one of the types in the First_Version_Support_Boundary of Requirement 14, and SHALL provide no type inference that supplies a missing declaration.
6. THE Rule_IR SHALL represent every numeric literal as a decimal literal without exponent that denotes an Exact_Number within the literal limits of Requirement 8 criterion 27 (at most 38 coefficient digits and at most 28 digits after the decimal point), and SHALL provide no floating-point numeric representation. The literal syntax is identical under both UD-3 options.
7. THE Rule_IR SHALL provide no construct for embedding scripts, expressions in a general-purpose language, or external code references, and a field that names such a construct SHALL be handled as an unknown structural name under Requirement 4 criterion 3.
8. IF a Rule_IR document declares an IR_Version that RuleTrace does not support, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return exactly one Unsupported Diagnostic that names the declared IR_Version and lists every supported IR_Version, and that has an Input_Location with the 1-based line and column of the declaration, so that no stage after parsing runs on that document.
9. IF a Rulebook contains two or more Rule_Revisions with the same identifier and the same Revision_Number, a Rule with no Source_Span, or a field, parameter, or expression result with no declared type, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic for each violation, where each Diagnostic names the violated constraint, identifies the Rule by its identifier and Revision_Number, and has the Location of the offending Rule or element as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL NOT produce a Compiled_Rulebook and THE Rules_Engine SHALL NOT execute that Rulebook.
10. FOR ALL valid Rulebooks, serializing a Rulebook and then parsing the serialized form SHALL produce a Rulebook equal to the original, where equality requires an identical Rule identifier, Revision_Number, Origin, field, type, Source_Span (document identifier, start offset, end offset, start line, and start column), document hash declaration, IR_Version, and Exact_Number value, and an Exact_Number equality that compares the numeric value and the canonical serialization (round-trip property).
11. WHEN a Rulebook is serialized, THE Rule_IR serializer SHALL emit Rules sorted by ascending Rule identifier (bytewise comparison of the UTF-8 encoding) and then by ascending Revision_Number, and SHALL emit the fields of each element in the single order that the IR_Version documents, so that two equal Rulebooks produce byte-identical output regardless of the order in which their Rules or fields were constructed.
12. THE Rulebook SHALL declare, for every Source_Document referenced by any Source_Span of any Rule, exactly one entry that holds the document identifier and the SHA-256 content hash of that document, encoded as exactly 64 characters from the set 0-9 and a-f (lowercase hexadecimal), and SHALL contain no entry for a document identifier that appears in two or more entries.
13. THE Rule_IR SHALL record for every Rule_Revision its Revision_Number and its Origin, where the Origin is exactly one of Manual or AI_Proposal, and SHALL keep the Origin outside the Rule_Content, as defined in Requirement 25, so that changing only the Origin leaves the Rule_Content_Hash unchanged.
14. THE Rule_IR SHALL declare no Approval_Status, no Approval_Record, and no other lifecycle field in a Rulebook, because the Approval_Status of each Rule_Revision is derived from the Approval_Ledger, and a Rulebook that supplies such a field SHALL be rejected under Requirement 22 criterion 17.
15. IF a Rule_IR document declares no IR_Version, or declares more than one IR_Version, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return one Error Diagnostic that states whether the IR_Version is missing or repeated, with an Input_Location of the Rulebook header for a missing IR_Version, or with an Input_Location of the second declaration (1-based line and column) and the first declaration as a related Location for a repeated IR_Version.
16. IF a Source_Span of a Rule names a document identifier that the Rulebook does not declare under criterion 12, has a start offset that is not less than its end offset, has an end offset greater than the byte length of the Source_Model of that document, has an offset that is not on a UTF-8 character boundary of that document, or is replaced by a Location of another variant, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic for each such Source_Span that identifies the Rule by its identifier and Revision_Number, names which of these conditions failed, and has the Input_Location of the Source_Span in the Rule_IR document as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL NOT produce a Compiled_Rulebook for that Rulebook.
17. IF a document hash declaration of a Rulebook is not exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters, or two declarations use the same document identifier, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return one Error Diagnostic for each such declaration, which names the document identifier, states whether the hash is malformed or the identifier is duplicated, and has the Input_Location of that declaration (1-based line and column) as its primary Location.

### Requirement 4: Rule_IR Parsing and Printing

**User Story:** As a developer, I want a reliable parser and printer for the Rule_IR format, so that rulebooks can be stored, diffed, and reviewed.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a syntactically valid Rule_IR document with no unknown structural names, no unrepresentable numeric literals, no duplicate Rule_Revisions (same identifier and same Revision_Number), and no field named in criterion 10 is provided, THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return a Rulebook that contains every Rule_Revision in the document and no other Rule_Revision, in document order, and SHALL return zero Error Diagnostics and zero Unsupported Diagnostics.
2. IF a Rule_IR document is syntactically invalid, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return an Error Diagnostic whose primary Location is an Input_Location with the 1-based line and 1-based column of the first syntax error, where the first syntax error is the syntax error with the lowest byte offset in the document and the column counts Unicode scalar values, as defined for Source_Spans in Requirement 1 criterion 2.
3. IF a Rule_IR document contains a structural field name that the declared IR_Version does not define, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return exactly one Unsupported Diagnostic for each occurrence of such a name, where each Diagnostic names the unknown element and has an Input_Location with the 1-based line and column of that occurrence.
4. IF a Rule_IR document contains a numeric literal that cannot be represented as an Exact_Number, including a literal in floating-point exponent notation, a fraction literal, or a literal that exceeds the literal limits of Requirement 8 criterion 27, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return exactly one Error Diagnostic for each such literal occurrence, where each Diagnostic contains the literal text and has an Input_Location with its 1-based line and column. This criterion is identical under both UD-3 options.
5. THE Rule_IR pretty printer SHALL produce, for a given Rulebook, a Rule_IR document that the Rule_IR parser accepts with zero Error Diagnostics and zero Unsupported Diagnostics, and SHALL produce byte-identical output on every invocation with the same Rulebook, in the same or a different process.
6. FOR ALL valid Rulebooks, parsing the output of printing the result of parsing a valid Rule_IR document SHALL produce a Rulebook equal to the first parse result, including every Rule identifier, Revision_Number, Exact_Number value, and Location (round-trip property).
7. IF a Rule_IR document contains two or more Rule_Revisions with the same Rule identifier and the same Revision_Number, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return one Error Diagnostic for each duplicate Rule_Revision, meaning each occurrence after the first, where each Diagnostic contains the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number, has the Input_Location of the duplicate occurrence as its primary Location, and lists the Input_Location of the first occurrence as a related Location.
8. IF a Rule_IR document contains more than one error, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return the Diagnostics for all detected errors, each as specified by criteria 2, 3, 4, 7, and 10, in the canonical order of Requirement 7 criterion 10, and SHALL return no Rulebook.
9. WHEN a Rule_IR document contains a Rule whose kind name or operator name is well-formed but is not in the First_Version_Support_Boundary, THE Rule_IR parser SHALL carry that Rule into the Rulebook as an unrecognized construct that cannot be compiled, SHALL return zero Error Diagnostics and zero Unsupported Diagnostics for that Rule, and THE Validator SHALL report it under Requirement 14 criterion 6.
10. IF a Rule_IR document contains a field named approval_status, is_approved, is_rejected, is_proposed, approval_record, has_errors, no_errors, or a validation status, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return no Rulebook and SHALL return, for each occurrence of such a field, exactly one Error Diagnostic, which is LIFECYCLE_FLAG_CONTRADICTION or DIAGNOSTIC_STATE_CONTRADICTION of Requirement 22 as that requirement assigns it to the field name, and SHALL NOT return the Unsupported Diagnostic of criterion 3 for that field, where each Diagnostic has an Input_Location of that field with its 1-based line and column and its field name.
11. THE Rule_IR parser SHALL return identical Rulebooks and identical Diagnostics, byte-for-byte when serialized, for identical Rule_IR document bytes, in the same or a different process and regardless of the order of previous parse invocations.

### Requirement 5: Approval and Review State

**User Story:** As a reviewer, I want only approved rules to be executable, so that no unreviewed interpretation affects a result.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive exactly one Approval_Status, with the value Proposed, Approved, or Rejected, for every Rule_Revision from the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger under Requirement 26 criterion 10, so that deriving the Approval_Status twice from the same Rulebook and Approval_Ledger yields the same value for every Rule_Revision, and the Rule_IR SHALL store no Approval_Status.
2. WHEN the Compiler receives a Validation_Result with no Blocking_Diagnostic, THE Compiler SHALL include in the Compiled_Rulebook every Effective_Revision, each of which has the derived Approval_Status Approved and a matching Approval_Record, and SHALL include no Rule_Revision with any other Approval_Status and no superseded Rule_Revision, so that the set of Rule_Revisions in the Compiled_Rulebook equals the set of Effective_Revisions of the Rulebook.
3. WHEN the Validator receives a Rulebook and an Approval_Ledger, THE Validator SHALL verify the integrity of the Approval_Ledger under Requirement 26 criterion 4 and SHALL derive the Approval_Status of each Rule_Revision, so that Approved means that a matching Approval_Record exists for the current Rule_Content_Hash, the IR_Version, and the document hashes of the Rule_Revision.
4. IF a Rule_Revision has at least one Approval_Record and no matching Approval_Record, THEN THE Validator SHALL derive the Approval_Status Proposed for that Rule_Revision, SHALL return one Warning Diagnostic APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE for each non-matching Approval_Record under Requirement 25 criterion 14, and SHALL apply Requirement 26 criterion 12 to the Rule_Revision, so that THE Compiler produces no Compiled_Rulebook while the Rule_Revision is in an Unresolved_Approval_State, in accordance with criterion 2 of Requirement 9.
5. WHEN a Rulebook contains a Rule_Revision in an Unresolved_Approval_State, THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED for each such Rule_Revision that contains the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, and the derived Approval_Status, and has the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook.
6. WHEN the Rule_Content, the source binding (a Source_Span or a declared document hash), or the IR_Version of a Rule_Revision differs from the value bound in an Approval_Record, THE Validator SHALL treat that Approval_Record as non-matching and SHALL apply criterion 4 to the Rule_Revision, so that a difference in any single one of these three values is sufficient to make the Approval_Record non-matching.
7. IF a Rulebook contains no Effective_Revision after validation, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES with the Input_Location of the Rulebook as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook, including no Compiled_Rulebook with zero Rules.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL permit exactly these operations on the Approval_Status: the creation of a Rule_Revision, whose derived Approval_Status is Proposed; the append of an approval Ledger_Record, which changes Proposed to Approved; and the append of a rejection Ledger_Record, which changes Proposed to Rejected, and SHALL permit no other operation, including no operation out of Approved and no operation out of Rejected.
9. WHEN a request to approve a Rule_Revision is made, THE RuleTrace SHALL append an approval Ledger_Record only if the request names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, a Reviewer that satisfies Requirement 23 criterion 15, a Rule_Content_Hash that equals the current Rule_Content_Hash of the Rule_Revision, and an approval time supplied by the caller, and only if validation of the Rule_Revision yields no Blocking_Diagnostic for that Rule_Revision other than its own APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED.
10. IF a request to change an Approval_Status is not one of the operations in criterion 8, names no Reviewer, supplies a Rule_Content_Hash that differs from the current one, or targets a Rule_Revision with a Blocking_Diagnostic other than its own APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL refuse the request with exactly one Error Diagnostic of the REVIEW family, SHALL append no Ledger_Record, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger, the Rulebook, and the Proposal_Store byte-identical.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL read no clock when it creates, validates, or stores a Ledger_Record, and SHALL use only the time that the caller supplied, so that the stored Ledger_Record time equals the caller-supplied time byte for byte, and two runs of the same request at different system clock values yield byte-identical Ledger_Records.
12. THE RuleTrace SHALL provide the operations of criterion 8 as library functions in a Core_Crate on which no AI_Crate depends, SHALL expose them only through the Review_Group of the ruletrace executable, and SHALL provide no pipeline command, no option of another command, no configuration value, and no environment variable that performs an operation of criterion 8. The Review_Group SHALL be the only executable code path that appends to the Approval_Ledger.
13. THE RuleTrace SHALL apply Requirements 5, 6, 9, 10, and 12 to Rule_Revisions as defined in Requirement 25, so that wherever one of these Requirements refers to an Approved Rule for compilation, conflict detection, or execution, the reference means an Effective_Revision.
14. WHEN a Rulebook contains a Rejected Rule_Revision, THE Validator SHALL return one Warning Diagnostic APPROVAL_REVISION_REJECTED for each such Rule_Revision, which contains the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number and has the first Source_Span of the Rule_Revision as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL exclude the Rejected Rule_Revision from the Compiled_Rulebook without blocking compilation.
15. WHEN the Rule_Content, the source binding, or the IR_Version of an Approved Rule_Revision changes, THE RuleTrace SHALL treat that change as the creation of content with no matching Approval_Record, so that the derived Approval_Status becomes Proposed and criterion 4 applies, and SHALL NOT treat the change as an operation on the Approval_Status.
16. IF a Rule_Revision has no Approval_Record and no rejection Ledger_Record for its current Rule_Content_Hash, THEN THE Validator SHALL derive the Approval_Status Proposed for that Rule_Revision and SHALL return no APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE Diagnostic for it, and WHEN a rejection Ledger_Record that names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, and the current Rule_Content_Hash exists, THE Validator SHALL derive the Approval_Status Rejected for that Rule_Revision.
17. WHEN the RuleTrace accepts a request that satisfies criterion 9 or a rejection request, THE RuleTrace SHALL append exactly one Ledger_Record to the Approval_Ledger, SHALL change no byte of the existing Approval_Ledger content before that Ledger_Record, SHALL leave the Rulebook byte-identical, and SHALL yield the derived Approval_Status Approved or Rejected, respectively, for that Rule_Revision in the next validation.

### Requirement 6: Semantic Validation

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want errors, ambiguities, and unsupported constructs found before execution, so that evaluation results can be trusted.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Rulebook is provided, THE Validator SHALL check the type of every expression and every operand in every Rule against the declared types, so that an expression or operand left unchecked counts as a violation of this criterion.
2. IF an expression has an operand whose type is incompatible with the type the expression expects, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per incompatible operand that contains the diagnostic code, the expression identifier, the expected type, the actual type, and has the Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and SHALL leave the Rulebook unmodified, so that the canonical serialization of the Rulebook after validation is byte-identical to the canonical serialization before validation.
3. IF a Rule refers to a field, participant attribute, or Rule that is not declared in the Rulebook, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per undeclared reference occurrence that contains the diagnostic code, the undeclared reference name, the kind of reference (field, participant attribute, or Rule), and has the Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location.
4. IF a Rule uses a construct outside the First_Version_Support_Boundary, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Unsupported Diagnostic per occurrence of the construct that contains the diagnostic code, the name of the unsupported construct, and has the Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and SHALL NOT attempt to interpret the construct, so that the Diagnostic_State contains no Diagnostic that derives from an interpretation of that construct.
5. IF a Rule is marked in the Rule_IR as having two or more plausible interpretations, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Ambiguity Diagnostic per marked Rule that contains the diagnostic code, every marked interpretation in the order recorded in the Rule_IR, and has the Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and SHALL NOT select any interpretation, so that no Diagnostic, Rule metadata, or Validation_Result field designates one interpretation as chosen.
6. IF a Rule is marked in the Rule_IR as an incomplete interpretation, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per marked Rule that contains the diagnostic code, the name of each missing element in the order recorded in the Rule_IR, and has the Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location.
7. IF two or more Approved Rules form a Conflict of a class defined in criteria 16 to 19 and no Precedence_Declaration resolves the Conflict, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per Conflict that contains the diagnostic code, the Conflict class, and the identifier of every Rule in the Conflict, that has the first Source_Span of the first Rule in ascending Rule identifier order as its primary Location, and that lists the first Source_Span of every other Rule in the Conflict as related Locations in ascending Rule identifier order, and SHALL NOT select a winning Rule, so that no Diagnostic or Validation_Result field designates one Rule of the Conflict as the winner.
8. WHEN a Precedence_Declaration resolves a Conflict, THE Validator SHALL return one Warning Diagnostic per resolved Conflict that contains the diagnostic code, the identifier of the Precedence_Declaration, the identifiers of the Rules in the Conflict, and has the Source_Span of the Precedence_Declaration as its primary Location.
9. IF the Rulebook contains a reference cycle among Rules, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per cycle that contains the diagnostic code and the identifiers of the Rules in the cycle in the order of reference starting from the Rule with the lowest Rule identifier, that has the first Source_Span of that first Rule as its primary Location, and that lists the first Source_Span of every other Rule in the cycle as related Locations in the same order of reference.
10. WHEN a Rule can produce a Missing_Value dependency, THE Validator SHALL record in the Rule metadata the complete set of input fields on which the Rule depends, including fields reached through referenced Rules, with each field name listed once, ordered by field name in ascending order.
11. THE Validator SHALL return every Diagnostic found in a Rulebook in a single validation result, including when the Rulebook contains Diagnostics of different severities and different Rules, so that the number of Diagnostics in the result equals the number of distinct findings and validation does not stop at the first Blocking_Diagnostic.
12. THE Validator SHALL order Diagnostics in the canonical order of Requirement 7 criterion 10, so that repeated validation of the same Rulebook yields byte-identical output.
13. IF a Rulebook contains no Diagnostic of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, THEN THE Validator SHALL mark the validation result as passed. The passed marking SHALL depend only on the Diagnostic_State, as defined in Requirement 22, and the Diagnostic_State of a passed result contains no APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED and no APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES Diagnostic, because those have Severity Error.
14. IF a Rulebook contains one or more Diagnostics of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, including an APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED or APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES Diagnostic, THEN THE Validator SHALL mark the validation result as failed. The failed marking SHALL depend only on the Diagnostic_State, as defined in Requirement 22.
15. WHEN a Rulebook is validated, THE Validator SHALL execute no Rule, so that the Validator reads no Competition_Data and computes no decision, Decision_Trace, or Rule output value. This obligation is independent of criterion 1: a validation that type-checks every expression and operand but executes any Rule violates this criterion, and a validation that executes no Rule but skips the type check of any expression or operand violates criterion 1.
16. WHEN two or more Approved Rules of kind eligibility predicate, derived numeric value, or weighted score declare the same Target, THE Validator SHALL treat those Rules as one Conflict of class DuplicateTarget, whether or not their definitions are equal.
17. WHEN two or more Approved Rules of kind ranking declare the same ranking Target, THE Validator SHALL treat those Rules as one Conflict of class DuplicateRankKey, whether or not their definitions are equal.
18. WHEN two or more Approved Rules of kind explicit default value cover the same input field with literal values that are not equal as Exact_Numbers, Booleans, text, dates, or enumeration members, or that have different types, THE Validator SHALL treat those Rules as one Conflict of class ContradictoryDefault, and WHEN the literal values are equal, THE Validator SHALL treat those Rules as one Conflict of class DuplicateTarget.
19. WHEN an Approved Rule of kind eligibility predicate, derived numeric value, weighted score, or ranking declares a Target equal to the name of an input field declared in the Rulebook, THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic of Conflict class InputShadowing that identifies the Rule and the input field, and THE Validator SHALL NOT accept a Precedence_Declaration as resolving that Conflict, so that the Error Diagnostic remains in the Diagnostic_State when a Precedence_Declaration that lists the Rule is present.
20. WHEN a Precedence_Declaration lists every Rule of a Conflict of class DuplicateTarget, DuplicateRankKey, or ContradictoryDefault in a total order, THE Validator SHALL treat that Conflict as resolved.
21. IF the Precedence_Declarations of a Rulebook contain a cycle, or a Precedence_Declaration lists a Rule that does not exist or has Approval_Status other than Approved, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per offending Precedence_Declaration that names the Precedence_Declaration and the offending Rule identifiers in ascending Rule identifier order, and has the Source_Span of the Precedence_Declaration as its primary Location.
22. THE RuleTrace SHALL document in the user-facing support boundary document of Requirement 14 the Conflict classes that the Validator cannot detect, including at least: Rules with different Targets that contradict each other in meaning, Rules whose expressions overlap or contradict only under satisfiability reasoning over arithmetic or set membership, and Rules that diverge from the meaning of their source clause.
23. WHEN validation completes, THE Validator SHALL record in the Validation_Result the identifiers of the Conflict classes that it checked, in ascending order and without repetition, whether the validation result is passed or failed, so that a passed validation result does not claim the absence of Conflicts of unchecked classes.
24. WHEN the Rulebook references a Source_Document, THE Validator SHALL verify against the supplied Source_Model that the declared document hash of Requirement 3 criterion 12 equals the recorded content hash of that Source_Model, and that every Source_Span of every Rule lies within the document, and IF no Source_Model is supplied for a referenced document, or the hash differs, or a Source_Span lies outside the document, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per failing document or Source_Span whose primary Location is the Source_Span when one is available and otherwise the Document_Location.
25. WHEN a Rulebook is validated, THE Validator SHALL validate every Rule with Approval_Status Approved or Proposed in full, and SHALL apply only the structural checks of Requirement 3 criteria 4 and 9 to a Rule with Approval_Status Rejected, so that a Blocking_Diagnostic on any validated Rule makes the Validation_Result failed.
26. IF a Rulebook contains no Diagnostic, or only Diagnostics of Severity Warning, THEN THE Validator SHALL mark the validation result as passed, so that a Warning Diagnostic, including the Warning Diagnostic of criterion 8, never changes a passed marking to failed.

### Requirement 7: Diagnostics Model

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want consistent diagnostics with locations, so that I can locate and fix problems quickly.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL represent every error, warning, ambiguity, and unsupported construct as a Diagnostic containing a code, a Severity, a message, a primary Location, and an ordered list of related Locations, where the code is unique per Diagnostic kind, the Severity is exactly one of Error, Warning, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, and each Location is exactly one variant of the Location type defined in the Glossary.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL list every Diagnostic code with its Severity and meaning in the Diagnostic_Catalogue, and SHALL keep the Severity and meaning of each code unchanged across releases that share the same IR_Version, as verified by a test that compares the Diagnostic_Catalogue with the baseline defined in criteria 11 and 12.
3. WHEN a Diagnostic originates from a Rule that has at least one Source_Span, THE RuleTrace SHALL include in the Diagnostic the Rule identifier, SHALL use the first Source_Span of that Rule, in the order in which the Rule stores its Source_Spans, as the primary Location, and SHALL list every other Source_Span of that Rule, in that stored order, as a related Location.
4. WHEN the CLI renders a Diagnostic in human-readable form, THE CLI SHALL show the Diagnostic code, the Severity, the message, and the description of the primary Location, and WHEN the primary Location is a Source_Span that resolves in a supplied Source_Model, THE CLI SHALL also show the text of the source line that contains the start of the span, the start byte offset, the end byte offset, and the 1-based start line and start column of the span.
5. WHERE the machine-readable output option is selected, THE CLI SHALL render all Diagnostics in a structured format that contains every Diagnostic field (the code, the Severity, the message, the primary Location, the related Locations, and the Rule identifier where criterion 3 applies), in the order of criterion 10, and SHALL produce byte-identical output on every run for the same input.
6. IF a Blocking_Diagnostic applies to any Rule of a Rulebook, THEN THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook for that Rulebook and SHALL NOT produce a Compiled_Rulebook from the remaining Rules.
7. IF the Validator returns at least one Diagnostic with Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported for a Rulebook, THEN THE Compiler SHALL return no Compiled_Rulebook and SHALL return all Diagnostics received from the Validator, unmodified and in the order of criterion 10.
8. IF the primary Location of a Diagnostic is a Source_Span or Document_Location whose document identifier or span does not exist in the supplied Source_Models, THEN THE CLI SHALL omit the source line entirely from the rendered Diagnostic and SHALL mark the Location as unresolved.
9. IF a Diagnostic's Location is unresolved under criterion 8, THEN THE CLI SHALL still show the Diagnostic code, Severity, and message.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL order every list of Diagnostics, across any number of documents and inputs, by a total order with these sort keys in sequence: first the rank of the primary Location variant (Source_Span first, then Document_Location, Input_Location, Cli_Location, and No_Location last); then, within Source_Span, the document identifier compared as UTF-8 bytes in ascending order, the start byte offset, and the end byte offset; within Document_Location, the document identifier; within Input_Location, the input identifier compared as UTF-8 bytes, then the line, then the column, where an absent line or column sorts first; within Cli_Location, the argument position then the argument name; within No_Location, the reason code; then the Diagnostic code in ascending lexicographic order; then the message compared as UTF-8 bytes; then the related Locations compared in list order by the same keys, where a related list that is a proper prefix of another sorts first; and finally the Rule identifier under criterion 22.
11.
