WHERE a previously released Diagnostic_Catalogue with the same IR_Version exists, THE RuleTrace SHALL fail the compatibility test of criterion 2 when any code in that previous catalogue is missing from the current catalogue or has a different Severity or a different meaning, where the meaning of a code is its catalogue meaning text compared byte for byte.
12. WHERE no previous release exists, THE RuleTrace SHALL treat the Diagnostic_Catalogue committed in the commit that is tagged as the first release as the baseline, SHALL store that catalogue as a baseline file in the repository, SHALL skip the compatibility test of criterion 2 only for that first release and SHALL report the skip and its reason, and for every later release SHALL fail the compatibility test when the baseline file is missing.
13. WHEN the CLI renders a Diagnostic whose primary Location is a Document_Location, Input_Location, Cli_Location, or No_Location, THE CLI SHALL show the textual description of that Location and, for a No_Location, the reason code, and SHALL show no source line.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL give each Diagnostic the most specific Location variant that exists for its subject, where specificity follows the rank order of criterion 10 (Source_Span most specific, then Document_Location, Input_Location, Cli_Location, and No_Location least specific), SHALL use the Source_Span variant only for a span derived from a Source_Model or from a Rule, and SHALL NOT construct a Source_Span, line number, or column number for which no source exists.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL list in the Diagnostic_Catalogue every Diagnostic code that Requirements 5, 13, 16, 22, 23, 25, and 26 to 30 name, and SHALL order and render those Diagnostics under criteria 4, 5, 10, and 13. A Diagnostic about a span that a Provider_Response or another untrusted input supplies SHALL have an Input_Location as its primary Location and SHALL NOT have a Source_Span as its primary Location, because that span is not derived from a Source_Model.
16. THE RuleTrace SHALL give every Diagnostic code a catalogue identifier of the form RT-family-number with four digits (families RT-DGN, RT-PRV, RT-APR, and RT-AI, and the families that the design assigns to the Diagnostics of Requirements 1 to 14, 19, and 21) and a mnemonic name, SHALL treat the identifier as the Diagnostic code that tests assert and that the CLI renders, and SHALL bind every mnemonic used in this document to exactly one identifier of the table in criterion 17.
17. THE Diagnostic_Catalogue SHALL contain at least the codes of the following table, each with its Severity, meaning, exit code, and executable, and SHALL contain exactly one code for each failure mode of ruletrace-ai (configuration, credential, consent, transport, retry, response, provenance, and store) and of the review command group.

The run is not failed | 0 | ruletrace-ai |

18. IF a Diagnostic is constructed with a code that is not in the Diagnostic_Catalogue or with a Severity that differs from the catalogue, THEN THE RuleTrace test suite SHALL fail, as verified by a test that scans every Diagnostic constructor.
19. THE RuleTrace SHALL verify by a test that the exit code column of the Diagnostic_Catalogue equals the exit code that the named executable returns for each code under the earliest-failing-stage policy of Requirement 16 criteria 14 and 17, and that each Warning code leaves the exit code and the derived flags of Requirement 22 unchanged.
20. THE RuleTrace SHALL map the earlier mnemonics STALE_APPROVAL_RECORD, PROV_*, AI_*, and REVIEW_* to the identifiers above in the first-release baseline of criterion 12, so that no earlier mnemonic is left without an identifier.
21. IF a Diagnostic originates from a Rule that has no Source_Span, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL include the Rule identifier in the Diagnostic, SHALL use as the primary Location the most specific Location variant that exists for the Rule under criterion 14 (a Document_Location, Input_Location, Cli_Location, or No_Location), SHALL leave the related Locations empty, and SHALL NOT construct a Source_Span.
22. WHEN two Diagnostics in one list are equal under every sort key of criterion 10 that precedes the Rule identifier, THE RuleTrace SHALL order them by the Rule identifier compared as UTF-8 bytes in ascending order, where a Diagnostic with no Rule identifier sorts first, and SHALL retain both Diagnostics without merging them.

23. THE RuleTrace SHALL list in the Diagnostic_Catalogue the code RANK_REFERENCE_CYCLE (Requirement 14 criterion 12, WHERE the option Rank_Reference of UD-32 is selected) and the Diagnostic codes that Requirement 31 criterion 9 names (APP_INPUT_MALFORMED, APP_INPUT_FIELD_MISSING, APP_INPUT_DUPLICATE_PARTICIPANT, APP_INPUT_VALUE_INVALID, APP_TARGET_NOT_DECLARED, APP_TARGET_KIND_MISMATCH), each with exactly one catalogue identifier, a Severity, a meaning, an exit code, and an executable (ruletrace-lab, which reports the Diagnostic that a Workflow_Module returns), and SHALL bind each of these mnemonics to its identifier under criterion 16. The design assigns the family of the application codes (RT-APP is proposed). The checks of Requirement 33 and 34 are test failures and not Diagnostics, and need no catalogue code. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

### Requirement 8: Exact Numeric Semantics

**User Story:** As a competition administrator, I want exact arithmetic for scores and amounts, so that comparisons and allocations are correct to the last digit.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Rules_Engine SHALL perform all arithmetic and comparison on Exact_Numbers, and, for the same compiled rulebook and the same Competition_Data and Questions, SHALL return byte-identical serialized Outcomes and Decision_Traces on every evaluation, regardless of the number of evaluations, the order of evaluation, or the presence of another rulebook evaluated in the same process.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL contain no floating-point type (no f32 or f64, and no floating-point literal) in the source of the Rule_IR, Compiler, or Rules_Engine data paths, and no floating-point type in the public types of the crates that implement them, as verified by a source-scan test that fails the build when one is present.
3. WHEN a Rule requires rounding, THE Rule_IR SHALL declare the rounding mode (one of the three modes of criterion 14) and the number of decimal places (an integer from 0 to 28 inclusive) explicitly, and THE Validator SHALL return an Error Diagnostic for a number of decimal places below 0, above 28, or not an integer.
4. IF a Rule requires rounding and the Rule_IR omits the rounding mode or the number of decimal places, THEN THE Validator SHALL return an Error Diagnostic that contains a diagnostic code from the catalogue, identifies the Rule, and has the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook, in accordance with criterion 2 of Requirement 9. (UD-3-dependent: the earlier text also covered a division, which now belongs to criterion 20 for Option D only.)
5. IF the exact result of an arithmetic operation is not representable within the Exact_Number_Limits of the selected UD-3 option, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome with an Error Diagnostic that identifies the operation, the Rule, and the operands (each operand in its canonical serialization), and SHALL NOT wrap, saturate, round, or truncate the result. (UD-3-dependent: the limits differ, and under Option D a result with more than 28 decimal places after removal of trailing zeros is not representable.)
6. IF a Rule divides by zero, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome with an Error Diagnostic that identifies the Rule and the operands (each operand in its canonical serialization), and SHALL NOT substitute zero or any other value for the result.
7. WHEN Competition_Data contains a numeric value that violates the literal limits of criterion 27 or that is not a valid decimal literal, THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome with an Error Diagnostic that identifies the field and the value as an Input_Location, and SHALL NOT round the value implicitly. This criterion is identical under both UD-3 options.
8. FOR ALL Exact_Numbers a and b where a + b and (a + b) - b are both within the supported range, THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate (a + b) - b as equal to a.
9. FOR ALL Exact_Numbers a, b, and c where every intermediate and final sum is within the supported range, THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate a + b + c to the same value in any grouping or order of the operands.
10. WHEN Competition_Data contains the decimal values 0.1 and 0.2, THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate 0.1 + 0.2 as exactly equal to 0.3, and SHALL evaluate the comparison (0.1 + 0.2) = 0.3 as true.
11. THE Rules_Engine SHALL support Exact_Numbers within the Exact_Number_Limits of the selected UD-3 option, and SHALL compare Exact_Numbers by numeric value, so that 1.0 equals 1.00 and, under Option R, 1/2 equals 0.5. (UD-3-dependent: the limits.)
12. THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate addition, subtraction, and multiplication so that the result is the exact mathematical result, and SHALL return the Indeterminate Outcome of criterion 5 when that exact result is not representable.
13. WHERE Option D of UD-3 is selected, WHEN a Rule contains a division, THE Rules_Engine SHALL compute the exact quotient, SHALL round it once to the number of decimal places and with the rounding mode that the Rule declares, and SHALL apply no other rounding in the Rule, including when the exact quotient has a terminating decimal expansion. (UD-3-dependent: Option R uses criterion 21.)
14. THE Rule_IR SHALL support exactly these rounding modes: half-even, half-away-from-zero, and toward-zero, SHALL reject any other rounding mode name with the Unsupported Diagnostic of Requirement 4 criterion 3, and each rounding SHALL be applied only at the point where a Rule declares it.
15. THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate the aggregations sum, minimum, and maximum on Exact_Numbers with the semantics of criteria 11 and 12, and count as a non-negative integer Exact_Number, and SHALL return the Indeterminate Outcome of criterion 5 when a sum is not representable, and the aggregation of an empty collection follows criteria 29 and 30.
16. THE Rule_IR serializer SHALL write each Exact_Number literal as a decimal string without an exponent and without trailing fractional zeros (and without a decimal point when the fractional part is zero), so that equal values have one serialized form, for example 1.50 as 1.5 and 2.0 as 2. (UD-3-dependent: under Option R, criterion 24 governs values that have no finite decimal expansion in Outcomes, Decision_Traces, and the Compiled_Rulebook.)
17. THE RuleTrace SHALL represent zero as one value, so that 0, 0.0, -0, and -0.00 compare equal, serialize to the single string 0, and produce the same hash input.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL implement Exact_Number arithmetic so that every operation reports overflow as a Diagnostic and none wraps, as verified by a differential property test against an independent arbitrary-precision reference model (Requirement 20 criterion 22).
19. THE RuleTrace SHALL record in the design document, before Rule_IR is finalized and before the user confirms UD-3, the selected Exact_Number option and sub-option together with its comparison against the alternatives on the six dimensions of the Exact_Number decision note (UD-3), the integer-width selection, the normalization, the overflow behavior, the rounding, and the serialization of the selected option, SHALL treat the choice as final only after the user confirms it, and SHALL justify any third-party dependency under Requirement 21 criterion 5.
20. WHERE Option D of UD-3 is selected, IF a Rule contains a division and the Rule_IR omits the rounding mode or the number of decimal places of that division, THEN THE Validator SHALL return an Error Diagnostic that contains a diagnostic code from the catalogue, identifies the Rule, and has the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook. (UD-3-dependent.)
21. WHERE Option R of UD-3 is selected, WHEN a Rule contains a division, THE Rules_Engine SHALL compute the exact quotient as a normalized rational, SHALL apply no rounding at the division, and SHALL round only at a rounding point that the Rule declares under criteria 3 and 14. (UD-3-dependent.)
22. WHERE Option R of UD-3 is selected, THE Rules_Engine SHALL hold every Exact_Number in lowest terms with a positive denominator and zero as 0/1, SHALL keep the numerator magnitude and the denominator within the Exact_Number_Limits, and SHALL compare two values by equality of the normalized pair, so that 1/2, 2/4, and 0.5 are equal. (UD-3-dependent.)
23. WHERE Option R of UD-3 is selected, THE Rules_Engine SHALL perform every intermediate step, including greatest-common-divisor normalization and cross-multiplication, with checked arithmetic of at least twice the bound width plus 2 bits, so that the Outcome depends only on whether the exact normalized result lies within the Exact_Number_Limits, and IF any checked step reports overflow, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome with an Error Diagnostic that identifies the operation, the Rule, and the operands, and SHALL NOT wrap, saturate, round, or truncate. (UD-3-dependent.)
24. WHERE Option R of UD-3 is selected, THE serializers of Outcomes, Decision_Traces, and the Compiled_Rulebook SHALL write an Exact_Number whose lowest-terms denominator has no prime factor other than 2 and 5 as the canonical decimal string of criterion 16, and SHALL write every other Exact_Number as the canonical fraction numerator/denominator in lowest terms with a positive denominator, a minus sign only on the numerator, no spaces, and no exponent, for example 1/3 and -2/3. (UD-3-dependent.)
25. WHERE Option R of UD-3 is selected, THE parsers SHALL accept the canonical fraction form only in a serialized Outcome, Decision_Trace, or Compiled_Rulebook, and SHALL reject it in a Rule_IR document, in Competition_Data, and in a Published_Result as a literal that is not a valid decimal literal. (UD-3-dependent.)
26. WHEN a numeric value of a Published_Result is compared with a computed Exact_Number, THE Rules_Engine SHALL compare the two by exact numeric value without rounding either, SHALL report them as different when they are not equal, and, WHERE the computed value has a non-terminating decimal expansion (Option R only), SHALL state in the reported difference that the computed value is non-terminating and that the Rule declares no rounding or a rounding that yields another value. This criterion holds under both UD-3 options.
27. THE Rule_IR, Competition_Data, and Published_Result parsers SHALL accept a numeric literal only as an optionally negative decimal string without exponent that has a coefficient of at most 38 decimal digits and at most 28 digits after the decimal point, so that a literal with 38 digits and a literal with 28 fractional digits are accepted and a literal with 39 digits, a literal with 29 fractional digits, a literal with an exponent, a literal with a leading plus sign, and the strings NaN and Infinity are rejected, and SHALL reject any other literal under Requirement 4 criterion 4 or criterion 7 of this Requirement. This criterion is identical under both UD-3 options.
28. WHEN a Rule rounds an Exact_Number to a declared number of decimal places, THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce the result of the declared mode on the exact value, so that rounding to 0 places gives 2.5 as 2 under half-even, 3 under half-away-from-zero, and 2 under toward-zero; -2.5 as -2, -3, and -2 respectively; 3.5 as 4, 4, and 3 respectively; and rounding 0.125 to 2 places gives 0.12, 0.13, and 0.12 respectively. This criterion is identical under both UD-3 options.
29. WHEN the Rules_Engine evaluates the aggregation count or sum over an empty collection that Competition_Data supplies, THE Rules_Engine SHALL return the Exact_Number 0 for count and the Exact_Number 0 for sum as ordinary values with no Diagnostic, and SHALL record the empty collection and the result in the Decision_Trace, and a collection that Competition_Data does not supply is missing information under Requirement 11 and not an empty collection.
30. IF the Rules_Engine evaluates the aggregation minimum or maximum over an empty collection and the result is required to determine an Outcome under the three-valued logic of Requirement 11 criterion 4, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome with the Error Diagnostic AGGREGATE_EMPTY_COLLECTION (RT-DGN-0005) that identifies the Rule and the collection (by its Competition_Data field name), that has the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and SHALL NOT substitute zero, false, or any other value for the result, and, WHEN the Outcome is determined without the result, SHALL return no Diagnostic for that aggregation.
31. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests that assert count = 0 and sum = 0 for an empty collection, that sum of an empty collection added to 5 equals 5, that minimum and maximum of an empty collection each give the Indeterminate Outcome with RT-DGN-0005 naming the Rule and the collection and the evaluate exit code 4, that a Rule that decides its Outcome without the minimum or maximum of an empty collection returns no RT-DGN-0005, that a collection absent from the Competition_Data gives the missing-information result of Requirement 11 and not these results, and a property that count and sum of a generated empty collection are 0 in every position of a generated expression.

### Requirement 9: Compilation

**User Story:** As a developer, I want validated rules compiled to a deterministic executable form, so that execution is fast to verify and impossible to alter by document content.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Compiler receives a Validation_Result whose Diagnostic_State contains zero Diagnostics of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, and the re-derivation of criterion 12 succeeds, THE Compiler SHALL produce exactly one Compiled_Rulebook. That Compiled_Rulebook SHALL contain exactly one compiled Rule for each Effective_Revision, and no other Rule, as defined by Requirement 5 criterion 2. Diagnostics of Severity Warning SHALL NOT prevent compilation.
2. IF the Compiler receives a Validation_Result whose Diagnostic_State contains one or more Diagnostics of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, THEN THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook. It SHALL return every one of those Diagnostics unchanged, with the same code, Severity, message, primary Location, and ordered related Locations, in the same order. It SHALL leave the input Rulebook byte-identical to its state before the call.
3. WHEN the Compiler produces a Compiled_Rulebook, THE Compiler SHALL retain in it, for every compiled Rule, the Rule identifier, the declared types, and every Source_Span carried by the Rule in the Rulebook, with no Source_Span dropped or altered. Every compiled Rule SHALL carry at least one Source_Span, and the count and order of Source_Spans of each compiled Rule SHALL equal those of the corresponding Effective_Revision.
4. WHEN the same Rulebook is compiled two or more times with the same Compiler version, THE Compiler SHALL produce Compiled_Rulebooks whose serialized forms are byte-identical. This holds across at least 2 runs in separate processes, with different wall-clock times, environment variable sets, working directories, and permutations of the order of the input collections.
5. WHEN the Compiler produces a Compiled_Rulebook, THE Compiler SHALL record in it the IR_Version, the Rulebook content hash, the Compiler version, and the SHA-256 content hash of each Source_Document that the Rulebook declares under Requirement 3 criterion 12 and that a compiled Rule references. The Rulebook content hash is the SHA-256 hash of the canonical serialization of the IR_Version, the type declarations, the Rule_Content of every Effective_Revision, the Precedence_Declarations, and the document references. The hash excludes every Origin, every Approval_Record, and every Rule_Revision that is not an Effective_Revision. Two Rulebooks that differ only in Origin, Approval_Records, or non-Effective Rule_Revisions SHALL therefore produce Compiled_Rulebooks with identical content hashes and byte-identical serialized forms, so that the Compiled_Rulebook does not depend on the Origin of any Rule.
6. THE Compiled_Rulebook SHALL contain no executable code, script, or expression in a general-purpose language derived from Source_Document text, and every Rule in it SHALL be a node of the constrained declarative Rule language.
7. THE Compiled_Rulebook SHALL own all of its state. Compiling or executing one Compiled_Rulebook SHALL NOT change the results of any other Compiled_Rulebook in the same process, in any order or interleaving of compilation and execution calls.
8. WHEN the Compiler produces a Compiled_Rulebook, THE Compiler SHALL fix the evaluation order of Rules at compile time as a topological order of Rule dependencies, in which every Rule appears after every Rule it depends on. Among Rules that are ready at the same step, the Compiler SHALL order them by ascending Rule identifier. The ordering SHALL be the same for every permutation of the input order.
9. IF the Rule dependencies contain a cycle, THEN THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook and SHALL return one Diagnostic of Severity Error for each cycle. The Diagnostic SHALL name every Rule identifier in the cycle, with each identifier listed once. Its primary Location SHALL be the first Source_Span of the first Rule of the cycle in ascending Rule identifier order. Its related Locations SHALL be the first Source_Span of each other Rule of the cycle, in ascending Rule identifier order.
10. THE Compiler SHALL produce either a complete Compiled_Rulebook or no Compiled_Rulebook. It SHALL NOT produce a Compiled_Rulebook from a subset of the Effective_Revisions. This includes a subset that omits a Rule_Revision in an Unresolved_Approval_State in order to avoid a Blocking_Diagnostic. When no Compiled_Rulebook is produced, no partial output SHALL be returned or persisted.
11. THE Compiler SHALL accept as input only a Validation_Result, which carries the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, and the Diagnostic_State. IF the input lacks the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, or the Diagnostic_State from the Validator, THEN THE Compiler SHALL treat it as invalid input, produce no Compiled_Rulebook, and return one Error Diagnostic for each missing component.
12. WHEN the Compiler receives a Validation_Result, THE Compiler SHALL re-derive the Approval_Status of every Rule_Revision from the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger of that Validation_Result. IF a Rule_Revision to be compiled does not derive Approved with a matching Approval_Record, or the Blocking_Diagnostics in the supplied Diagnostic_State differ in set from those the re-derivation produces, THEN THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook and SHALL return the Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_RECHECK_FAILED.
13. IF the Validation_Result contains no Effective_Revision, THEN THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook, including no Compiled_Rulebook with zero Rules, and SHALL return the Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES. The primary Location of this Diagnostic SHALL be the Rulebook document Location.
14. THE Compiler SHALL perform no file access, network access, or clock, randomness, or environment reads. Its output SHALL be a function only of the Validation_Result and the Compiler version.

### Requirement 10: Deterministic Execution

**User Story:** As a competition administrator, I want identical results from identical inputs, so that published results can be reproduced and audited.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Compiled_Rulebook and Competition_Data are provided with a list of requested questions, THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce exactly one Outcome for each requested question, and each Outcome SHALL be one of the values defined in the Glossary (including Indeterminate).
2. THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce byte-identical serialized Outcomes and byte-identical serialized Decision_Traces for identical Compiled_Rulebooks and identical Competition_Data across 100 repeated runs and across every platform of the continuous integration platform matrix defined in Requirement 21 criterion 10.
3. THE Rules_Engine SHALL derive each Outcome only from the Compiled_Rulebook and the Competition_Data.
4. WHEN the input records of Competition_Data are supplied in a different order, THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce identical Outcomes and identical Decision_Traces, except for a Rule that explicitly defines an order-dependent operation over a declared ordering field.
5. IF two or more records have equal values in the declared ordering field of an order-dependent Rule, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Error Diagnostic that identifies the Rule and the tied records, and SHALL produce an Indeterminate Outcome for that Rule.
6. IF a value in Competition_Data fails type checking against the declared input schema of the Compiled_Rulebook, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per failing value that identifies the field, the record, and the expected type, and has an Input_Location for the value. THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce an Indeterminate Outcome for every question that depends on the failing value, and SHALL leave Outcomes that do not depend on it unchanged.
7. WHILE a Compiled_Rulebook is executing, THE Rules_Engine SHALL perform zero file, network, clock, random, or environment accesses.
8. WHEN two Compiled_Rulebooks are executed in the same process, in any order or interleaved, THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce for each Compiled_Rulebook the same Outcomes and Decision_Traces as when that Compiled_Rulebook is executed alone.
9. IF a Rule would require unbounded iteration or recursion, THEN THE Validator SHALL return an Unsupported Diagnostic that identifies the Rule by the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook that contains that Rule.
10. THE Rules_Engine SHALL accept only in-memory typed values as input, namely a Compiled_Rulebook, Competition_Data, a list of Questions, and an optional Published_Result, and SHALL accept no Source_Model, file handle, file path, or network address.
11. THE Rules_Engine SHALL record in each Decision_Trace only Locations and document content hashes to identify source clauses, and SHALL record no Source_Document text.

### Requirement 11: Missing Information Handling

**User Story:** As a competition administrator, I want missing data reported as missing, so that an absent value never becomes a false eligibility or a zero score.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. IF a Rule requires a field whose value in Competition_Data is a Missing_Value, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce an Indeterminate Outcome for that Rule, and the Outcome SHALL contain the Rule identifier, the name of the missing field, and the Rule's Source_Span.
2. THE Rules_Engine SHALL NOT substitute false, zero, an empty string, an empty collection, or any other default for a Missing_Value, except WHERE the Rulebook declares a default for that field with a Source_Span, in which case THE Rules_Engine SHALL use the declared default and SHALL record the default's Source_Span in the Decision_Trace.
3. WHEN an Outcome depends on an Indeterminate sub-result, THE Rules_Engine SHALL set that Outcome to Indeterminate and SHALL list every originating Missing_Value (field name and Competition_Data record identifier) in the Decision_Trace entry for that Outcome, in a deterministic order.
4. WHEN a Boolean combination has at least one Indeterminate operand, THE Rules_Engine SHALL evaluate it with Kleene three-valued logic, where AND yields false if any operand is false, OR yields true if any operand is true, NOT of Indeterminate yields Indeterminate, and every other combination with an Indeterminate operand yields Indeterminate.
5. THE Rules_Engine SHALL classify a field value in Competition_Data as present if it is explicitly supplied as zero, false, or an empty string, and as a Missing_Value only if the field is absent or explicitly marked missing.
6. IF a Rule requires a field that is a Missing_Value and the Rulebook declares no default for it, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL complete evaluation of all other Rules and SHALL NOT abort the run, and SHALL NOT return a numeric or Boolean Outcome for the affected Rule.

### Requirement 12: Conflict and Tie Handling at Execution

**User Story:** As a competition administrator, I want unresolved ties and conflicts reported, so that no participant is ranked by an arbitrary choice.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a ranking Rule orders participants, THE Rules_Engine SHALL apply the declared tie-breaking criteria in the order declared in the Rulebook, and SHALL evaluate each criterion only for participants still tied after the preceding criterion.
2. IF participants remain tied after all declared tie-breaking criteria are applied, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return the tied participants as one explicit Tie_Group in the Outcome, SHALL assign every member of the group the same Rank_Position, and SHALL NOT order the tied participants by input order, identifier, or any undeclared criterion. The serialized listing of the members of a Tie_Group SHALL be in ascending participant identifier order solely so that serialization is byte-identical, and that listing order SHALL carry no ranking meaning.
3. IF a ranking Rule declares no tie-breaking criteria and two or more participants have equal values on the ranking key, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return those participants as an explicit Tie_Group in the Outcome.
4. IF a participant has no value for the ranking key or for a declared tie-breaking criterion that must be evaluated, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome for the ranking position of that participant and of every participant whose position depends on that comparison, with a Diagnostic of Severity Error that identifies the participant and the missing input, and SHALL NOT treat the missing value as zero, false, or lowest.
5. IF two or more Rules produce incompatible results for the same Target and the same subject at execution time, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome for that subject, SHALL NOT select any of the results, and SHALL emit a Diagnostic of Severity Error that identifies each incompatible Rule by its Source_Span. THE Rules_Engine SHALL apply this criterion as a defensive check that does not depend on the static checks of Requirement 6.
6. WHEN a Rule declares a tie-handling policy that is within the First_Version_Support_Boundary, THE Rules_Engine SHALL apply the declared policy and SHALL record the policy and its Source_Span in the Decision_Trace.
7. WHEN the Rules_Engine returns a Tie_Group or an Indeterminate Outcome under this requirement, THE Rules_Engine SHALL record in the Decision_Trace the compared values, the tie-breaking criteria applied in order, and the Source_Span of each Rule involved.
8. WHEN a ranking Rule is evaluated over a set of participants, THE Rules_Engine SHALL assign every participant in that set exactly one of the following: a Rank_Position held by that participant alone, a Rank_Position shared as a member of exactly one Tie_Group, or an Indeterminate position, and SHALL assign no participant more than one and none fewer than one.
9. WHEN the Rules_Engine assigns Rank_Positions, THE Rules_Engine SHALL assign each participant the Rank_Position equal to 1 plus the number of participants that are strictly ahead of that participant, so that a Tie_Group of k participants at position p is followed by the next position p + k, and THE Rules_Engine SHALL include in one Tie_Group every participant that is equal on the ranking key and on every declared tie-breaking criterion, so that Tie_Groups are disjoint and complete.
10. WHEN a Precedence_Declaration resolves a Conflict among Rules for the same Target, THE Rules_Engine SHALL use only the result of the Rule that the Precedence_Declaration lists first, SHALL list each other Rule in the Decision_Trace as considered and not applied with the identifier of the Precedence_Declaration as the reason, and IF the first-listed Rule yields an Indeterminate result, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return an Indeterminate Outcome and SHALL NOT use the result of any other Rule.
11. IF the Rules_Engine returns an Indeterminate Outcome under criterion 5, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL emit the Error Diagnostic EXECUTION_CONFLICT_DETECTED (RT-DGN-0004) once for the subject, with the first Source_Span, in ascending order of Rule identifier compared as UTF-8 bytes, of the incompatible Rules as its primary Location and every other Source_Span of those Rules as related Locations, and SHALL record each incompatible Rule and its result in the Decision_Trace.
12. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that runs the Rules_Engine on a test-only Compiled_Rulebook, built in test code and not produced by the Compiler, in which two Rules give incompatible results for one Target and one subject, and that asserts the Indeterminate Outcome for that subject, that no result of either Rule is selected, the Diagnostic RT-DGN-0004 with the exact primary Location and related Locations, and, through the CLI evaluate command, the exit code 4 and not the exit code 3 (Requirement 16 criterion 23), and a test that a Rulebook that compiles through the Compiler never triggers criterion 5 on any Fixture of the vertical slice.

### Requirement 13: Decision Trace and Provenance

**User Story:** As an auditor, I want every decision explained with its sources, so that I can verify a result against the original rulebook.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Rules_Engine produces an Outcome, THE Rules_Engine SHALL produce exactly one Decision_Trace for that Outcome, so that the number of Decision_Traces equals the number of Outcomes and each Decision_Trace is associated with exactly one Outcome. The Decision_Trace SHALL be produced for every Outcome value, including Indeterminate Outcomes and Outcomes that carry Diagnostics.
2. THE Decision_Trace SHALL list each applicable Rule with its identifier and every Source_Span of that Rule as retained in the Compiled_Rulebook, together with the document content hash that the Compiled_Rulebook records for the document of that Source_Span. The number of Source_Spans listed for a Rule SHALL equal the number of Source_Spans the Compiled_Rulebook retains for that Rule, and no Source_Span SHALL be omitted, added or altered.
3. THE Decision_Trace SHALL list, in the order of evaluation, each input value read with its name and exact value, each calculation step with its operation, its operands and its exact result, and each intermediate decision with the Rule that produced it. The order of evaluation SHALL be the order in which the Rules_Engine performed the reads, steps and decisions, and the same Compiled_Rulebook and inputs SHALL always produce the same order. Each entry SHALL appear exactly once for each time it was performed.
4. THE Decision_Trace SHALL list each Rule that was considered and did not apply, with its identifier, its Source_Spans, and a reason that names exactly one of the following: the specific condition that was not satisfied, the input value that was missing, or the Precedence_Declaration that overrode the Rule. The reason SHALL name that condition, input value or Precedence_Declaration by its identifier or name, and SHALL NOT be a generic statement such as "not applicable".
5. IF an input value required by a Rule is missing, THEN THE Decision_Trace SHALL record the missing input by name, SHALL mark the affected intermediate decision and the Outcome as Indeterminate, and SHALL NOT record the missing input as false, zero, or success. The Decision_Trace SHALL contain no input-read entry with a value for the missing input.
6. IF the Rules_Engine reports a conflict between Rules, THEN THE Decision_Trace SHALL list each conflicting Rule with its identifier and Source_Spans and SHALL record the Diagnostic code for the conflict, without selecting any of the conflicting Rules as the winner. The Decision_Trace SHALL contain no entry that marks any conflicting Rule as applied or as the winner.
7. WHEN the Explainer verifies a Decision_Trace against caller-supplied Source_Models, THE Explainer SHALL check for each Source_Span in the Decision_Trace that a Source_Model for the document exists, that its recorded content hash equals the hash in the Decision_Trace, and that the span lies within the bounds of the document. The Explainer SHALL check every Source_Span of the Decision_Trace and SHALL NOT stop at the first failure, so that one Diagnostic is returned for each Source_Span that fails.
8. IF a Source_Span in a Decision_Trace does not resolve under criterion 7, THEN THE Explainer SHALL return a Diagnostic with Severity Error that identifies the unresolved Source_Span and the Rule that references it, and SHALL NOT report the Decision_Trace as verified, where the verified status is derived from the verification Diagnostic_State only. The Diagnostic code SHALL be the code that criterion 16 defines for the failure.
9. WHEN a Decision_Trace is requested and Source_Models are supplied, THE CLI SHALL render, through the Explainer, the Decision_Trace in a human-readable form that includes, for each referenced Source_Span, the Source_Document identifier, the span position, and the exact source text within the span. The rendered source text SHALL be byte-for-byte equal to the bytes of the Source_Model between the span start and the span end, with no truncation, trimming or normalization.
10. WHERE the machine-readable output option is selected, THE CLI SHALL render the Decision_Trace in a structured format whose field order and element order are identical across repeated runs with the same Compiled_Rulebook and inputs, so that the two outputs are byte-for-byte identical. The output SHALL NOT contain a timestamp, a random value, an absolute file path or an environment-dependent value.
11. FOR ALL Decision_Traces, recomputing each recorded calculation step from its recorded operation and operands, and recomputing the Outcome from the recorded intermediate decisions, SHALL produce an Outcome equal to the original Outcome, with exact numeric equality and no tolerance (replay property). (UD-3-dependent: under Option R the replay parses the fraction form of Requirement 8 criterion 25.)
12. WHEN a published result is supplied for comparison, THE Rules_Engine SHALL report whether the published result equals the computed Outcome, and IF the results differ, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL list each differing value with the published value, the computed value, and the reference to the Decision_Trace entry that produced the computed value, and THE Rules_Engine SHALL apply Requirement 8 criterion 26 to each numeric difference. For a ranking Target the published value of a participant is a Rank_Position, and THE Rules_Engine SHALL compare it exactly with the computed Rank_Position of that participant. IF the published result gives different positions to participants of one computed Tie_Group, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL list one difference, of kind `tie_broken_without_declared_criterion`, for each participant of that Tie_Group whose published position differs from the shared computed position, with the computed Tie_Group members attached. IF the published result gives one position to participants that the computed result separates, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL list one difference, of kind `tie_not_in_computed_result`, for each such participant whose published position differs from its computed position, with the published tie members attached. Every other differing value is of kind `value_differs`. A participant that the published result does not list is not compared, and a computed position that is Indeterminate is never equal to a published value and never classifies a tie. Each difference SHALL carry the published value, the computed value and the reference to the Decision_Trace entry of the computed position. THE CLI evaluate command SHALL report each difference as one Warning Diagnostic whose code follows its kind: RT-EXE-0011 for `value_differs`, RT-EXE-0012 for `tie_broken_without_declared_criterion` and RT-EXE-0013 for `tie_not_in_computed_result`, and a difference SHALL NOT change the exit code. Equality SHALL be exact, with no numeric tolerance, and the differing values SHALL be listed in a fixed order that is the same across repeated runs. (UD-3-dependent.)
13. WHEN no Source_Model is supplied for rendering, THE CLI SHALL render each Source_Span of the Decision_Trace by its Location description only and SHALL state in the output that provenance was not verified. The output SHALL contain no source text for any Source_Span, and the Decision_Trace SHALL carry the Diagnostic PROV_NOT_VERIFIED_NO_SOURCES.
14. THE Explainer SHALL accept only a Decision_Trace and pre-loaded Source_Models as input, and SHALL read no file and open no network connection. THE Explainer SHALL NOT read the environment, the clock, the working directory or a random source.
15. WHEN the Explainer verifies a Decision_Trace, THE Explainer SHALL resolve each Source_Span only against the Source_Model of the document identified in the Source_Span, and SHALL derive the source text of a Source_Span only from that Source_Model, and SHALL NOT derive source text from the Decision_Trace, the Compiled_Rulebook, or any other source.
16. IF the Explainer cannot resolve a Source_Span of a Decision_Trace, THEN THE Explainer SHALL return the Diagnostic defined for the failure in the following table, SHALL render no source text for that Source_Span, SHALL render the recorded Source_Span position and the recorded document hash only, and SHALL mark the Decision_Trace as not verified. Each failed Source_Span SHALL produce exactly one Diagnostic, and the table row that applies SHALL be the first row, in table order, whose failure holds for that Source_Span.

| Failure | Diagnostic code | Severity | Primary Location |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No Source_Model supplied for the document identifier of the span, while other Source_Models were supplied | PROV_DOCUMENT_MISSING | Error | The Source_Span as recorded in the Decision_Trace |
| Recorded document hash differs from the hash of the supplied Source_Model | PROV_HASH_MISMATCH | Error | The Source_Span as recorded in the Decision_Trace |
| Span end exceeds the document length, or start is not less than end | PROV_SPAN_OUT_OF_RANGE | Error | The Source_Span as recorded in the Decision_Trace |
| Span start or end does not lie on a UTF-8 scalar boundary of the document | PROV_SPAN_NOT_ON_BOUNDARY | Error | The Source_Span as recorded in the Decision_Trace |
| Supplied Source_Model is invalid or has no Source_Span information | PROV_SOURCE_MODEL_INVALID | Error | The Document_Location of the document |
| No Source_Model supplied at all | PROV_NOT_VERIFIED_NO_SOURCES | Warning | The Cli_Location of the argument that names the Source_Models, or a No_Location with a reason code when the caller is a library |

17. THE Explainer SHALL mark a Decision_Trace as verified only when every Source_Span of that Decision_Trace resolved and the verification Diagnostic_State contains no Diagnostic of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, and the marking SHALL depend only on that Diagnostic_State. A Decision_Trace with zero Source_Spans SHALL NOT be marked as verified.
18. IF the evaluate command verifies a Decision_Trace and any Diagnostic of Severity Error of criterion 16 arises, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 2 as defined by Requirement 16 criterion 8 only when no earlier stage of the order of Requirement 16 criterion 17 failed, so that an Indeterminate Outcome of stage 6 (exit code 4) outranks a provenance Error of stage 7 and the exit code is 4 when both apply, SHALL write the Diagnostics to standard error, and SHALL write the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces with each affected Decision_Trace marked as not verified, so that no Outcome is presented as verified. Each Decision_Trace whose Source_Spans all resolved SHALL remain marked as verified.
19. IF the Explainer fails to resolve a Source_Span, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL leave every Outcome, every calculation step, and every Location in the Decision_Trace unchanged and SHALL change only the verification status and the attached verification Diagnostics, because the Rules_Engine reads no Source_Model. The Outcomes, calculation steps and Locations written after a failed verification SHALL be byte-for-byte equal to those written for the same Compiled_Rulebook and inputs without verification.
20. WHEN a published result is supplied for comparison and equals the computed Outcome under criterion 12, THE Rules_Engine SHALL report that the results are equal and SHALL list no differing value.
21. WHEN the Rules_Engine records the reason for a considered Rule that did not apply under criterion 4, THE Rules_Engine SHALL record exactly one reason for that Rule in that Decision_Trace.
22. IF a Decision_Trace has no Source_Span, or every Source_Span of the Decision_Trace fails to resolve under criterion 16, THEN THE Explainer SHALL mark that Decision_Trace as not verified, SHALL render no source text for it, and, for a Decision_Trace with no Source_Span, SHALL add the Warning PROV_TRACE_NO_SOURCE_SPANS (RT-PRV-0007) with the Location of the Decision_Trace as the primary Location and no Source_Span, so that a Decision_Trace with no resolvable source reference is never marked as verified.
23. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests that assert that a Decision_Trace with zero Source_Spans, a Decision_Trace whose every Source_Span fails with a different row of the table of criterion 16, and a Decision_Trace with one resolved and one failed Source_Span are each marked as not verified, that the first two have no source text rendered, that the third renders source text only for the resolved Source_Span, that a Decision_Trace whose every Source_Span resolves is marked as verified, and that the Outcomes and Locations are byte-identical in all cases (criterion 19).
24. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a CLI test of the evaluate command in which at least one Outcome is Indeterminate and at least one Decision_Trace fails verification under criterion 16 in the same run, and that asserts exit code 4 (not 2), both sets of Diagnostics on standard error, the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces written with each failed Decision_Trace marked as not verified, and, for the same inputs with all Outcomes Decided, exit code 2.

### Requirement 14: First-Version Support Boundary

**User Story:** As a project owner, I want a precise list of what the first version supports, so that no rulebook is wrongly presented as supported.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL support exactly these five Rule kinds in the first version: eligibility predicate, derived numeric value, weighted score, ranking with ordered tie-breaking criteria, and explicit default value.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL support exactly these expression operations in the first version: Exact_Number addition, subtraction, multiplication and division; comparison (equal, not equal, less than, less than or equal, greater than, greater than or equal); Boolean conjunction, disjunction and negation; membership in a set declared in the Rulebook; field access; and aggregation (sum, minimum, maximum, count) over a collection declared in the Rulebook.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL support exactly these value types in the first version: Exact_Number, Boolean, text, date without time, and enumeration declared in the Rulebook.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL support exactly the rounding modes listed in Requirement 8 criterion 14.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT support in the first version: tournament progression, seeding, bracket generation, prize allocation, slot-based qualification (the allocation of a limited number of places, slots, or stages; the Qualification_Predicate of criterion 11 is not slot-based qualification), multi-stage competitions, time-of-day values, user-defined functions, loops, recursion, and rules expressed only in free text.
6. IF a Rule requires a Rule kind, expression operation, value type, or rounding mode outside the lists in criteria 1 to 5 (as extended by criterion 12 where it applies), THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Unsupported Diagnostic per unsupported construct. Each Diagnostic SHALL name the capability and have the first Source_Span of the Rule containing the construct as its primary Location. THE Validator SHALL NOT compile the Rule, SHALL NOT approximate the capability, and SHALL NOT silently drop the Rule.
7. IF a Rulebook contains both supported and unsupported Rules, THEN THE Validator SHALL return Unsupported Diagnostics for the unsupported Rules and SHALL mark the validation result as failed, so that the Compiler produces no Compiled_Rulebook until every unsupported Rule is removed or replaced.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL document the First_Version_Support_Boundary in a user-facing document that lists every item in criteria 1 to 5, the Qualification_Predicate of criterion 11, and the UD-32 option that criteria 12 and 13 apply, and each release SHALL update that document before the release is tagged.
9. WHEN a release adds support for a Rule kind, THE RuleTrace SHALL include in that release at least one Fixture with golden expected decisions and traces, and at least one negative test that asserts the exact Diagnostic code and Location for an invalid use of that Rule kind.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL support the Precedence_Declaration as a Rule_IR element that is not a Rule kind, and SHALL accept it only for the Conflict classes named in Requirement 6 criterion 20.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL express qualification in the first version only as a Qualification_Predicate, which is a Rule of kind eligibility predicate whose operands are limited to constants, fields, derived numeric values, and weighted scores that the operations of criterion 2 can express (for example, a weighted score greater than or equal to a declared threshold, or a count of a declared collection greater than or equal to a declared minimum), so that a Qualification_Predicate decides for each participant whether a stated condition holds, allocates no place, slot, or stage, and fixes no number of qualifiers. The existing operations of criterion 2 cannot express a condition on the Rank_Position of a participant, because a Rank_Position is part of the Outcome of a ranking Rule and is neither a field, a derived numeric value, nor a weighted score of the participant (UD-32).
12. WHERE the UD-32 option Rank_Reference is selected (it is, see `tools/decisions/UD-32.md`), THE RuleTrace SHALL support exactly one additional expression operation, rank reference, which reads the Rank_Position of the participant under one declared ranking Target as an Exact_Number operand, and THE Validator, THE Compiler, and THE Rules_Engine SHALL apply these rules: (a) WHEN the ranking Outcome for the participant is Decided, the operand SHALL be the Rank_Position, and every member of a Tie_Group SHALL read the shared Rank_Position of the group; (b) WHEN the position of the participant is Indeterminate, the operand SHALL be Indeterminate and criterion 4 of Requirement 11 SHALL apply; (c) IF the ranking Target is not declared, THEN the Validator SHALL return the undeclared-reference Error of Requirement 6 criterion 3; (d) IF a ranking Rule depends, directly or through other Rules, on a Rule that reads the rank of that same ranking Target, THEN the Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic RANK_REFERENCE_CYCLE per cycle that names every Rule on the cycle, with the first Source_Span of the first Rule in ascending Rule identifier order as its primary Location; (e) WHEN a Tie_Group straddles a threshold of the form Rank_Position less than or equal to N, the Rules_Engine SHALL give the same result to every member of the group and SHALL NOT truncate the group to N participants; (f) the Decision_Trace SHALL record the ranking Rule, the Rank_Position read, and the Tie_Group membership; and (g) THE Validator SHALL accept a rank reference only in a Rule that an applicable competition rule explicitly declares to read the rank, and THE Rules_Engine SHALL record the Source_Spans of that declaring Rule as the provenance of the read. (UD-32-dependent.)
13. WHERE the UD-32 option Threshold_Only is selected (it is not; retained for the case that the user changes the option), IF a Rule requires a condition on the Rank_Position of a participant, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Unsupported Diagnostic per occurrence under criterion 6 that names the capability rank reference, and THE Bracket_Check demonstration SHALL express qualification only as Qualification_Predicates over scores and derived values and SHALL report each rank-based qualification clause of the demonstration document as Unsupported (Requirement 33 criterion 6). (UD-32-dependent.)
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a Fixture with a Qualification_Predicate over a weighted score with golden Outcomes and Decision_Traces, a Fixture in which a Tie_Group straddles the threshold of the Qualification_Predicate (WHERE the option Rank_Reference is selected, a threshold on the Rank_Position), a negative test of criterion 12 (d) that asserts the code RANK_REFERENCE_CYCLE, the Severity Error, and the primary Location, and, WHERE the option Threshold_Only is selected, a negative test that asserts the Unsupported Diagnostic of criterion 13 with its capability name and primary Location.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep the rank reference operation of criterion 12 and the External_Ranking_Value separate: THE Rules_Engine SHALL NOT derive an External_Ranking_Value from a Rank_Position, and the rank reference operation SHALL NOT read an External_Ranking_Value. (UD-32 constraint 2.)
16. THE Validator SHALL accept an External_Ranking_Value only as a declared Rulebook input whose declaration names the input that carries the ranking authority, the input that carries the edition or effective date, the input that carries the provenance, and at least one Source_Span of the competition clause that gives the value its use, and SHALL accept no declared default for any of these inputs.
17. IF the declaration of an External_Ranking_Value omits the authority input, the edition or effective date input, the provenance input, or the Source_Span, or declares a default for any of them, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic EXTERNAL_RANKING_DECLARATION_INVALID per declaration that names the missing or defaulted element, with the first Source_Span of the declaration or, where none exists, the document Location of the Rulebook as its primary Location.
18. THE Validator SHALL accept an External_Ranking_Value only as the tie-breaker at the index that the declaration of criterion 16 assigns within the ordered tie-breakers of one declared ranking Rule, and SHALL record the Source_Span of the tie-breaking clause that assigns that index.
19. IF a Rule uses an External_Ranking_Value as a ranking key, as a tie-breaker at an index other than the declared index, or as an operand of any other Rule kind or expression, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic EXTERNAL_RANKING_POSITION_INVALID per use, with the first Source_Span of the Rule containing the use as its primary Location, and SHALL NOT produce a Compiled_Rulebook.
20. WHEN the Rules_Engine evaluates a tie-breaker that is an External_Ranking_Value, THE Rules_Engine SHALL read only the value, the authority, the edition or effective date, and the provenance that Competition_Data supplies for the participant, and SHALL NOT fetch, infer, estimate, or invent any of them.
21. IF a tied participant has an External_Ranking_Value that is unverifiable, THEN THE Rules_Engine SHALL return Indeterminate Outcomes under Requirement 11 criteria 1, 3 and 6 and Requirement 12 for every member of the tied set, naming the field, and SHALL NOT return a numeric ordering of that set. An External_Ranking_Value is unverifiable WHEN the value, the authority, the edition or effective date, or the provenance is absent, explicitly marked missing, or an empty string. THE Rules_Engine SHALL perform no network access and no comparison with the original source, so the verification of the provenance against the ranking authority is a human review step that the Decision_Trace supports.
22. WHEN the Rules_Engine applies an External_Ranking_Value, THE Decision_Trace SHALL record the value, the authority, the edition or effective date, the provenance, the participant, the ranking Rule, the declared index, and the Source_Spans of the declaring Rule and of the tie-breaking clause.
23. WHILE participants of a ranking Rule are separated by a tie-breaker that precedes the declared index of an External_Ranking_Value, THE Rules_Engine SHALL NOT read the External_Ranking_Value of those participants, and THE Decision_Trace SHALL contain no read entry for it.
24. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests that assert: (a) a Fixture in which the External_Ranking_Value separates a Tie_Group at the declared index with golden Outcomes and Decision_Traces; (b) a negative test with a missing authority and a negative test with a missing edition, each asserting an Indeterminate Outcome that names the field and no numeric ordering; (c) a negative test with an empty provenance; (d) negative tests of criterion 19 for each of the three misuses, each asserting the code EXTERNAL_RANKING_POSITION_INVALID, the Severity Error and the primary Location; (e) a negative test of criterion 17 for each omitted element; and (f) a test of criterion 23 that the earlier tie-breaker alone decides and no read entry exists.
25. IF the Competition_Data format of the design cannot carry the authority, the edition or effective date, and the provenance of an External_Ranking_Value, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT add the carrying form without a recorded user decision (RN-30), and THE Validator SHALL return the Unsupported Diagnostic of criterion 6 for every Rule that requires an External_Ranking_Value until that decision is recorded.

### Requirement 15: Shared Foundation for Prize Check and Bracket Check

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want Prize Check and Bracket Check to share one engine, so that business logic is never duplicated.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Rules_Engine crate SHALL contain zero identifiers, type names, field names, or string literals that name Prize_Check or Bracket_Check, and SHALL contain zero domain-specific terms outside the Rule_IR vocabulary, as verified by an automated source scan that fails the build on any match.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL expose each pipeline stage (ingestion, extraction, Rule_IR, validation, compilation, execution, explanation) as a public library function whose input and output are typed values, callable by an application without invoking the CLI binary or spawning a process.
3. IF a future application requires a Rule kind that the Rule_IR does not define, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject the Rule with a Diagnostic that has a code, Severity, message, and Location indicating an unsupported Rule kind, and SHALL accept the new Rule kind only after it is added to the Rule_IR, Validator, Compiler, and Rules_Engine.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL implement each pipeline stage in a separate crate with a documented public interface, SHALL implement the Explainer in a crate that is separate from the Rules_Engine crate, and SHALL contain no dependency from any stage crate on a crate for a later stage, where later is defined by the stage numbers of the Pipeline table in the Introduction and by the Pipeline_Contracts of Requirement 24, as verified by an automated dependency-graph check that fails the build on a violation or a cycle.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep application-specific workflow code, including input mapping and report layout, in crates that depend on the Rules_Engine crate, and SHALL contain no dependency from the Rules_Engine crate on any application crate, as verified by the same dependency-graph check.
6. WHERE Prize_Check or Bracket_Check is added, THE RuleTrace SHALL compute its eligibility, scoring, ranking, and tie-breaking results by calling the shared Rules_Engine, and SHALL contain no second implementation of those computations in the application crate.
7. WHEN two applications evaluate the same Compiled_Rulebook against the same Competition_Data through the shared Rules_Engine, THE RuleTrace SHALL produce byte-identical decisions and Decision_Traces.
8. THE first version of RuleTrace SHALL contain exactly two Application_Layer crates, the Prize_Check crate and the Bracket_Check crate (Requirement 31), and SHALL contain in no crate any code that performs prize allocation, slot-based qualification, seeding, bracket generation, or tournament progression, as verified by the source scans of Requirement 31 criteria 10 and 11. This criterion supersedes the earlier statement that the first version contains no Prize_Check crate and no Bracket_Check crate.
9. THE Rules_Engine crate SHALL contain no dependency on the Explainer crate, on a crate that reads files, on a network client crate, or on any Auxiliary_Crate, as verified by the dependency-graph check of criterion 4.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL make criteria 5, 6, and 7 testable for the first-version Prize_Check and Bracket_Check crates through Requirement 31 criteria 4 to 11, and criterion 1 SHALL continue to apply to the Rules_Engine crate, so that the Rules_Engine crate contains no identifier, type name, field name, or string literal that names Prize_Check, Bracket_Check, or an Application_Layer.

### Requirement 16: Minimum End-to-End Vertical Slice

**User Story:** As a project owner, I want a small end-to-end slice delivered first, so that the architecture is proven before it is widened.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Source_Document, a Rule_IR file, an Approval_Ledger file, and a Competition_Data file are provided and the CLI commands ingest, validate, compile, and evaluate are run in that order, each consuming the artifact written by the preceding command, and every command succeeds, THE CLI SHALL exit with code 0 from each of the four commands, and THE evaluate command SHALL write the Outcomes and Decision_Traces to the output location, with one Outcome and one Decision_Trace for each participant in the Competition_Data file.
2. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one Fixture containing at least one eligibility predicate, at least one weighted score using exact decimal weights, and at least one ranking with at least one tie-breaking criterion, and the Fixture SHALL have a stored expected output that contains the expected Outcomes and Decision_Traces for every participant of the Fixture.
3. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one Fixture in which a participant has a Missing_Value used by a Rule, the Outcome for that participant is Indeterminate, and the Decision_Trace of that participant names the missing field and gives the Source_Span (document id, start offset, end offset) of the Rule that required it, and the test SHALL assert the missing field name and each of the three Source_Span values.
4. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one Fixture in which two or more participants remain tied after all declared tie-breaking criteria, the Outcome is a single Tie_Group listing every tied participant in ascending participant identifier order, and no participant in the group is ranked above another, and the test SHALL assert that the Outcomes contain exactly one Tie_Group for those participants.
5. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one Fixture with an unsupported Rule, and the test SHALL assert that the Diagnostic has the unsupported-construct code, Severity Unsupported, and a primary Location that is a Source_Span equal to the first Source_Span of that Rule (document id, start offset, end offset).
6. WHEN the vertical slice runs on a Fixture into an initially empty output location, THE CLI SHALL produce output files whose bytes are identical to the stored expected output files, and the comparison SHALL fail if any file differs by one byte or is missing or extra.
7. WHEN the same Fixture inputs are run twice in separate processes, THE CLI SHALL produce byte-identical output files, byte-identical standard output and standard error, and the same exit code on both runs.
8. IF a command ends in invalid input, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 2. Invalid input means a missing, unreadable, or malformed input file; a Source_Document that is not valid UTF-8, is oversize, or has an unsupported format; a Rule_IR syntax error, unknown structural name, unrepresentable numeric literal, or unrecognized IR_Version; an unknown command name or a missing required argument; a supplied Source_Model that is missing for a document or whose hash differs from the recorded hash; an Approval_Ledger or Proposal_Store that is missing, unreadable, malformed, or fails the integrity check of Requirement 26; a review command that names a Rule_Revision that does not exist, a Source_Model that is not supplied, or an invalid rejection reason; or a contradictory diagnostic or lifecycle state (Requirement 22). THE CLI SHALL use exit code 2 for invalid input only and for no other failure.
9. IF the CLI exits with a nonzero code, THEN THE CLI SHALL write at least one Diagnostic or an Indeterminate Outcome to the output location (or to standard error when the failure occurs before any output location can be used), and SHALL NOT write partial Outcomes as if execution had succeeded, except that WHERE Requirement 13 criterion 18 applies, THE CLI SHALL also write the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces marked as not verified, and except that WHERE the validate command fails only because of blocking Diagnostics (Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported) in the Rulebook, THE CLI SHALL write the Validation_Result carrying those Diagnostics to the output location, which is not an executable artifact and which the compile command refuses with exit code 3 (Requirement 17 criterion 9).
10. IF a validate or compile command ends with a Blocking_Diagnostic in the Diagnostic_State of its result, including APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED and APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 3, and a result whose Diagnostic_State contains only Warning Diagnostics SHALL NOT cause exit code 3.
11. IF an evaluate command completes with at least one Indeterminate Outcome, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 4.
12. IF a command fails for a reason that is not invalid input, not a validation or compilation failure, and not an Indeterminate Outcome, including an internal invariant violation or a failure to write the output location when no earlier stage failed, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 70 (internal error), SHALL write a Diagnostic of Severity Error that identifies the failure to standard error, and SHALL NOT exit with code 2, 3, or 4 for that failure alone.
13. THE CLI SHALL exit with code 0 only when every stage that the command ran succeeded, meaning that the Diagnostic_State of the command contains no Blocking_Diagnostic, no input was invalid, no Outcome is Indeterminate, and no internal error occurred.
14. IF more than one failure case applies in the same command run, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with the code of the earliest pipeline stage that failed within that command.
15. THE CLI SHALL use exactly these exit codes, as summarized in the following table: 0 for success; 2 for invalid input (criterion 8); 3 for validation or compilation failure (criterion 10) and for a refused review action (criterion 19); 4 for Indeterminate Outcome (criterion 11); 70 for internal error (criterion 12). No other exit code SHALL be returned by the ruletrace executable.

| Exit code | Meaning | Commands that can return it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Success, with at most Warning Diagnostics | ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, review list, review approve, review reject |
| 2 | Invalid input | ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, review list, review approve, review reject |
| 3 | Validation or compilation failure, or a refused review action | validate, compile, review approve, review reject |
| 4 | At least one Indeterminate Outcome | evaluate |
| 70 | Internal error | ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, review list, review approve, review reject |

16. THE AI_Tool (ruletrace-ai) SHALL use exactly the exit codes of the following table, and the table of criterion 15 SHALL apply to the ruletrace executable only. The exit code 4 is not used by the AI_Tool. A stored batch whose Proposals carry Semantic_Outcome Diagnostics is a success with exit code 0 and the Warning AI_BATCH_HAS_BLOCKING_PROPOSALS (UD-26).

| Exit code | Meaning | Diagnostic mnemonics | Commands that can return it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Success, with at most Warning Diagnostics | AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED, AI_BATCH_HAS_BLOCKING_PROPOSALS, AI_CONFIG_PERMISSIONS | propose |
| 2 | Invalid input: AI disabled, invalid AI_Configuration, inline secret, insecure endpoint, invalid limit, unknown provider, endpoint host not permitted, refused training-mode model, unsupported capability, missing credential, missing consent, invalid request inputs, invalid Proposal_Store, and the codes of Requirement 22 | AI_DISABLED, AI_CONFIG_INVALID, AI_CONFIG_INLINE_SECRET, AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_INSECURE, AI_CONFIG_LIMIT_INVALID, AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN, AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_HOST_NOT_PERMITTED, AI_MODEL_TRAINING_MODE_REFUSED, AI_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED, AI_CREDENTIAL_MISSING, AI_CONSENT_MISSING, AI_REQUEST_INVALID, AI_STORE_INVALID | propose, models |
| 3 | Provenance failure: the complete batch is rejected | AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_UNRESOLVED, AI_PROPOSAL_HASH_MISMATCH, AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_OUTSIDE_CONTEXT, AI_PROPOSAL_QUOTE_MISMATCH | propose |
| 5 | Provider failure after the retry policy, and model catalogue failure | AI_PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED, AI_PROVIDER_ERROR, AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED, AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, AI_PROVIDER_TLS_FAILED, AI_MODEL_DISCOVERY_FAILED, AI_MODEL_NOT_IN_CATALOGUE | propose, models |
| 6 | Provider_Response malformed or excluded: the complete batch is rejected | AI_RESPONSE_MALFORMED, AI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA_VIOLATION, AI_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE, AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD, AI_RESPONSE_INCOMPLETE | propose |
| 70 | Internal error, including a failed Transmission_Log or Proposal_Store write | AI_TRANSMISSION_LOG_FAILED, AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED, an internal error code | propose |

17. IF more than one failure applies in the same run of an executable, THEN THE executable SHALL exit with the code of the earliest stage of the applicable order. For ruletrace pipeline commands the order is: argument and input file reading (invalid input), then stages 1 to 7 of the Pipeline table in stage order, then the writing of the output location. For the ruletrace review command group the order is: argument parsing, Approval_Ledger and Proposal_Store load with integrity check, Rulebook and Source_Model load, the refusal checks of Requirement 5 criterion 10, then the atomic write. For ruletrace-ai the order is the table that follows this criterion, and invalid input precedes all other steps.

| Order | Step of ruletrace-ai | Failure mnemonics | Exit code |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Argument parsing | AI_DISABLED (no AI_Configuration named), Cli_Location errors | 2 |
| 2 | AI_Configuration load and check, in the order of Requirement 28 criterion 17 | AI_DISABLED (AI not enabled), AI_CONFIG_INVALID, AI_CONFIG_INLINE_SECRET, AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_INSECURE, AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_HOST_NOT_PERMITTED, AI_CONFIG_LIMIT_INVALID, AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN, AI_MODEL_TRAINING_MODE_REFUSED, AI_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED | 2 |
| 3 | Credential and consent resolution | AI_CREDENTIAL_MISSING, AI_CONSENT_MISSING | 2 |
| 4 | Input load, request build and credential check, and idempotency check | AI_REQUEST_INVALID, AI_REQUEST_CONTAINS_CREDENTIAL, AI_STORE_INVALID, the codes of Requirement 22 | 2 |
| 5 | Request build and Transmission_Log write | AI_TRANSMISSION_LOG_FAILED | 70 |
| 6 | Model verification (WHERE verify_model is true) and provider call with the retry policy | AI_MODEL_DISCOVERY_FAILED, AI_MODEL_NOT_IN_CATALOGUE, AI_PROVIDER_* | 5 |
| 7 | Response read and parse | AI_RESPONSE_MALFORMED, AI_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE, AI_RESPONSE_INCOMPLETE | 6 |
| 8 | Schema validation | AI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA_VIOLATION, AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD, AI_REVISION_TARGET_MISMATCH | 6 |
| 9 | Provenance verification | AI_PROPOSAL_* | 3 |
| 10 | Semantic validation | none, because a Semantic_Outcome is not a failure | none |
| 11 | Atomic Proposal_Store write | AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED | 70 |

18. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one AI-assisted Fixture in which the Mock_Provider returns a scripted Provider_Response for a Source_Document, ruletrace-ai stores the Proposal in the Proposal_Store, ruletrace review approve with a caller-supplied Reviewer and time adopts the Rule_Revision into the Rulebook and appends the Approval_Record to the Approval_Ledger, and the CLI commands validate, compile, and evaluate then produce Outcomes and Decision_Traces whose bytes are identical to the stored expected files. The Compiled_Rulebook of the Fixture SHALL be byte-identical to the Compiled_Rulebook that the same CLI commands produce from a manually authored Rulebook whose Rule_Revisions have the same Rule_Content and the same Revision_Numbers.
19. IF a review approve or review reject command refuses the request under Requirement 5 criterion 10, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 3, SHALL write the REVIEW Diagnostic to standard error, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger, the Rulebook, and the Proposal_Store byte-identical, and IF the atomic write fails, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 70 and leave the same files byte-identical.
20. THE vertical slice SHALL include at least one Fixture with an Approval_Ledger in which a Rule_Revision is in an Unresolved_Approval_State, and the test SHALL assert the Diagnostic APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, the Severity Error, the first Source_Span of the Rule as the primary Location, exit code 3, and no Compiled_Rulebook.
21. WHEN the compile command exits with code 3, THE CLI SHALL NOT write a Compiled_Rulebook file, and the output location SHALL contain no file that is a partial Compiled_Rulebook.
22. WHEN a Fixture test of criterion 3 runs evaluate, THE CLI SHALL exit with code 4, and WHEN a Fixture test of criterion 4 runs evaluate and no Outcome is Indeterminate, THE CLI SHALL exit with code 0.
23. IF an evaluate command completes with an Indeterminate Outcome that the execution-time conflict check of Requirement 12 criterion 5 produced, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 4 under criterion 11, SHALL NOT exit with code 3, and SHALL NOT exit with code 0, because exit code 3 is returned only for a Blocking_Diagnostic of validate or compile and for a refused review action (criterion 15), and the Error Diagnostic EXECUTION_CONFLICT_DETECTED (RT-DGN-0004) of an evaluate run does not change that classification.
24. IF a ruletrace-ai propose run ends with the Warning AI_RUN_INCOMPLETE (RT-AI-0306), THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL exit with the exit code that the table of criterion 16 gives for the failing Provider_Request (3, 5, 6, or 70, or 2 for invalid input), SHALL apply criterion 17 to choose among several failures, and SHALL treat the Warning as leaving that exit code unchanged, so that the Warning is not a member of the row for exit code 0 of the table of criterion 16.

### Requirement 17: Command-Line Interface

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want to run each pipeline stage from the command line, so that I can inspect intermediate results.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE CLI SHALL provide exactly four pipeline commands named ingest, validate, compile, and evaluate, plus one non-pipeline review command group with exactly the subcommands list, approve, and reject, and each command SHALL invoke only the stages assigned to it in this table and SHALL consume only the typed artifacts named in this table. A test SHALL assert that every other command or subcommand name returns exit code 2 under criterion 5.

| Command | Pipeline stages | Consumes | Produces |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ingest | 1 (and 2 only with the --candidates option) | Source_Document files | Source_Model files, and with --candidates a Candidate_Clause list |
| validate | 3 (parsing) and 4 | Rule_IR file, Approval_Ledger file, Source_Model files, optional Candidate_Clause list | Validation_Result artifact |
| compile | 5 | Validation_Result artifact | Compiled_Rulebook artifact |
| review (list, approve, reject) | Not a pipeline stage; the Approval_Status operations of Requirement 5 criterion 8 | Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Proposal_Store, Review_Decision arguments, Source_Model files | Appended Approval_Ledger, Rulebook with the adopted Rule_Revision, updated Proposal_Store reference, or a listing |
| evaluate | 6 and 7 | Compiled_Rulebook artifact, Competition_Data file, Questions, optional Source_Model files, optional Published_Result | Outcomes and Decision_Traces |

2. WHEN a command succeeds and the user specifies an output location, THE CLI SHALL write the complete result to that location, write zero bytes to standard output except an optional confirmation of exactly one line terminated by a line feed, write nothing to standard error except Warning Diagnostics, and return exit code 0.
3. WHEN a command succeeds and the user specifies no output location, THE CLI SHALL write the complete result to standard output, write nothing to standard error except Warning Diagnostics, and return exit code 0.
4. IF a command fails, THEN THE CLI SHALL write each Diagnostic, including its code, Severity, message, and Location, to standard error, and SHALL write zero bytes of result to standard output and create no file at the specified output location, except the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces of an evaluation that contains Indeterminate Outcomes (Requirement 16, criterion 9) the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces marked as not verified under Requirement 13 criterion 18, and the Validation_Result that the validate command writes when only blocking Diagnostics prevent success (Requirement 16 criterion 9), and SHALL return the nonzero exit code that Requirement 16 criterion 15 assigns to the earliest failing stage (criterion 14 of Requirement 16).
5. IF a command receives an unknown command name, a missing required argument, or an input file that does not exist or cannot be read, THEN THE CLI SHALL write at least one Diagnostic to standard error, perform no pipeline stage, create or modify no output file, and return exit code 2. The primary Location of the Diagnostic SHALL be a Cli_Location that names the command name or the argument for an unknown command name or a missing required argument, and an Input_Location that names the input file for a file that does not exist or cannot be read.
6. IF validation or evaluation completes with at least one Diagnostic of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, THEN THE CLI SHALL return the nonzero exit code defined in Requirement 16 criteria 8 to 12 for that failure, and IF it completes with Diagnostics of only Severity Warning, THEN THE CLI SHALL return exit code 0 and write each Warning Diagnostic to standard error.
7.
