WHEN the same command is run twice in separate processes with identical arguments and byte-identical input files, THE CLI SHALL produce byte-identical standard output, byte-identical standard error, byte-identical output files, and identical exit codes. A test SHALL assert this under at least two different values of the system clock, the environment variables, the working directory, and the process locale.
8. THE CLI SHALL complete every command without opening a network connection, without requiring an API key or any AI adapter configuration, without reading any AI-related environment variable or AI_Configuration file, without invoking any AI_Crate, and without executing code contained in any input document, and SHALL produce byte-identical standard output, standard error, output files, and exit code whether or not an AI_Configuration file or an API key exists on the machine.
9. IF validation reports at least one Diagnostic of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, THEN THE CLI SHALL create no Compiled_Rulebook file, SHALL run no later pipeline stage, and SHALL exit with code 3.
10. THE CLI SHALL perform all file reading and writing, SHALL load each Source_Model into memory before it calls the Explainer, and SHALL pass only in-memory typed values to the Rules_Engine, so that the Rules_Engine and the Explainer open no file in any test.
11. THE review command group SHALL read no environment variable, no AI_Configuration, and no clock, SHALL take the Reviewer identity and the time only from command arguments, and SHALL write the Approval_Ledger only by the atomic append of Requirement 26 criterion 19. IF the Reviewer identity or the time argument is missing, THEN THE review command group SHALL return exit code 2 under criterion 5 and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger byte-identical.
12. WHEN a command writes more than one Diagnostic to standard error, THE CLI SHALL write them in the order of the Diagnostic_State of the command result, so that two runs of criterion 7 produce the same order.
13. IF a command fails and the specified output location names a file that existed before the command started, THEN THE CLI SHALL leave that file byte-identical to its content before the command started.

### Requirement 18: Optional AI Boundary and Trust Rules

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want a provider-independent AI boundary with strict trust rules, so that AI can propose rules without weakening trust in the compiler.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHERE no AI component is present or AI is not enabled, THE RuleTrace SHALL execute every function specified in Requirements 1 to 17, 19 to 22, 24 to 26, and the criteria of Requirement 27 that concern ruletrace, without requiring an API key, network access, or AI provider library, and SHALL produce byte-identical output of ruletrace for identical inputs with and without any AI_Crate or the ruletrace-ai executable installed, built, or configured. Output comprises standard output, standard error, the exit code, and every file ruletrace writes.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL define the AI_Boundary as a typed Proposal interface that accepts exactly five typed Proposal kinds: clause interpretation, structured rule suggestion, clause relationship, conflict highlight, and candidate test. The interface SHALL accept no sixth kind.
3. WHEN a Proposal is received through the Proposal interface and passes the checks of criterion 4, THE RuleTrace SHALL return a typed Proposal value with the proposer identity (1 to 200 Unicode scalar values) and at least one Source_Span the Proposal refers to, and the Proposal SHALL have an Approval_Status of Proposed and never Approved.
4. IF a received Proposal has an unrecognized kind, an empty proposer identity, a proposer identity longer than 200 Unicode scalar values, zero Source_Spans, or a Source_Span that does not resolve to an existing document span in a supplied Source_Model, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject the Proposal with one Diagnostic of Severity Error per defect, each with exactly one catalogue code from the Diagnostic_Catalogue (Requirement 7 criterion 17) and an Input_Location naming the offending field or span (Requirement 7 criterion 15), and SHALL return no Proposal value and write no Proposal_Store entry.
5. WHEN a Proposal of kind structured rule suggestion is converted into a Rule, THE Validator SHALL apply to that Rule the same validation checks as to a human-authored Rule and SHALL emit the same Diagnostic code and Severity for the same defect. For two Rules with identical Rule_Content and different Origin, the Diagnostic lists SHALL be identical.
6. WHEN a Rule created from a Proposal is to become Approved, THE RuleTrace SHALL apply the transition rules of Requirement 5 criteria 8 and 9 and no other path to Approved.
7. IF a request to set Approval_Status to Approved for a Rule created from a Proposal lacks an Approval_Record, names no Reviewer, or targets a Rule with a Blocking_Diagnostic, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL refuse the change as defined in Requirement 5 criterion 10. The Approval_Ledger and the Rule_Revision SHALL remain byte-identical after the refusal.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a Rule created from a Proposal in a Compiled_Rulebook or an evaluation only when its Approval_Status is Approved and a matching Approval_Record exists, and SHALL include no Proposal that has not become such a Rule. For a Proposal-origin Rule_Revision in the Proposed or Rejected state, the Compiled_Rulebook SHALL contain zero compiled elements derived from it.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL declare no dependency on an AI provider library, network client library, or API key in any core crate, as verified by inspection of each core crate's dependency list. The inspection SHALL be an automated check that fails when any core crate's resolved dependency list contains such a library.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL place every AI_Provider implementation, every network client, and every credential access only in an AI_Integration_Crate or the Mock_Provider crate (and the Mock_Provider crate SHALL contain no network client and no credential access), SHALL make no network call from any Core_Crate or from the ruletrace executable, and SHALL make a network call only from an AI_Integration_Crate while AI is enabled by an explicit AI_Configuration and a per-invocation Transmission_Consent covers every document sent. This criterion supersedes the earlier statement that the first version contains no AI provider implementation and makes no network call.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL leave an Approved Rule_Revision byte-identical when any Proposal refers to it, and SHALL NOT modify an Approved Rule_Revision except through a content change that Requirement 25 criterion 8 treats as invalidating its Approval_Records. A change of an Approved Rule is made only by creating a new Proposed Rule_Revision (Requirement 25 criterion 10).
12. THE RuleTrace SHALL define the AI_Adapter as a provider-independent typed interface in an Auxiliary_Crate outside the Core_Crates, whose public signature takes a Provider_Request and returns either the Provider_Response as untrusted bytes with provider metadata or a Provider_Failure, and whose public signature contains no type that belongs to a specific AI provider.
13. THE RuleTrace SHALL contain no dependency from any AI_Crate on the Compiler crate, on the Rules_Engine crate, on the crate that contains the Approval_Status operations of Requirement 5 criterion 12, or on any crate that contains the Approval_Ledger append function, as verified by the dependency-graph check of Requirement 24 criterion 5, so that no AI component can compile a rule, execute a rule, approve a Rule_Revision, or write the Approval_Ledger. The check SHALL cover direct and transitive dependencies and SHALL fail on any one such edge.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL give every Proposal and every Rule_Revision that the AI_Tool creates the Approval_Status Proposed and Origin AI_Proposal, and WHEN a Provider_Response supplies an Approval_Status other than Proposed, an Approval_Record, a Reviewer, or a validation status, THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the Provider_Response under Requirement 23 criterion 5. The rejection SHALL store no part of that Provider_Response.
15. WHILE no AI_Configuration with AI enabled is supplied, or WHILE no Transmission_Consent names a document, THE AI_Tool SHALL treat AI as disabled for that document, SHALL make no network call, SHALL read no credential, and SHALL send no Source_Document text to any AI_Provider. The observable result SHALL be zero network connections opened, zero environment variable reads of the credential, and zero bytes of Source_Document text in any Provider_Request for that document.
16. THE RuleTrace SHALL treat every Provider_Response as untrusted data, and SHALL NOT execute, evaluate, load, or follow as an instruction any content of a Provider_Response, including text that asks to approve, skip validation, change a status, or reveal configuration. For a Provider_Response that contains such text, the Approval_Ledger, the validation outcome, and every output that does not quote the text SHALL be identical to those for the same Provider_Response with the text removed.
17. IF an AI_Provider fails, returns malformed output, or is unavailable, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL leave every Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Compiled_Rulebook, and Proposal_Store entry that existed before the failed run byte-identical (except for a batch that an earlier Provider_Request of the same run kept under Requirement 23 criteria 35 to 37), and SHALL leave every function of Requirements 1 to 17 operable, as defined in Requirement 23 criteria 17 to 20 and Requirement 27 criterion 8. The byte comparison SHALL cover every file of those artifacts before and after the run, and the Proposal_Store is compared with the failed-run store state of Requirement 23 criterion 35.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL treat the Proposal kinds clause relationship, conflict highlight, and candidate test as advisory, so that no advisory Proposal creates a Rule_Revision, enters the Rulebook, or enters any executable artifact. After a run that stores only advisory Proposals, the Rulebook and the Compiled_Rulebook SHALL be byte-identical to their state before the run.

### Requirement 19: Safety of Document Content

**User Story:** As a security-conscious operator, I want document content treated as data, so that a malicious rulebook cannot run code on my machine.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL treat all Source_Document, Rule_IR, and Competition_Data content as data, so that no byte sequence in that content changes which code path RuleTrace runs, other than the parsing, validation, compilation, and evaluation paths that the Rule_IR language defines.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT execute, evaluate, or load code from Source_Document, Rule_IR, or Competition_Data content, including embedded scripts, expressions, shell commands, and references to external programs or libraries. This means RuleTrace SHALL NOT spawn a process, load a dynamic library, or open a network connection because of such content.
3. IF an input file's size in bytes is greater than the effective size limit, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject the file before parsing any of its content, return an Error Diagnostic that names the file, the effective limit in bytes, and the actual size in bytes, and leave all other loaded inputs unchanged.
4. IF a Rule_IR document's nesting depth is greater than the effective nesting depth limit, THEN THE Rule_IR parser SHALL stop parsing, return an Error Diagnostic that identifies the document id, the effective limit, and the actual depth reached, with an Input_Location giving the byte offset or the line and column where the limit was exceeded, and produce no partial Rule_IR. Nesting depth is the count of open JSON-like containers (objects and arrays) at a point in the document, and the outermost container has depth 1.
5. WHEN a Diagnostic or Decision_Trace includes Source_Document text, THE CLI SHALL replace each Unicode control character (general category Cc, which covers U+0000 to U+001F, U+007F, and U+0080 to U+009F, and includes ESC U+001B, NUL U+0000, DEL U+007F, tab U+0009, and carriage return U+000D) in the rendered output with a visible escaped form that contains only printable ASCII characters and identifies the code point in hexadecimal. The only exception is the line feed (U+000A) that terminates an output line. A line feed inside the included text is also replaced.
6. IF no size limit or nesting depth limit is configured, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL apply a default limit for each, each default being a positive integer fixed in the design document (UD-9), so that criteria 3 and 4 are always enforced; the effective limit is the configured limit or, if none is configured, the default limit.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL declare `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` in every crate, except a crate that has a written justification and a recorded review for each unsafe block. A source scan that finds a crate without the declaration and without a recorded justification and review for each unsafe block SHALL fail the build.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL apply criteria 1 to 6 to every AI_Configuration file, Approval_Ledger file, Proposal_Store file, Mock_Provider script, and Provider_Response, and WHEN the Review_Group renders text that a Provider_Response supplied (including a quoted text, a rationale, or a proposer identity), THE Review_Group SHALL replace each Unicode control character as criterion 5 requires.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL accept an input file whose size in bytes equals the effective size limit, and SHALL accept a Rule_IR document whose nesting depth equals the effective nesting depth limit, so that criteria 3 and 4 reject only values strictly greater than the limit.
10. WHEN the CLI renders Source_Document text under criterion 5 or criterion 8, THE CLI SHALL produce output in which every Unicode control character other than the terminating line feed of each output line is absent, so that a byte scan of the output for Cc code points finds only those line feeds.
11. WHEN RuleTrace processes a Source_Document, Rule_IR, or Competition_Data file that contains text resembling a script, a shell command, or an external program or library reference, THE RuleTrace SHALL produce the same Diagnostics, Decision_Trace, and output bytes as it produces for the same file with that text replaced by inert text of the same length, apart from the text itself, and SHALL start no process and open no network connection.

### Requirement 20: Quality, Testing, and Regression Control

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want comprehensive automated tests, so that the compiler's guarantees stay true as it evolves.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL include at least one passing unit test for each of these stages: ingestion, Rule_IR parsing, validation, compilation, execution, and explanation, where each stage has at least one test for a valid input that asserts the exact expected output value of the stage, and at least one test for an invalid input that asserts the exact Diagnostic code and Severity that the stage reports.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL include property-based tests for these invariants: Rule_IR serialize-then-parse round-trip equality, Source_Span text reproduction, deterministic execution, input-order independence, Exact_Number arithmetic laws (commutativity and associativity of addition, and checked overflow reported as a Diagnostic), ranking position completeness (every participant receives exactly one of a Rank_Position held alone, a Rank_Position shared in exactly one Tie_Group, or an Indeterminate position, and no arbitrary order is imposed on tied participants), tie-group completeness (Tie_Groups are disjoint and contain every participant that is equal on the ranking key and every declared tie-breaking criterion), Diagnostic canonical ordering (Requirement 7 criterion 10 is a total order and is independent of the order in which Diagnostics are produced), and Decision_Trace replay, where each property test runs at least 256 generated cases per test run, and a property test that generates fewer than 256 cases fails.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL include at least one negative test for each of these Rulebook categories: invalid, ambiguous, conflicting, and unsupported, where each test asserts the exact Diagnostic code, the Diagnostic Severity, and the primary Location (for a Source_Span, the Source_Document identifier and the start and end offsets).
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL include at least one regression Fixture, where each Fixture contains a Source_Document, a Rulebook, an Approval_Ledger that approves every Effective_Revision, input data, an expected Outcome file, and an expected Decision_Trace file, and the test SHALL fail on any byte difference between the produced and expected Outcome or Decision_Trace, including a difference in only one byte, in line endings, or in trailing whitespace.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that runs the Explainer over every Decision_Trace produced from every Fixture, with the Source_Models of that Fixture loaded in memory before the Explainer is called, and verifies that each referenced Source_Span lies within the bounds of its Source_Document (start offset less than or equal to end offset, and end offset less than or equal to the document length), resolves to non-empty source text, and has a document hash equal to the hash in the Compiled_Rulebook.
6. THE RuleTrace SHALL include an isolation test that executes two different Rulebooks in the same process in the orders A then B, B then A, and interleaved per input record, and asserts that the Outcomes and Decision_Traces of each Rulebook are byte-identical across all three orders and identical to the results from executing that Rulebook alone.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that fails with an error identifying the offending crate and type if a floating-point type (`f32` or `f64`) appears in the source of the Rule_IR, Compiler, or Rules_Engine crates.
8. WHEN a defect is fixed, THE RuleTrace SHALL include, in the same change, a regression test that fails when run against the code without the fix and passes with the fix.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that fails with an error identifying the offending crate and dependency name if the dependency list of any Core_Crate includes an AI provider library or a network client library.
10. IF any test described in this Requirement fails, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL report a non-zero exit status from the test command and SHALL print the fully qualified name of each failing test to the test command output.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that fails with an error identifying the offending crate and item if the source or dependency list of the Rules_Engine crate contains a file, network, clock, random, or environment access, or a dependency on the Explainer crate.
12. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a negative test for each Conflict class of Requirement 6 criteria 16 to 19 and a positive test for criterion 20 that asserts the exact Diagnostic code, the Conflict class, the identifiers of the Rules, and the primary and related Locations, and SHALL include a test of Requirement 12 criterion 10 for a Precedence_Declaration whose first-listed Rule is Indeterminate.
13. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that verifies every Location variant: a Diagnostic with a Document_Location, an Input_Location, a Cli_Location, and a No_Location with a reason code is constructed, rendered, and ordered by Requirement 7 criteria 10 and 13, and a test that fails when any Diagnostic carries a Source_Span that does not exist in a supplied Source_Model.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test of the Diagnostic_Catalogue baseline of Requirement 7 criteria 11 and 12, including a test that the missing baseline file fails the test for every release after the first.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a property test that mutates exactly one part of the Rule_Content of a Rule_Revision (including one Source_Span offset, a document hash, an operand, a rounding declaration, and the Revision_Number) and asserts that the Rule_Content_Hash changes and the Approval_Record becomes non-matching, a property test that changes only the Origin, an Approval_Record, or the Approval_Status and asserts that the Rule_Content_Hash stays equal, and tests of Effective_Revision selection, superseded exclusion, the Warning APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE, the derivation of the Approval_Status from the Approval_Ledger, and the absence of Proposed, Rejected, and superseded Rule_Revisions from every Compiled_Rulebook (Requirements 25 and 26), each with at least 256 generated cases where the test is a property test.
16. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that builds the Core_Crates and the ruletrace executable with every Auxiliary_Crate excluded from the workspace, runs the vertical slice, and asserts that the output bytes equal those of the build that includes the Auxiliary_Crates, and a test that the ruletrace output is byte-identical when an AI_Configuration file and a credential environment variable are present and when they are absent.
17. THE RuleTrace SHALL include, for each rule of Requirement 24 criterion 5, a negative test on a synthetic dependency graph that fails the dependency-graph check with a message that names the offending crates, and a test that fails with an error naming the crate and item when a scan under Requirement 24 criterion 3 finds a prohibited item.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test for each Explainer failure of Requirement 13 criterion 16 that asserts the Diagnostic code, the Severity, the primary Location, the not verified marking, the absence of rendered source text for the span, and the CLI exit code 2 with the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces written, and a test for the Warning PROV_NOT_VERIFIED_NO_SOURCES with exit code 0.
19. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of the Requirement 22 criteria 16 and 17 for every input kind that they name, asserting the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity Error, the names of the contradictory fields, and the Location, and asserting that the input is rejected as invalid input and that no has_errors, no_errors, validation status, or lifecycle flag supplied by the input is used.
20. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test with a Source_Document that contains text that instructs an AI to approve rules, to skip validation, or to reveal credentials, and a Mock_Provider response that follows the instruction, and SHALL assert that no Rule_Revision becomes Approved, that the Provider_Response is rejected under Requirement 23 criterion 5, that the Approval_Ledger and the Proposal_Store are unchanged except as Requirement 23 permits, and that no credential value appears in any output.
21. THE RuleTrace SHALL include the tests of Requirement 23 criteria 26, 27, and 31 in the tests that the required continuous integration jobs run, and SHALL run them with no network access beyond the loopback address and no credential.
22. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a differential property test that compares the Exact_Number addition, subtraction, multiplication, division with each rounding mode, comparison, aggregation, and serialization with an independent reference model that is written only in test code and that uses arbitrary-precision integers, with at least 256 generated cases per operation and with generated cases at and beyond the Exact_Number_Limits of the selected UD-3 option, and, while UD-3 is pending, SHALL run the same cases against both Option D and Option R.
23. THE RuleTrace SHALL include in the required continuous integration jobs the tests of Requirement 26 criterion 23, Requirement 27 criterion 10, Requirement 28 criterion 16, Requirement 29 criterion 14, and Requirement 30 criterion 8, and SHALL run them with no network access beyond the loopback address and no credential.
24. IF a property test described in this Requirement fails, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL print the random seed and the minimal failing generated case to the test command output, so that rerunning with that seed reproduces the failure.
25. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test for the Diagnostic_State of Requirement 22 that asserts has_errors and no_errors are both derived from the single Diagnostic list, that they are never both true and never both false for any generated Diagnostic list, and that an input that supplies both as independent values is rejected as invalid input with the Diagnostic codes DIAGNOSTIC_STATE_CONTRADICTION or DIAGNOSTIC_STATE_BOTH_FLAGS.

26. THE RuleTrace SHALL treat the demonstration acceptance test of Requirement 33 criterion 15 as a first-class release criterion that is required in the continuous integration jobs and required before a release is tagged, and SHALL keep every other required test of this Requirement and of Requirements 10, 13, 24, 26, 27, and 22 (determinism, isolation, provenance, approval gating, diagnostic state, no network in the core, exact numeric) required and unchanged. The number of property tests is not itself a release criterion, and no criterion of this document is removed or weakened by this criterion.
27. THE RuleTrace SHALL run the tests of Requirement 31 criteria 5 and 10 to 12 and the demonstration acceptance test of Requirement 33 criterion 15 with no network access beyond the loopback address and no credential, and SHALL run the live-provider step of Requirement 33 criterion 10 only in a separate job that is not required for a change to pass, as Requirement 21 criterion 14 requires for live tests.

### Requirement 21: Build, Formatting, Linting, and Continuous Integration

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want automated quality gates, so that every change is checked the same way.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL be implemented in Rust (stable channel, edition 2021 or later) as a single Cargo workspace in which each crate has a written statement of its purpose and the crates it may depend on, and in which the crate dependency graph contains no cycles. A test SHALL fail when the Cargo workspace metadata contains a dependency cycle, when a crate has no such written statement, or when a crate depends on a crate its statement does not list.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL pin the exact Rust toolchain version (a full major.minor.patch version, not a channel name) in a toolchain file at the repository root, and every continuous integration job SHALL build with that pinned version. A continuous integration job SHALL fail when the version reported by `rustc --version` differs from the pinned version.
3. WHEN a change is proposed, THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL run all of the following on every platform of the matrix in criterion 10: the formatting check (`cargo fmt --check`), the lint check (`cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`), and the full test suite (`cargo test --all-features`). The pipeline SHALL record a separate pass or fail result for each check on each platform.
4. IF any continuous integration check exits with a non-zero status, THEN THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL mark the change as failing, SHALL retain the complete standard output and standard error of the failed check as an artifact attached to that run, and SHALL NOT mark the change as passing.
5. WHEN a change adds or updates a third-party dependency, THE RuleTrace SHALL include a written justification for that dependency, in the commit message or the pull request description of that change, stating its purpose, the alternatives considered (including the standard library), and its maintenance status.
6. IF a change adds a third-party dependency that has no written justification, THEN THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL mark the change as failing. A dependency has no written justification when the justification of criterion 5 is missing or lacks any of the three stated items (purpose, alternatives considered including the standard library, maintenance status).
7. WHERE at least one third-party dependency exists, THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL run a dependency check (`cargo deny` or `cargo audit`) on every run and SHALL mark the change as failing if the check reports a violation or exits with a non-zero status.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL contain no microservice, database, or background service, and no crate SHALL start a long-running process or open a network listener, except that test code of an AI_Integration_Crate MAY bind a listener to the loopback address for the duration of one test, as Requirement 23 criterion 27 requires. This exception supersedes the earlier absolute statement for that test code only. A source-scan test SHALL fail when any other crate contains a network listener or a call that starts a background process.
9. WHEN built and tested on a clean checkout, THE RuleTrace SHALL complete the build and the full test suite successfully with network access used only to fetch dependencies pinned in the lockfile, and with no API key or AI service required. The build and the test suite SHALL succeed with no API key environment variable set.
10. THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL run on exactly this platform matrix: Linux on x86_64, macOS on arm64, and Windows on x86_64, and SHALL pin the exact runner image version of each platform in the design document (UD-15), and SHALL mark the change as failing when any platform fails, is skipped, or is cancelled before it reports a result.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL store Fixture and golden files so that no checkout setting converts line endings or encoding, and SHALL compare golden files byte-for-byte on every platform of the matrix. A test SHALL fail when a Fixture or golden file differs from its stored bytes after checkout on any platform of the matrix.
12. WHEN the continuous integration pipeline runs the determinism tests on each platform of the matrix, THE RuleTrace SHALL compare the serialized Outcomes, Decision_Traces, Compiled_Rulebooks, and Diagnostics of that platform with the stored golden files and SHALL fail on any byte difference. The failure output SHALL name the golden file and the platform.
13. THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL include a required job that builds and tests the Core_Crates and the ruletrace executable with every Auxiliary_Crate excluded from the workspace, that inspects the resolved dependency tree of each Core_Crate and of the ruletrace binary, and that fails when a tree contains a network client library, an AI provider library, or an AI_Crate. The failure output SHALL name the offending crate and the Core_Crate or binary whose tree contains it.
14. THE continuous integration pipeline SHALL run the tests of the Auxiliary_Crates in the required jobs with the Mock_Provider and the loopback stub only, and SHALL run a live test of an AI_Integration_Crate only in a separate job that is not required for a change to pass. A failure of that separate job SHALL NOT change the pass or fail status of the change.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL apply criteria 5, 6, and 7 to the dependencies of every Auxiliary_Crate, and each AI_Integration_Crate dependency SHALL have a written justification that names the alternatives considered, including the standard library.

### Requirement 22: Canonical Diagnostic State

**User Story:** As an auditor, I want a single source of truth for whether a result has errors, so that contradictory states such as no_errors = true together with has_errors = true can never be accepted.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL define the Diagnostic_State as the ordered list of Diagnostics, each with a Severity, in the order in which the producing stage emitted them, and SHALL treat that list as the only canonical diagnostic state.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive has_errors as true when the Diagnostic_State contains at least one Diagnostic with Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, and as false otherwise. This blocking set includes every APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES, APPROVAL_REJECTED_REVISION_MODIFIED, and APPROVAL_RECHECK_FAILED Diagnostic, because each has Severity Error.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive no_errors as true when the Diagnostic_State contains no Diagnostic with Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported, and as false otherwise.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive the validation result as passed when no_errors is true, and as failed when has_errors is true.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL compute has_errors, no_errors, and the validation result as functions of the Diagnostic_State only, so that two Diagnostic_States with equal Diagnostic lists yield equal values regardless of any other field of the result.
6. THE RuleTrace SHALL store none of has_errors, no_errors, or the validation result as an independent field in any type, serialized form, or Compiled_Rulebook metadata.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL deserialize none of has_errors, no_errors, or the validation result as an independent field.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL define the types of the validation result so that no value with has_errors and no_errors both true, or both false, can be constructed, as verified by an automated test of the public API of the validation result type.
9. IF a serialized validation result, Compiled_Rulebook metadata, CLI input, library input, Proposal, or Fixture supplies has_errors, no_errors, or a validation status that disagrees with the value derived from its Diagnostic list, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject that input as invalid input and SHALL return exactly one Error Diagnostic for that input that names each contradictory field.
10. IF an input supplies both has_errors and no_errors, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject that input as invalid input and SHALL return exactly one Error Diagnostic for that input that names both fields.
11. IF an input is rejected under criterion 9 or 10, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL run no later pipeline stage on that input and SHALL produce no Validation_Result and no Compiled_Rulebook from it.
12. IF an input is rejected under criterion 9 or 10, THEN THE Error Diagnostic SHALL carry the dedicated code DIAGNOSTIC_STATE_CONTRADICTION (criterion 9) or DIAGNOSTIC_STATE_BOTH_FLAGS (criterion 10) from the Diagnostic_Catalogue and a primary Location that is the Input_Location of the first offending field in input order, or the Cli_Location of the offending argument, or a No_Location with a reason code when the input has no position, and SHALL list the Locations of any further offending fields as related Locations in input order.
13. IF the CLI rejects an input under criterion 9, 10, or 17, THEN THE CLI SHALL exit with code 2.
14. FOR ALL Diagnostic_States, including the empty list and lists that contain only Warning Diagnostics, has_errors SHALL equal the negation of no_errors, and both SHALL equal the values defined by criteria 2 and 3 for that list (property-based test of at least 256 generated cases).
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a negative test for each input kind named in criterion 9 that asserts the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity Error, the names of the contradictory fields, and the Location, and a negative test for criterion 10 that asserts the same, and a CLI test that asserts exit code 2 and that no later stage ran.
16. IF a Proposal, a Proposal_Store entry, a Review_Decision, an Approval_Ledger, or an output of ruletrace-ai or of the Review_Group supplies has_errors, no_errors, or a validation status, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject that input under criteria 9 and 10, and IF a Provider_Response supplies one of these fields, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the whole Provider_Response with the Diagnostic AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD and exit code 6, and in every case THE Validator SHALL derive every validation status of a Proposal from the Diagnostic_State that the Validator itself produced and SHALL NOT trust a validation status that an AI_Provider asserts.
17. IF a Rulebook, a Proposal_Store entry, an Approval_Ledger, or a Review_Decision supplies an independent lifecycle flag (any of the fields is_approved, is_rejected, or is_proposed) or supplies a stored Approval_Status, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject that input as invalid input with exactly one Error Diagnostic with the code LIFECYCLE_FLAG_CONTRADICTION that names each offending field, and SHALL run no later stage on that input, so that the Approval_Status, which is derived from the Approval_Ledger, is the only canonical lifecycle field.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive the presence of an Unresolved_Approval_State from the Diagnostic_State as the presence of a Diagnostic with the code APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, and SHALL store that presence in no independent field.
19. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep Warning Diagnostics out of has_errors, no_errors, and the validation result, so that adding or removing a Warning_Diagnostic never changes a derived flag, compilability, or an exit code.
20. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a property-based test of at least 256 generated cases that adds and removes Warning Diagnostics in an arbitrary Diagnostic_State and asserts that has_errors, no_errors, the validation result, compilability, and the exit code are unchanged (criterion 19).
21. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a negative test for criterion 17 for each input kind named there that asserts the code LIFECYCLE_FLAG_CONTRADICTION, the Severity Error, the names of the offending fields, the Location, that no later stage ran, and, for the CLI, exit code 2.

22. IF an Application_Input_Format file, a Published_Result file, a Recorded_Provider_Script, or a Demo_Corpus manifest supplies has_errors, no_errors, a validation status, or a lifecycle flag of criterion 17, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject that input under criteria 9, 10, and 17 with the same codes and exit code 2, whether or not the value agrees with the derived value, and THE Application_Layers and THE Proof_Report SHALL derive every validity statement from the Diagnostic_State of the underlying result only and SHALL write no independent validity field.

### Requirement 23: AI-Assisted Proposal Pipeline

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want an optional AI-assisted pipeline that proposes structured rules from clauses of my rulebook, so that I can review and approve proposals instead of writing every rule by hand, without trusting the AI.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE AI_Tool SHALL perform the proposal pipeline as these ordered steps, each with the typed inputs and outputs of Requirement 24: step 1 checks the AI_Configuration, the credential, and the Transmission_Consent (Requirement 28); step 2 builds the Clause_Context and the Provider_Request and writes the Transmission_Log entry; step 3 invokes the AI_Adapter under the call policy of Requirement 29; step 4 parses the Provider_Response; step 5 validates the parsed Provider_Response against the Proposal_Schema; step 6 verifies the provenance of every Source_Span of every Proposal; step 7 validates every structured rule suggestion with the Validator, which yields a Semantic_Outcome for each; step 8 writes the batch to the Proposal_Store atomically. THE review command group of ruletrace SHALL perform the human review of criteria 12 to 16 after step 8. THE AI_Tool SHALL run no step after a failed step 1 to 6 for that Provider_Response, SHALL report the Semantic_Outcome Diagnostics of every Proposal for step 7 before the run ends, and SHALL exit, after a failed step, with the exit code that the table of Requirement 16 criterion 17 assigns to the earliest failed step.
2. WHEN the AI_Tool builds a Provider_Request, THE AI_Tool SHALL include in the Clause_Context, for each supplied Candidate_Clause, the document identifier, the document SHA-256 content hash, the start and end byte offsets, the 1-based start line and column, and the exact clause text, SHALL include the type declarations of the Rulebook and the vocabulary of the First_Version_Support_Boundary, and SHALL include no other Source_Document text.
3. WHEN the AI_Tool builds a Provider_Request twice from identical Candidate_Clauses, Source_Models, Rulebook type declarations, and AI_Configuration, THE AI_Tool SHALL produce byte-identical Provider_Requests, and the Request_Identifier of Requirement 29 criterion 7 SHALL be identical for both.
4. THE Proposal_Schema SHALL be a versioned, typed data schema that has exactly the five Proposal kinds of Requirement 18 criterion 2, that expresses a structured rule suggestion in the Rule_IR form defined by Requirements 3 and 14, and that has no field that carries a script, an expression of a general-purpose language, a shell command, an external reference, an Approval_Status other than Proposed, an Approval_Record, a Reviewer, a validation status, has_errors, or no_errors.
5. IF a Provider_Response is not valid UTF-8, is not parseable in the data format of the Proposal_Schema, exceeds the effective response size limit, reports a refusal or an incomplete status, contains a duplicate key, an unknown field, a missing required field, or a field of the wrong type, exceeds the effective nesting depth limit, or contains a field that Proposal_Schema criterion 4 excludes as an Approval_Status, an Approval_Record, a Reviewer, a validation status, has_errors, or no_errors (the names listed in the design, section 3.7.4), THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the whole Provider_Response with exactly one Diagnostic, chosen as the first applicable of this order: AI_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE (size limit), AI_RESPONSE_MALFORMED (not parseable or not valid UTF-8), AI_RESPONSE_INCOMPLETE (refusal or incomplete status), AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD (one of the excluded names above), or AI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA_VIOLATION (every other case). A field that would carry a script, an expression of a general-purpose language, a shell command, or an external reference is not defined by the closed Proposal_Schema at any nesting level, so it is an unknown field and is AI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA_VIOLATION, never AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD. The Diagnostic SHALL have Severity Error and a primary Location that is an Input_Location with the response identifier and the field name or the byte offset of the first violation. THE AI_Tool SHALL store nothing from that Provider_Response, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store, the Rulebook, and the Approval_Ledger byte-identical, and SHALL exit with code 6. The local parser is authoritative (design section 3.7.3). It accepts a rule draft with no `markers` member and reads it as an empty list, while the strict schema that is sent to a provider lists `markers` as required. The two differ on purpose: the schema asks a provider for the member, and a response without it is not rejected.
6. THE AI_Tool SHALL reject a Proposal whose kind, proposer identity (1 to 200 characters), or Source_Span list violates Requirement 18 criterion 4, under criterion 5 of this Requirement, so that the Diagnostic, the Severity, the exit code 6, and the unchanged state are those of criterion 5.
7. WHEN the AI_Tool verifies the provenance of a Source_Span of a Proposal, THE AI_Tool SHALL check against the supplied Source_Models that: the document identifier exists in the supplied Source_Models, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_UNRESOLVED; the document hash stated in the Proposal equals the recorded hash of that Source_Model, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_HASH_MISMATCH; the span has a start less than its end, lies within the document, and starts and ends on UTF-8 scalar boundaries, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_UNRESOLVED; the line and column stated in the Proposal equal the line and column that the Source_Model gives for the start of the span, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_UNRESOLVED; the span lies within the span of at least one Candidate_Clause that the Provider_Request supplied, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_OUTSIDE_CONTEXT; and the quoted text of the Proposal equals the bytes of the span in the Source_Model byte for byte, otherwise AI_PROPOSAL_QUOTE_MISMATCH. THE AI_Tool SHALL apply these checks in the listed order and SHALL return for each Source_Span only the Diagnostic of the first failed check. Each Diagnostic SHALL have Severity Error and an Input_Location of the offending field as its primary Location, and THE AI_Tool SHALL exit with code 3 when only provenance checks fail.
8. THE AI_Tool SHALL NOT correct, trim, normalize, re-anchor, search for, or otherwise repair a Source_Span or a quoted text of a Proposal, and SHALL NOT construct any Source_Span that the Provider_Response did not supply and that the check of criterion 7 did not verify.
9. WHEN the AI_Tool converts a structured rule suggestion into a Rule_Revision held in the Proposal_Store, THE AI_Tool SHALL give it Origin AI_Proposal and the next Revision_Number, SHALL leave its derived Approval_Status Proposed because no Ledger_Record exists, and SHALL validate it with the Validator, the same component and the same checks that validate a manually authored Rule_Revision, so that identical Rule_Content yields identical Diagnostic codes, Severities, and Locations whatever the Origin.
10. IF any Proposal of a Provider_Response fails a check of criteria 5 to 8, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL store no Proposal and no Rule_Revision from that Provider_Response, SHALL return the Diagnostics of every failing Proposal in ascending order of the zero-based Proposal index, SHALL exit with code 6 if any schema-level check of criteria 5 and 6 failed and otherwise with code 3 (the earliest failed step decides, Requirement 16 criterion 17), and SHALL leave the Proposal_Store and the Rulebook byte-identical to their state before the write step of that Provider_Response. A Semantic_Outcome of criterion 9 is not a failure of this criterion (Requirement 30 criterion 2). WHEN every check of criteria 5 to 8 passes for every Proposal, THE AI_Tool SHALL write all Proposals to the Proposal_Store in one atomic write.
11. WHEN the AI_Tool stores a Proposal, THE AI_Tool SHALL record in the Proposal_Store the proposer identity, the AI_Provider identifier, the model identifier, the AI identities that the AI_Configuration lists, the AI_Adapter version, the Request_Identifier, the SHA-256 hash of the Provider_Response bytes, the Proposal identifier of Requirement 29 criterion 9, and a creation time that the caller supplied, SHALL read no clock for that time, and SHALL record all of these values as Origin metadata outside the Rule_Content.
12. WHEN a Reviewer requests review list, THE Review_Group SHALL list the Proposals and the Rule_Revisions in an Unresolved_Approval_State in the Proposal_Store and the Rulebook, and SHALL show for the selected Rule_Revision its Origin, its Rule_Content_Hash, its Diagnostics, and, for every Source_Span, the source text that the Review_Group resolved from a supplied Source_Model through the Explainer. The Review_Group SHALL render the Origin of an AI_Proposal revision as `origin: AI proposal '<proposal id>' (proposer claimed by the model: '<proposer>', provider '<provider>', model '<model>')`, on every platform, with each Unicode control character of the claimed proposer replaced as criterion 8 of Requirement 19 requires. The proposer is text that the Provider_Response supplied and is a claim of the model, not an identity that RuleTrace verified, whereas the provider and the model are the identifiers that the AI_Tool took from the AI_Configuration. The claimed proposer is nevertheless a member of the set that criterion 15 refuses as a Reviewer identity. The Origin of a Manual revision is rendered as before.
13. WHEN a Reviewer requests approval of a Rule_Revision, THE Review_Group SHALL require the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Reviewer identity, a caller-supplied time, the Rule_Content_Hash that the Reviewer saw, and the Source_Models of every document of the Approval_Binding, SHALL approve exactly one Rule_Revision per invocation, SHALL provide no option that approves several Rule_Revisions or all Rule_Revisions, SHALL perform the operation only under Requirement 5 criterion 9, and, for a Rule_Revision that exists only in the Proposal_Store, SHALL adopt it into the Rulebook and append the approval Ledger_Record in one all-or-nothing operation (Requirement 26 criterion 19).
14. WHEN a Reviewer requests rejection of a Rule_Revision, THE Review_Group SHALL require the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Reviewer identity, a caller-supplied time, and a reason text of 1 to 2000 characters, SHALL append a rejection Ledger_Record that records the Reviewer, the Rule_Content_Hash, and the reason, SHALL change the derived Approval_Status from Proposed to Rejected, and SHALL leave Rejected as a terminal state of that Rule_Revision.
15. IF a Review_Decision names a Reviewer identity that is empty, is longer than 200 characters, or equals (compared as exact Unicode scalar values) the proposer identity, the AI_Provider identifier, the model identifier, or any AI identity that the Origin metadata of the Rule_Revision or of the Proposal_Store entry records, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL refuse the Review_Decision with the Diagnostic REVIEW_SELF_APPROVAL (when the identity equals a proposer, provider, model, or recorded AI identity) or REVIEW_REVIEWER_INVALID (when the identity is empty or too long), each with Severity Error and exit code 3, SHALL leave the Rule_Revision, the Approval_Ledger, the Proposal_Store, and the Rulebook byte-identical, and SHALL append no Ledger_Record. The type of a Reviewer SHALL have no AI variant, and no AI_Crate SHALL be able to call the function that creates a Ledger_Record.
16. WHEN a Reviewer wants a revision of a Rule_Revision, THE Reviewer SHALL create a new Rule_Revision under Requirement 25 criterion 10 by editing the Rulebook with Origin Manual, because the Review_Group has no revise subcommand, and WHERE AI is enabled, THE AI_Tool SHALL accept a request to propose a revision of a named Rule_Revision with Reviewer feedback text as additional context, and SHALL pass the resulting Proposal through criteria 1 to 11 as a new Rule_Revision with Origin AI_Proposal. The feedback text SHALL be a non-empty UTF-8 file of at most 8,192 bytes (the effective size limit of this input under Requirement 19 criterion 3, which accepts a file of exactly that size under criterion 9), and a larger file SHALL be refused with LIMIT_FILE_TOO_LARGE (RT-LIM-0001) that names the file, the limit, and the file size reported by the file system. IF a rule suggestion of a Provider_Response of such a request names a Rule identifier other than the one named by the request, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the whole Provider_Response with AI_REVISION_TARGET_MISMATCH (RT-AI-0207), exit code 6, and an unchanged Proposal_Store. This check is a protocol check of the response schema stage: it runs after the checks of criterion 5, so a response with a schema violation is AI_RESPONSE_SCHEMA_VIOLATION, and before the provenance check of criterion 7 and the credential check of Requirement 28 criterion 9.
17. IF an AI_Provider returns a Provider_Failure, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return the Diagnostic defined in the following table with a primary Location that is the Input_Location of the provider field of the AI_Configuration, and the number of attempts made (1 to 3) in the message, and SHALL exit with the code in the table. Each failed attempt that Requirement 29 retries yields the Warning of the per-attempt column, and the final Error follows Requirement 29 criteria 5 and 6. The Warnings leave the exit code unchanged, and a run that ends after a retried attempt with a valid Provider_Response exits with code 0 (success after retry).

| Provider_Failure | Per-attempt Warning | Final Error | Exit code | Retried (Requirement 29) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Unavailable (connection refused, connection reset, name resolution failure, HTTP 5xx) | AI_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE | AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED or AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED | 5 | Yes |
| Timeout | AI_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT | AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED or AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED | 5 | Yes |
| Rate-limited (HTTP 429) | AI_PROVIDER_RATE_LIMITED | AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED or AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED | 5 | Yes |
| Authentication failed | none | AI_PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED | 5 | No |
| TLS or certificate failure | none | AI_PROVIDER_TLS_FAILED | 5 | No |
| Other provider error (HTTP 4xx other than 429, other) | none | AI_PROVIDER_ERROR | 5 | No |

18. WHEN a call to an AI_Provider has no response within the effective per-attempt timeout (default 20 seconds, Requirement 29 criterion 1), THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL end the call and return a timeout Provider_Failure, and THE AI_Tool SHALL apply the deadline, the retry policy, and the idempotency rule of Requirement 29 to every Provider_Failure. This criterion supersedes the earlier statement that default timeout and retry values are fixed in the design (UD-19).
19. IF the AI_Tool is run while AI is disabled, with an AI_Configuration that is missing a required field or contains an unknown field, with a provider identifier that no available AI_Provider implements, without the credential that the AI_Configuration names, or without a Transmission_Consent that names exactly the documents to send, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_DISABLED, AI_CONFIG_INVALID, AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN, AI_CREDENTIAL_MISSING, or AI_CONSENT_MISSING respectively (and the other codes of Requirement 28 for their cases), each with Severity Error and a primary Location that is the Cli_Location of the argument or the Input_Location of the AI_Configuration field, SHALL make no network call, SHALL send no document text, SHALL append no Transmission_Log entry, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store, the Rulebook, and the Approval_Ledger byte-identical, and SHALL exit with code 2.
20. IF any step of the proposal pipeline fails, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL write no partial Proposal, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store, every Rulebook, every Compiled_Rulebook, and every Approval_Record byte-identical to their state before the failed Provider_Response was handled (criterion 35 defines this state for a run with several Provider_Requests, as the failed-run store state), and SHALL NOT alter the behavior or the exit code of any later run of the CLI. A Transmission_Log entry that was appended before the failure SHALL remain, and no Transmission_Log entry SHALL be removed or changed.
21. THE AI_Tool SHALL enable AI only WHEN the user invokes ruletrace-ai explicitly and supplies an AI_Configuration file explicitly, by naming it in a command argument, and SHALL read no AI_Configuration from a default path, from the working directory, or from the environment, and the AI_Configuration SHALL contain the contents that Requirement 28 criterion 2 lists.
22. THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL read a credential only from the environment variable whose name the AI_Configuration gives, and THE RuleTrace SHALL contain no credential value in source code, in a Fixture, in an AI_Configuration file, in the Transmission_Log, or in the Proposal_Store, and the AI_Configuration schema SHALL have no field that holds a credential value (Requirement 28 criterion 4).
23. THE RuleTrace SHALL replace every occurrence of a credential value with the text [REDACTED] in every Diagnostic, log line, standard output, standard error, Proposal_Store entry, and Origin metadata, including text that a provider error message or response echoes, as verified by a test that supplies a canary credential value and asserts that the value appears in no output, for every failure mode of criteria 17, 19, and 33.
24. WHEN the AI_Tool is about to send a Provider_Request, THE AI_Tool SHALL write to standard error the AI_Provider identifier, the endpoint host, the document identifiers, the number of Candidate_Clauses, and the byte size of the Provider_Request, and SHALL send the Provider_Request only WHILE the AI_Configuration permits transmission of Source_Document text and the Transmission_Consent of the invocation names every document sent.
25. THE Mock_Provider SHALL return, for a Provider_Request, the Provider_Response bytes or the Provider_Failure that a scripted file names, SHALL use no network, clock, random source, or environment, and SHALL return identical results for identical scripts and Provider_Requests. No raw Provider_Response is kept by the RuleTrace except the accepted response payload that the Proposal_Store retains under Requirement 30 criterion 1, and a credential, an authorization header, or other transport data that is not part of that payload is never kept (UD-36).
26. THE RuleTrace SHALL include Mock_Provider scripts and tests for each of these cases, each asserting the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity, the primary Location, the exit code, and the unchanged Proposal_Store (the failed-run store state of criterion 35), Rulebook, and Approval_Ledger: a valid response; each Provider_Failure of criterion 17; each failure of criteria 5 to 8; each failure mode of criteria 33 and 34; a response that claims an Approval_Status, an Approval_Record, or a Reviewer; a response that supplies has_errors or no_errors; a response that contains an instruction to approve a rule; a Reviewer that equals the proposer identity; a Semantic_Outcome with an Ambiguity, an Unsupported, and an Error Diagnostic that is stored and not executable; and a successful run from Proposal through ruletrace review approve, validate, compile, and evaluate.
27. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of the real AI_Integration_Crate against a stub server that listens only on the loopback address for the duration of the test and that simulates a success, an unavailable server, a timeout, a rate-limit response, an authentication failure, an oversize response, and a malformed response, and the required continuous integration jobs SHALL run these tests without any external network access and without any credential.
28. WHERE a credential for the real AI_Provider is present in the environment of a separate, non-required continuous integration job, THE RuleTrace SHALL run a live test of the AI_Integration_Crate, and a failure of that job SHALL NOT change the result of the required jobs.
29. THE RuleTrace SHALL contain at least one AI_Integration_Crate that implements the AI_Adapter for the OpenAI Responses protocol, using the provider's strict structured JSON Schema output mode, in a crate separate from the Core_Crates and from the Mock_Provider crate (UD-17, amended by UD-37). That one crate, `rt-ai-responses`, serves the Provider_Profiles of the Meta Model API (the first real provider) and of OpenAI, so that no crate exists per provider. The Provider_Response remains untrusted and passes criteria 4 to 11 in full, and the provider identifier, the protocol, and the model are replaceable by AI_Configuration values without a code change (Requirement 28 criterion 12).
30. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep network client libraries, credential access, and AI provider libraries only in AI_Integration_Crates, and SHALL keep the Core_Crates, the ruletrace executable, and the Mock_Provider crate free of them, as verified by the dependency-graph check of Requirement 24 criterion 5 and by the dependency-tree test of Requirement 27 criterion 5.
31. WHEN identical Provider_Response bytes are supplied through the Mock_Provider and through the real AI_Integration_Crate against the stub server, THE AI_Tool SHALL produce identical Proposals and identical Diagnostics from steps 4 to 8, so that these steps do not depend on the AI_Provider.
32. WHEN the effective maximum number of Candidate_Clauses per Provider_Request (UD-25) would be exceeded, THE AI_Tool SHALL split the Candidate_Clauses into several Provider_Requests in ascending start offset order, SHALL give each Provider_Request its own Request_Identifier, and SHALL handle each Provider_Response under criteria 1 to 11 and 17 to 20 and under Requirements 29 and 30 independently, and SHALL write the Proposals of a Provider_Response only after that Provider_Response passes all checks of criteria 5 to 8.
33. IF a failure of the following table occurs, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return exactly the listed Diagnostic with the listed Severity, primary Location, and exit code, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store (in the failed-run store state of criterion 35), the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, and every Compiled_Rulebook byte-identical, and SHALL act as the last column states. The failures of criteria 5, 7, 17, and 19 follow their own criteria.

| Step (Requirement 16 criterion 17) | Failure | Diagnostic | Severity | Primary Location | Exit code | Further effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 4 | Candidate_Clauses, Source_Models, Rulebook type declarations, or the caller-supplied creation time are missing or invalid, a revision request names an unknown Rule identifier or Revision_Number, or the feedback file is empty or not UTF-8 | AI_REQUEST_INVALID | Error | Cli_Location of the argument or Input_Location of the invalid input | 2 | No provider call, no Transmission_Log entry |
| 4 | A built Provider_Request holds the credential value or a run of at least 8 consecutive characters of it, whether it comes from a clause text, the feedback, or the revised rule content | AI_REQUEST_CONTAINS_CREDENTIAL | Error | Input_Location of the request member that holds the match, such as `requests[0].revise.feedback`, never the matched text | 2 | No provider call, no Transmission_Log entry, no Proposal_Store write |
| 4 | The existing Proposal_Store is malformed | AI_STORE_INVALID | Error | Input_Location of the first malformed entry | 2 | No provider call, no Transmission_Log entry |
| 4 | A supplied input carries contradictory validity flags (Requirement 22) | the code of Requirement 22 | Error | Input_Location of the field | 2 | No provider call, no Transmission_Log entry |
| 4 | The Proposal_Store holds a batch with the Request_Identifier (Requirement 29 criterion 10) | AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED | Warning | Cli_Location of the argument | 0 | No provider call, nothing sent |
| 5 | The Transmission_Log entry cannot be appended | AI_TRANSMISSION_LOG_FAILED | Error | Input_Location naming the Transmission_Log path of the AI_Configuration | 70 | Nothing sent |
| 11 | The atomic Proposal_Store write fails | AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED | Error | Input_Location naming the Proposal_Store path | 70 | The store holds no part of the batch, except under the failed-run store state of criterion 35 |

Note on the table of criterion 33: the order of its rows is not a precedence among failures of one Requirement 16 criterion 17 step. Within step 4, the inputs load before the existing Proposal_Store is checked (design §3.7.1), so a failure in an input file (the first and fourth rows) is returned before a malformed Proposal_Store (the third row), whatever the row order. The credential check of the second row runs on the built requests, after the inputs load and before the Proposal_Store is checked. A `--dry-run` reads no credential, so it does not apply the second row.

34. IF a review approve or review reject request fails a check of the following table, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL return exactly the listed Diagnostic with Severity Error and the listed exit code, SHALL write it to standard error, SHALL append no Ledger_Record, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger, the Rulebook, and the Proposal_Store byte-identical.

| Failure | Diagnostic | Exit code |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The Rule identifier and Revision_Number name no Rule_Revision | REVIEW_TARGET_NOT_FOUND | 2 |
| A Source_Model of the Approval_Binding is not supplied | REVIEW_SOURCE_MODEL_MISSING | 2 |
| The rejection reason is empty or longer than 2000 characters | REVIEW_REASON_INVALID | 2 |
| The Reviewer is empty or longer than 200 characters | REVIEW_REVIEWER_INVALID | 3 |
| The Reviewer equals a proposer, provider, model, or recorded AI identity | REVIEW_SELF_APPROVAL | 3 |
| The Rule_Content_Hash that the Reviewer saw differs from the current hash | REVIEW_STALE_HASH | 3 |
| The Rule_Revision already has a decision, or the operation is not permitted by Requirement 5 criterion 8 | REVIEW_INVALID_TRANSITION | 3 |
| The Rule_Revision has a Blocking_Diagnostic other than its own APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED | REVIEW_BLOCKING_DIAGNOSTIC | 3 |
| The atomic write of the Approval_Ledger or the Rulebook fails | REVIEW_WRITE_FAILED | 70 |

35. WHEN a run of the AI_Tool handles several Provider_Requests under criterion 32, THE AI_Tool SHALL process them in ascending order of their first Candidate_Clause start offset, SHALL send no further Provider_Request after the first Provider_Request that fails at a step 1 to 11, SHALL keep every batch that an earlier Provider_Request of the same run wrote, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store byte-identical to its state after the last successful write of the run (or, for a failed directory sync after the hard link, the failed-run store state defined below), and SHALL exit with the code of the failure of the first failed Provider_Request. The exit code is 0 only WHEN every Provider_Request succeeded (including one whose directory sync the platform skipped under criterion 36) or returned AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED. A run that keeps an earlier batch and stops at a failed Provider_Request is an incomplete run under criterion 36, and the byte-identical rules of Requirement 18 criterion 17, Requirement 27 criterion 8, Requirement 29 criterion 17, and Requirement 30 criteria 1 and 11 apply to the state before the failing Provider_Request, not to the state before the run. The failed-run store state is the Proposal_Store as it was before the failing Provider_Request, plus the whole batch file of that Provider_Request only WHEN its directory sync failed after the hard link (AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED, RT-AI-0303, criterion 36). Every other criterion that refers to the failed-run store state uses this definition.
36. WHEN a ruletrace-ai propose run ends after it built at least one Provider_Request, THE AI_Tool SHALL write a Run_Summary to standard output that lists the processed, the stored, and the unprocessed Candidate_Clause identifiers in ascending order of start byte offset, so that the processed and the unprocessed sets are disjoint, their union is every requested Candidate_Clause, and the stored set is a subset of the processed set, and the Run_Summary SHALL state complete only WHEN the unprocessed set is empty and SHALL state incomplete in every other case. The stored set lists only batches whose directory sync succeeded. IF the platform skips the directory sync after the hard link (Requirement 29 criterion 19), THEN the batch is stored and the Provider_Request succeeded, so the Run_Summary SHALL list its Candidate_Clauses as processed and in a list headed `stored, directory durability not confirmed (skipped by platform)` and not in the stored set, that list SHALL appear only WHEN it is not empty and SHALL NOT make the run incomplete, and the AI_Tool SHALL emit the Warning AI_STORE_DIRECTORY_SYNC_SKIPPED (RT-AI-0309) exactly once for the run, on standard error, WHEN at least one batch that the run stored had its directory sync skipped by the platform, and SHALL NOT emit it otherwise. The Warning does not change the exit code. Its location is the command `propose`, like the location of RT-AI-0306. A run summary with an empty unprocessed set and an empty unconfirmed set whose skip list is not empty SHALL state `run summary: complete, directory durability not confirmed` in its first line, and every other complete run SHALL state `run summary: complete`. A run summary that is incomplete SHALL state `run summary: incomplete` whether or not the skip list is empty, because the list under its own heading and the Warning carry the skip. A skipped directory sync SHALL NOT be reported as durability confirmed. Diagnostics of one run are written in the order of the Diagnostic ordering, which sorts by location (source span, document, input file, command line, none), then by code, then by message, so RT-AI-0301, RT-AI-0305, and RT-AI-0307, which point at input files, precede RT-AI-0306 and RT-AI-0309, which point at the command, and RT-AI-0306 precedes RT-AI-0309. One exception applies: IF the directory sync after the hard link of the batch of a Provider_Request fails (AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED, RT-AI-0303), THEN the complete batch file stays in the Proposal_Store, and the Run_Summary SHALL list the Candidate_Clauses of that Provider_Request in a fourth list headed `stored, durability not confirmed` and not as unprocessed. Those Candidate_Clauses are neither processed nor unprocessed, so the processed, the unconfirmed and the unprocessed sets are pairwise disjoint and their union is every requested Candidate_Clause, the fourth list appears only WHEN it is not empty, and the Run_Summary SHALL state complete only WHEN both the unprocessed and the unconfirmed set are empty. The Warning RT-AI-0306 keeps the trigger of criterion 37, the batch of an earlier Provider_Request, and the Warning RT-AI-0305 follows such a batch as it follows every stored batch under Requirement 30 criterion 3.
37. IF a ruletrace-ai propose run keeps the batch of an earlier Provider_Request and a later Provider_Request fails at a step 1 to 11, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL emit the Warning AI_RUN_INCOMPLETE (RT-AI-0306) once for the run with the Run_Summary, SHALL list every Candidate_Clause of the failed Provider_Request (except under criterion 36) and of every later Provider_Request as unprocessed, SHALL write no statement in any output that all requested Candidate_Clauses were processed, and SHALL exit with the exit code of the failed Provider_Request under Requirement 16 criterion 16 and not with exit code 0. The batch file that the failing Provider_Request itself keeps under criterion 36 does not trigger RT-AI-0306.
38. THE RuleTrace SHALL include Mock_Provider tests of a run with three Provider_Requests that assert, for a failure of the second Provider_Request with each of the codes RT-PRV-0101, RT-AI-0106, RT-AI-0202, and RT-AI-0303 in turn, that the Proposal_Store holds exactly the batch of the first Provider_Request (for RT-AI-0303 caused by a refused write or link; the failed directory sync is the exception that the last test of this criterion covers), that the third Provider_Request is never sent, that the Run_Summary states incomplete with the exact processed, stored, and unprocessed identifiers, that the Warning RT-AI-0306 is present, that the exit code equals the exit code of the failing code (3, 5, 6, and 70), and that no output claims completeness, and a test that asserts the same states with the first Provider_Request failing (processed and stored empty, Proposal_Store byte-identical to its state before the run) and a test that a fully successful run states complete with an empty unprocessed set, exit code 0, and no RT-AI-0306, and tests with an in-memory file system of the three outcomes of the directory sync (synced, skipped by the platform with exit code 0, the skip list and the header of criterion 36 and the Warning RT-AI-0309 once, and an unexpected failure, including PermissionDenied, with RT-AI-0303 and exit code 70), and a test of a run with three Provider_Requests whose second directory sync fails after its hard link that asserts exit code 70, the codes RT-AI-0303 and RT-AI-0306, the whole batch files of the first and the second Provider_Request in the Proposal_Store, the exact Run_Summary with the second batch listed as stored with durability not confirmed and the third Candidate_Clauses listed as unprocessed, and that the third Provider_Request is never sent.
39. THE RuleTrace SHALL send to every provider one canonical Proposal schema in a provider-neutral flat node-table form, in which an expression is a table of nodes that refer to their children by index, so that the schema has no recursive `$ref`, has a nesting depth of at most 10 levels and at most 5,000 properties, sets `additionalProperties` to false on every object, and lists every property of an object in `required` (UD-37). The local strict parser SHALL lower the table to the same Rule_Content as the nested form of Proposal_Schema version 1, and SHALL refuse a table with a missing child, a cycle, a node that is referenced twice, or a node that no node and no rule root references, each with RT-AI-0202, and SHALL lower with no recursion deeper than a fixed bound, so that an expression above the expression depth limit of 16 lowers unchanged and reaches the validator (RT-LIM-0003) and a hostile table cannot exhaust the stack. A lowered rule that nests more than 61 containers, which is the batch container limit of 64 less the three containers around a stored rule, SHALL be refused for the whole response with RT-LIM-0003 before the decoder runs, so that every accepted batch can be read back from the Proposal_Store. The schema generator SHALL live outside the AI_Integration_Crate. A test SHALL show that an expression of depth 16 round-trips through the schema to a Rule_Content that is equal to the original, that the generated schema meets the three limits as measured by an independent counter, and that a mutated schema that exceeds a limit is detected.

### Requirement 24: Pipeline Contracts and Crate Boundaries

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want every stage to declare all of its typed inputs and outputs and the crate graph to be checked automatically, so that no stage depends on hidden state or on a hidden crate.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL declare for every stage a Pipeline_Contract that lists every typed input and every typed output of the stage, and the Pipeline_Contracts SHALL be the Pipeline table of the Introduction together with the following Auxiliary stages.

| Auxiliary stage | Typed inputs | Typed outputs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| AI proposal generation (AI_Tool, steps 1 to 8 of Requirement 23) | Candidate_Clauses, Source_Models, Rulebook type declarations, AI_Configuration, Transmission_Consent, the named credential, effective limits, a caller-supplied creation time, the Proposal_Store for the idempotency check | Proposals and Proposal_Store entries, Transmission_Log entries, or Diagnostics |
| Review (Review_Group of ruletrace) | Proposal_Store entry, Review_Decision, Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Source_Models | Appended Approval_Ledger, Rulebook with the adopted Rule_Revision, updated Proposal_Store reference, or Diagnostics |
| Provenance resolution (Explainer, stage 7) | Decision_Trace, pre-loaded Source_Models | Verification status, rendered Decision_Trace, Diagnostics |

2. THE RuleTrace SHALL expose each stage and each Auxiliary stage as a public library function whose parameters are exactly the typed inputs of its Pipeline_Contract, in the order and with the types that the Pipeline_Contract lists, and whose result type is exactly the typed outputs of its Pipeline_Contract, so that a test that compares each public function signature with its Pipeline_Contract finds 0 additional parameters, 0 missing parameters, and 0 additional result components.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL contain in every Core_Crate no mutable global variable, no static mutable item, no thread-local item, no lazily initialized global, no read of an environment variable, of the working directory, of the locale, of the clock, or of a random source, and no file or network access, as verified by an automated source and dependency scan that exits with a non-zero status and reports the crate name and the offending item (or, for a dependency, the dependency name) for each violation found, except for the argument parsing, file reading, and file writing of the CLI binary crate.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL give each Auxiliary_Crate the same restrictions as criterion 3, verified by the same scan, except for the file access of the AI_Tool, the network access and the credential access of the AI_Integration_Crate, and the monotonic timer that the AI_Integration_Crate uses to enforce the timeouts and the deadline of Requirement 29, and SHALL list each of these exceptions in the crate statement of purpose of Requirement 21 criterion 1.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL store in the repository a machine-readable list of the allowed crate dependencies, SHALL document the crate dependency graph in the design document, and SHALL fail the build in an automated check that reads the actual dependency graph, exits with a non-zero status, and reports the crate name and the offending dependency edge (or, for a cycle, the crates on the cycle) when: the graph contains a cycle; the graph contains a dependency that is not in the list of allowed dependencies; a Core_Crate depends on an Auxiliary_Crate; a Core_Crate, the ruletrace binary, or the Mock_Provider crate depends on a network client library, an AI provider library, or an AI_Integration_Crate; an AI_Crate depends on the Compiler crate, the Rules_Engine crate, the crate that contains the Approval_Status operations, or a crate that contains the Approval_Ledger append function; the Rules_Engine crate depends on the Explainer crate; or a stage crate depends on a crate for a later stage.
6. WHEN a stage function is called twice with equal typed inputs, THE RuleTrace SHALL return equal typed outputs for every stage of the seven pipeline stages, where equal means identical canonical serialized bytes, as verified by a property test of at least 256 generated cases per stage in which any case with unequal outputs fails the test.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL allow a stage to consume the output of an earlier stage only through the typed value that the Pipeline_Contract of that stage lists, and SHALL permit a caller-supplied typed value such as an effective limit or a Source_Model only where the Pipeline_Contract lists it, so that a stage function has no parameter, global, or other input that its Pipeline_Contract does not list.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL permit source resolution, source hash verification, and source text rendering only in the Explainer and in the display function of the Review_Group that calls the Explainer, and the Rules_Engine, the Compiler, and the Validator SHALL perform none of them beyond the check of Requirement 6 criterion 24 against caller-supplied Source_Models, as verified by the scan of criterion 3 together with the edge check of criterion 5.
9. IF the machine-readable list of allowed crate dependencies is missing, cannot be parsed, or names a crate that is not in the workspace, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL fail the automated check of criterion 5 with a non-zero exit status and a message that names the list and the problem, and SHALL NOT treat the graph as passing.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL include at least one negative test fixture for each failure condition that is listed in criterion 5 and each violation that is listed in criterion 3, and each fixture SHALL cause the corresponding automated check to fail with the expected crate name and offending item or edge.
11. WHEN the automated checks of criteria 3 and 5 run on the unmodified workspace, THE RuleTrace SHALL report 0 violations and exit with status 0.

12. THE RuleTrace SHALL add to the allowed-dependency list of criterion 5 the edges of each Application_Layer crate, which are limited to the Rules_Engine crate, the crates for the Compiled_Rulebook and Competition_Data types and their parsers, the Explainer crate, the Source_Model crate, and the shared Diagnostic, Location, and Exact_Number crates, and SHALL fail the automated check of criterion 5, with the offending crate name and edge, WHEN an Application_Layer depends, directly or transitively, on the ruletrace binary crate, on the review library crate, on a crate that contains the Approval_Ledger append function, on the Validator, Compiler, Ingestor, or Extractor crate, on an Auxiliary_Crate, or on a network client library, or WHEN a Core_Crate or Auxiliary_Crate depends on an Application_Layer crate. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
13.
