THE RuleTrace SHALL add to the allowed-dependency list of criterion 5 the edges of the ruletrace-lab crate, which are limited to the two Application_Layer crates, the shared Diagnostic, Location, and Exact_Number crates, and one HTTP server library with a written justification, and SHALL fail the automated check of criterion 5, with the offending crate name and edge, WHEN ruletrace-lab depends, directly or transitively, on an AI_Crate, an AI provider library, the review library crate, a crate that contains the Approval_Ledger append function, the Validator, Compiler, Ingestor, or Extractor crate, or WHEN a Core_Crate, an Auxiliary_Crate, an Application_Layer crate, ruletrace, or ruletrace-ai depends on ruletrace-lab or on its HTTP server library, and SHALL fail the source scan of Requirement 36 criterion 5 WHEN a source file of ruletrace-lab calls a Rules_Engine evaluation function (UD-38). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

### Requirement 25: Rule Revisions and Approval Lifecycle

**User Story:** As a reviewer, I want every change of a rule to create a new revision that needs its own approval, so that an approval always refers to the exact content that I reviewed.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Rule_IR SHALL identify each Rule_Revision by the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number, SHALL start the Revision_Numbers of a Rule identifier at 1, SHALL increase them by exactly 1 for each new Rule_Revision, SHALL accept a Revision_Number only as an integer from 1 to 4,294,967,295, and SHALL reject a Rulebook in which the Revision_Numbers of a Rule identifier have a gap, a duplicate, or a value outside that range, as invalid input.
2. THE Rule_IR SHALL record for every Rule_Revision an Origin that is either Manual (with an optional author identity of 1 to 200 characters, counted as Unicode scalar values) or AI_Proposal (with the proposer identity, the AI_Provider identifier, the model identifier, the AI_Adapter version, the Provider_Request hash, the Provider_Response hash, and the Proposal identifier).
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep the Origin out of the Rule_Content, so that two Rule_Revisions with equal Rule_Content and different Origin have equal Rule_Content_Hashes, equal Diagnostics from the Validator, and byte-identical Compiled_Rulebooks.
4. THE Rule_Content of a Rule_Revision SHALL consist of the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the kind, the Target, every typed operand and expression, every declared type, every rounding declaration, every declared default, every interpretation marker for ambiguity and for incompleteness, and every Source_Span (the document identifier, the start offset, and the end offset) with the declared document hash that each Source_Span refers to, and SHALL exclude the Approval_Status, every Approval_Record, the Origin, every review comment and rejection reason, and every value that the Validator derives from the Rule_Content.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL compute the Rule_Content_Hash as the SHA-256 hash of the canonical serialization of the Rule_Content, with the IR_Version as part of the hashed input, so that the hash is independent of the order in which the Rule or its fields were constructed (Requirement 3 criterion 11).
6. THE Approval_Record SHALL be a Ledger_Record of Requirement 26 that names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Reviewer, the caller-supplied approval time, the Rule_Content_Hash, the IR_Version, and the Approval_Binding, and an Approval_Record SHALL be matching for a Rule_Revision only WHEN its Rule identifier, Revision_Number, Rule_Content_Hash, IR_Version, and Approval_Binding all equal those of the Rule_Revision and of the Rulebook, so that a difference in any one of the five values makes it non-matching.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL append an Approval_Record only through the approval operation of Requirement 5 criterion 9 in the Review_Group, so that an Approval_Record exists only for a Rule_Revision that a Reviewer approved while its derived Approval_Status was Proposed and its validation had no Blocking_Diagnostic other than its own APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED.
8. WHEN any part of the Rule_Content of a Rule_Revision changes, including one Source_Span offset, the addition or removal of a Source_Span, a declared document hash, a rule operand, a rounding declaration, a default, an interpretation marker, or the Revision_Number, or WHEN the IR_Version of the Rulebook changes, THE Rule_Content_Hash or the bound IR_Version SHALL differ, so that every existing Approval_Record of that Rule_Revision becomes non-matching, and the Validator SHALL apply Requirement 5 criterion 4 to the Rule_Revision.
9. WHEN a change of Origin, of an Approval_Record, of a review comment, or of the Approval_Status alone occurs, THE Rule_Content_Hash SHALL stay unchanged (byte-identical 64-character lowercase hexadecimal value).
10. WHEN a new revision of a Rule is requested, THE RuleTrace SHALL create a Rule_Revision with the Revision_Number that is 1 greater than the highest Revision_Number of that Rule identifier, with Approval_Status Proposed, with no Approval_Record, and with the requested Origin, and SHALL leave every existing Rule_Revision of that Rule identifier byte-identical. A Rule_Revision with Approval_Status Proposed MAY have its Rule_Content edited in place because it has no matching Approval_Record.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL determine the Effective_Revision of a Rule identifier as the Rule_Revision with the highest Revision_Number whose derived Approval_Status is Approved, and IF a Rule_Revision with a higher Revision_Number has lost its matching Approval_Record, THEN its derived Approval_Status SHALL be Proposed, Requirement 26 criterion 12 SHALL apply, and THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT fall back to an Approved Rule_Revision with a lower Revision_Number while that Rule_Revision is unresolved.
12. WHEN a Rule identifier has an Approved Rule_Revision with a lower Revision_Number than the Effective_Revision, THE RuleTrace SHALL treat the lower Rule_Revision as superseded, SHALL retain it and its Approval_Records unchanged, SHALL exclude it from compilation, conflict detection, and execution, and SHALL apply only the structural checks of Requirement 3 criteria 4 and 9 to it.
13. WHEN a Rule identifier has a Rule_Revision in an Unresolved_Approval_State with a Revision_Number higher than the Effective_Revision, THE Validator SHALL validate that Rule_Revision in full under Requirement 6 criterion 25, SHALL return the Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook until a Reviewer approves or rejects it. WHEN a Rule_Revision is Rejected, THE RuleTrace SHALL leave the Effective_Revision of that Rule identifier unchanged and SHALL NOT block compilation. This criterion supersedes the earlier statement that a Proposed higher revision keeps the Effective_Revision executable with a Warning.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL never delete or modify an Approval_Record, and WHEN an Approval_Record matches no current Rule_Revision of the Rulebook or does not match its Rule_Revision under criterion 6, THE Validator SHALL return exactly one Warning Diagnostic with the code APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE for that Approval_Record that names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, and the recorded Rule_Content_Hash, and the Validator SHALL not count that Approval_Record as an approval.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL apply Requirement 5 criteria 2 and 3, Requirement 6 criteria 7 and 16 to 21, Requirement 9 criterion 1, and Requirement 12 criterion 10 to Effective_Revisions only.
16. IF an Approval_Ledger contains a second decision for the same Rule_Revision with the same Rule_Content_Hash, or an approval Ledger_Record after a rejection Ledger_Record of the same Rule_Revision, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject the Approval_Ledger as invalid input with the Error Diagnostic LEDGER_RECORD_CONTRADICTORY that names the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, and the contradictory sequence numbers, and IF a Rulebook or Proposal_Store entry supplies a stored Approval_Status, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject it under Requirement 22 criterion 17. This criterion supersedes the earlier contradiction between a stored status and a matching Approval_Record.
17. THE RuleTrace SHALL include no Rule_Revision with Approval_Status Proposed or Rejected, no superseded Rule_Revision, and no Origin in any Compiled_Rulebook, Decision_Trace, or other executable artifact, except that a Decision_Trace SHALL identify each applied Rule by the Rule identifier and the Revision_Number.
18. FOR ALL Rule_Revisions, serializing and parsing a Rulebook SHALL preserve the Rule_Content_Hash of every Rule_Revision, and FOR ALL Rulebooks, the set of Rule_Revisions in the Compiled_Rulebook SHALL equal the set of Effective_Revisions with a matching Approval_Record (property tests of at least 256 generated cases).
19. IF the Rule_Content of a Rejected Rule_Revision differs from the Rule_Content_Hash recorded in its rejection Ledger_Record, THEN THE Validator SHALL return the Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_REJECTED_REVISION_MODIFIED with the first Source_Span of the Rule as its primary Location, and SHALL keep the Rule_Revision Rejected.
20. WHEN the Validator returns APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE for an Approval_Record under criterion 14, THE RuleTrace SHALL leave the derived has_errors and no_errors flags of Requirement 22, the compilability of the Rulebook, and the exit code unchanged compared with the same input without that Approval_Record.
21. WHILE a Rule identifier has an Approved Rule_Revision with Revision_Number N and a Proposed Rule_Revision with Revision_Number N+1 in an Unresolved_Approval_State, THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook, and THE Validator SHALL return one APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED Error Diagnostic that names the Rule identifier and Revision_Number N+1 and none that names Revision_Number N.
22. WHEN the Rule_Content of one Rule_Revision is serialized in any two field orders, THE RuleTrace SHALL compute byte-identical Rule_Content_Hash values of 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.

### Requirement 26: Approval Ledger and Approval Gate

**User Story:** As a reviewer, I want approvals recorded in a separate, tamper-evident ledger that gates compilation, so that no unreviewed or stale rule becomes executable.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep every Approval_Record and every rejection record in an Approval_Ledger that is a separate file from the Rulebook, that is append-only, and that is serialized canonically in a versioned data format fixed by the design (UD-2 and UD-27).
2. THE Ledger_Record SHALL contain a sequence number, the kind (approval or rejection), the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Rule_Content_Hash, the IR_Version, the Approval_Binding, the Reviewer identity, a caller-supplied time, the reason text (mandatory for a rejection), the hash of the previous Ledger_Record, and the hash of the Ledger_Record itself.
3. THE Approval_Ledger SHALL order its Ledger_Records by sequence numbers that start at 1 and increase by exactly 1 with no gap and no reuse, and the file order SHALL equal the sequence order.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL protect the Approval_Ledger by a hash chain in which the hash of each Ledger_Record is the SHA-256 hash of the canonical serialization of its other fields including the hash of the previous Ledger_Record, the first Ledger_Record names a previous hash of 64 zero characters, and the verification of the chain SHALL precede every use of the Approval_Ledger.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL supply the Approval_Ledger to the Validator as an explicit typed input, to the Compiler only inside the Validation_Result, and to the Review_Group as an explicit typed input, and no function SHALL locate, read, or default an Approval_Ledger by itself.
6. IF the ledger argument of a validate, compile, or review command names a file that does not exist or cannot be read, THEN THE CLI SHALL return the Error Diagnostic LEDGER_NOT_FOUND and exit with code 2, except that (a) WHERE the option --create-ledger is given to review approve or review reject, THE Review_Group SHALL create the file with the first Ledger_Record, and (b) WHEN the ledger argument of review list names a file that does not exist, THE Review_Group SHALL treat it as an empty, uninitialized Approval_Ledger, SHALL show for each Pending_Proposal the Revision_Number that its adoption into that empty ledger would give, SHALL exit with code 0, and SHALL create or change no file (no ledger, no lock file, no temporary file); a ledger file that exists but is malformed, truncated, or fails its hash chain SHALL still be rejected with the same Diagnostic and exit code as for every other reader, and a ledger file with a header and zero records SHALL be a valid empty Approval_Ledger.
7. THE Approval_Binding of an approval Ledger_Record SHALL list, sorted by document identifier in UTF-8 byte order, the pair of document identifier and SHA-256 document hash for every Source_Document that a Source_Span of the approved Rule_Revision references, and the Ledger_Record SHALL bind the IR_Version of the Rulebook.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL treat an Approval_Record as matching a Rule_Revision only WHEN its Rule identifier, Revision_Number, Rule_Content_Hash, IR_Version, and every pair of the Approval_Binding equal the current values of that Rule_Revision and of the Rulebook, and the hash of each bound document equals the hash that the Rulebook declares under Requirement 3 criterion 12.
9. WHEN the Rule_Content, a Source_Span, a declared document hash, or the IR_Version changes, THE RuleTrace SHALL treat every existing Approval_Record of the affected Rule_Revisions as non-matching, as Requirement 25 criterion 8 states.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL derive the Approval_Status of a Rule_Revision as Rejected WHEN a rejection Ledger_Record names its Rule identifier and Revision_Number, otherwise as Approved WHEN at least one matching Approval_Record exists, and otherwise as Proposed, and SHALL store the derived value in no file.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL define an Unresolved_Approval_State as a Rule_Revision whose derived Approval_Status is Proposed and for which no Rule_Revision of the same Rule identifier with a higher Revision_Number has the derived Approval_Status Approved or Rejected.
12. IF a Rule_Revision is in an Unresolved_Approval_State, THEN THE Validator SHALL return one Error Diagnostic APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED for it, THE validation result SHALL be failed, and THE Compiler SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook. A Rule_Revision whose earlier approval became non-matching is Proposed again and unresolved, because an Error for a state that the derivation cannot produce would be inconsistent, and the stale Approval_Record is reported by the Warning APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE.
13. WHEN a Rule_Revision is Rejected, THE RuleTrace SHALL retain it in the Rulebook or the Proposal_Store and its rejection Ledger_Record with the reason in the Approval_Ledger, SHALL include it in no Compiled_Rulebook, and SHALL NOT let it block compilation.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL classify a Diagnostic of Severity Warning as a Warning_Diagnostic that never changes compilability, a Diagnostic of Severity Error, Ambiguity, or Unsupported as a Blocking_Diagnostic that prevents compilation, and an APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED Diagnostic as a Blocking_Diagnostic of Severity Error with a dedicated code, and SHALL create no further Severity.
15. WHEN the Compiler produces a Compiled_Rulebook, THE Compiler SHALL have received a Validation_Result in which every Rule identifier has either an Effective_Revision with no Rule_Revision in an Unresolved_Approval_State, or only Rejected and superseded Rule_Revisions, and in which at least one Effective_Revision exists, so that compilation is atomic and the Rulebook is complete.
16. IF an approve request names a Rule_Revision with a Blocking_Diagnostic other than its own APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL refuse it with the Diagnostic REVIEW_BLOCKING_DIAGNOSTIC, exit with code 3, and append nothing, so that the Approval_Ledger and the Rulebook are byte-identical to their state before the request.
17. THE Review_Group SHALL refuse a Reviewer identity under Requirement 23 criterion 15, SHALL take the Reviewer identity only from a command argument, and SHALL record no Ledger_Record whose Reviewer is empty, longer than 200 characters, or equal to an AI identity. WHEN the Review_Group refuses a Reviewer identity, it SHALL return REVIEW_REVIEWER_INVALID for an empty or over-long identity or REVIEW_SELF_APPROVAL for an AI identity, a proposer, a provider, or a model, SHALL exit with code 3, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger byte-identical.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL provide no command option, flag, configuration file, environment variable, Rulebook field, Proposal_Store field, or Provider_Response content that sets a Rule_Revision to Approved or appends a Ledger_Record, other than the Review_Group, and a Ledger_Record SHALL be appended only by the review approve and review reject commands of the Review_Group.
19. WHEN the Review_Group appends a Ledger_Record, THE Review_Group SHALL write the complete Ledger_Record or nothing, SHALL write a Ledger_Record only at the end of the Approval_Ledger with the next sequence number, SHALL write no other Ledger_Record, and, for an adoption of a Proposal_Store Rule_Revision, SHALL change the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger both or neither, and IF any write fails, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL attempt a rollback, SHALL exit with code 70, SHALL return the Diagnostic REVIEW_WRITE_FAILED (RT-APR-0208) whose message states exactly one of three cases, and SHALL NOT state that the files are unchanged or consistent in any other case: (a) the rollback succeeded, so that the Approval_Ledger and the Rulebook are byte-identical to their earlier state; (b) the rollback failed after both new files were in place, so that the complete new pair is preserved, the commit journal is retained, and the message states that the decision is committed; or (c) the rollback failed while the two files were in an intermediate state, so that the commit journal is retained, the message states that the files are in an intermediate state and must not be used, and criteria 32 and 33 apply until a review command has recovered the transaction. A physically inconsistent intermediate pair SHALL NOT be described as consistent; the guarantee of case (c) is that no reader can consume it as valid application state.
20. THE Approval_Ledger parser and the Approval_Ledger pretty printer SHALL satisfy the round-trip property that parsing the printed form of a valid Approval_Ledger yields an equal Approval_Ledger, and printing the same Approval_Ledger twice SHALL yield byte-identical output.
21. IF an Approval_Ledger is not parseable, has an unknown or missing field, a broken hash chain, a sequence gap, a duplicate or contradictory decision, or an invalid Reviewer, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL reject it as invalid input with the Diagnostic LEDGER_MALFORMED, LEDGER_CHAIN_BROKEN, LEDGER_SEQUENCE_INVALID, LEDGER_RECORD_CONTRADICTORY, or LEDGER_REVIEWER_INVALID, an Input_Location of the first offending record, and exit code 2, and SHALL run no later stage, so that no Approval_Status is derived and no Compiled_Rulebook is produced from that Approval_Ledger.
22. THE RuleTrace SHALL document that the hash chain detects edits of earlier Ledger_Records but does not authenticate a Reviewer and cannot detect a complete rewrite of the file by a person with write access (R-8).
23. WHEN an Approval_Ledger and a Rulebook are generated, THE RuleTrace SHALL satisfy these properties of at least 256 generated cases each: the derived Approval_Status is a function of the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger only; a change of any bound value makes every earlier Approval_Record non-matching; a change of one byte of any Ledger_Record breaks the chain; a Rulebook with an Unresolved_Approval_State never yields a Compiled_Rulebook; and a Rulebook with no Effective_Revision never yields a Compiled_Rulebook.
24. IF the Rule_Content_Hash that a Reviewer names in an approve or reject request differs from the current Rule_Content_Hash of the named Rule_Revision, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL refuse the request with the Diagnostic REVIEW_STALE_HASH, exit with code 3, and append nothing.
25. IF an approve or reject request names a Rule_Revision that already has a rejection Ledger_Record, or an approve request names a Rule_Revision that already has a matching Approval_Record, THEN THE Review_Group SHALL refuse it with the Diagnostic REVIEW_INVALID_TRANSITION, exit with code 3, and append nothing, so that Rejected stays terminal.
26. WHEN a test supplies each of these channels one at a time, namely a command option or flag, a configuration file entry, an environment variable, a Rulebook field, a Proposal_Store field, and a Provider_Response that claims approval, THE RuleTrace SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger byte-identical, SHALL derive the Approval_Status Proposed for the affected Rule_Revision, and SHALL produce no Compiled_Rulebook that contains it, with at least one test for each channel.
27. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a build-time source scan and a dependency-graph test, under Requirement 27, that fail the build WHEN any function that appends a Ledger_Record is referenced from a crate other than the Review_Group crate and the CLI crate that invokes it, or WHEN an AI crate depends on that function's crate.
28. WHILE a Pending_Proposal is held in the Proposal_Store, THE RuleTrace SHALL keep it in the Proposal_Store, separate from the Rulebook, SHALL NOT count it as a Rule_Revision of the Rulebook, and SHALL NOT let it block compilation of the existing Rulebook, so that the validate and compile commands return the same Diagnostics, the same exit code, and the same Compiled_Rulebook bytes for the same Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, and Source_Models whatever Pending_Proposals the Proposal_Store holds, including none, and whatever Blocking_Diagnostics their Semantic_Outcomes carry, and review list SHALL show each Pending_Proposal.
29. WHILE a Rule_Revision is present in the Rulebook with the derived Approval_Status Proposed, THE RuleTrace SHALL treat it as in an Unresolved_Approval_State under Requirement 5 criterion 5 and Requirement 25 criterion 13, so that it blocks compilation with APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED (RT-APR-0001) and exit code 3 whatever its Origin, and SHALL NOT apply this treatment to a Pending_Proposal in the Proposal_Store.
30. WHEN review approve adopts a Pending_Proposal, THE Review_Group SHALL add the Rule_Revision to the Rulebook as a new Rule_Revision together with its approval Ledger_Record in the single all-or-nothing operation of criterion 19, so that, after a successful operation, the derived Approval_Status of that Rule_Revision is Approved, and, at no point that a reader of the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger can observe, does the Rulebook contain that Rule_Revision without its matching Approval_Record.
31. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests that assert, for each of 0, 1, and several Pending_Proposals, including one whose Semantic_Outcome has a Blocking_Diagnostic, that validate and compile on an unchanged Rulebook give byte-identical Diagnostics, exit code, and Compiled_Rulebook; that the same Rule_Revision, placed in the Rulebook with no Approval_Record, gives APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, exit code 3, and no Compiled_Rulebook; that review approve of a Pending_Proposal with a write failure injected at each write step leaves the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, and the Proposal_Store byte-identical; and that after a successful review approve the Compiled_Rulebook contains the adopted Rule_Revision as an Effective_Revision.
32. THE validate command, THE review list command, THE evaluate command WHEN it reads a Rulebook and an Approval_Ledger for a Proof_Report, and every later application that reads that Rulebook and Approval_Ledger (Prize Check and Bracket Check) SHALL use the two files only as a pair that passed every one of these steps in order: take the shared review lock, which every file system and platform that RuleTrace runs on must provide; check for the commit journal; read the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger; check for the commit journal again; read both files a second time; and compare each file byte for byte with its first read. IF the file system or platform has no advisory locks, so that the shared lock cannot be taken because locking is unsupported, THEN THE command SHALL return the Error Diagnostic REVIEW_LOCKING_UNSUPPORTED (RT-APR-0215) that names the path and states that the command refused because advisory locks are required, SHALL exit with code 70, SHALL read no file, SHALL change no file, SHALL write no output, and SHALL NOT use any lock file, lock marker or other substitute for the advisory lock; WHEN the lock file does not exist, THE command SHALL establish that locking is supported by a shared lock on the Rulebook before it reads any file. IF a journal exists at either check, or a journal cannot be inspected, or a review command holds the review lock, or the lock file did not exist at the start and exists at the end, or either file differs between the two reads, THEN THE command SHALL return the Error Diagnostic REVIEW_COMMIT_JOURNAL_BLOCKS_READ (RT-APR-0214) that names the journal, the lock or the changed file, SHALL exit with code 2, SHALL change no file, SHALL write no output, and SHALL use no byte of either file. WHEN a journal exists at the first check, THE command SHALL read neither the Rulebook nor the Approval_Ledger nor any other input. THE command SHALL parse only the bytes of the second read and SHALL NOT read either file again. THE compile command reads only a Validation_Result, never the live Rulebook or Approval_Ledger, and so is covered by the refusal of the validate command that produces its input.
33. WHEN review approve or review reject has taken its lock, THE Review_Group SHALL recover an existing commit journal before it loads or changes the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, the Proposal_Store, or the Source_Models, so that: both new files lead to cleanup only; a new ledger with the old Rulebook and the intact new Rulebook file leads to completion of the commit; a new ledger with the old Rulebook and no new Rulebook file leads to restoration of the ledger from its backup, which SHALL match the journal hash, or to removal of the ledger WHEN the commit created it; both old files lead to cleanup only; and any other state, a journal that does not name the files of the command, or a damaged backup leads to the Error Diagnostic REVIEW_WRITE_FAILED with exit code 70 and no changed file. THE Review_Group SHALL restore the Rulebook before the ledger in a rollback, so that no recovery or rollback step produces a Rulebook that holds a Rule_Revision while the Approval_Ledger lacks its matching Approval_Record.
34. WHEN an adoption has replaced both files and only its cleanup fails, THE Review_Group SHALL exit with code 0, SHALL return the Warning REVIEW_COMMIT_JOURNAL_PRESENT (RT-APR-0211) that states that the commit completed, and SHALL leave the journal in place, so that criterion 32 makes every reader refuse until the next review approve or review reject completes the cleanup under criterion 33. WHEN a recovery has run, THE Review_Group SHALL report what it did with the same Warning.
35. THE RuleTrace SHALL include fault-injection tests, with a file system that fails or ends the process at a numbered operation, that inject a failure and a crash at each rename, each rollback step, and each recovery step of an adoption, singly and as a second fault during the handling of a first, for an existing and for a created ledger, and that assert the exact Diagnostic code, the exit code, the byte content of both files, that no reached state is a Rulebook with the adopted Rule_Revision and a ledger without its record, that every reader of criterion 32 refuses in each state in which a journal exists, that each reader refuses with RT-APR-0214 and changes nothing WHEN a commit, a rollback, a journal, or a lock file appears or completes between its two reads, in either direction and for a change of one file alone, that each reader blocks WHEN its journal check or its lock fails, and that a second recovery after any recovery fault reaches the old pair or the new pair with no commit file left.
36. THE RuleTrace SHALL document, in the user-facing description of the adoption protocol, that an attempt marker torn by a process kill or a full disk is refused and kept, and that a person may remove the marker file named in the message only WHEN no review command is running.
37. WHEN review approve or review reject starts, THE Review_Group SHALL take the exclusive review lock on the lock file next to the ledger before it reads or changes any file, SHALL never wait for it, and SHALL NOT substitute a lock file created with an exclusive-create operation or any other marker for an advisory lock. IF another process holds the lock, THEN THE command SHALL return the Error Diagnostic REVIEW_LOCK_UNAVAILABLE (RT-APR-0212) with exit code 70. IF the file system or platform has no advisory locks, THEN THE command SHALL return the Error Diagnostic REVIEW_LOCKING_UNSUPPORTED (RT-APR-0215) that names the lock path and states that the command refused because advisory locks are required, SHALL exit with code 70, SHALL read no file, SHALL leave no file created and no file changed, and SHALL do the same for the recovery of an interrupted commit. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests, with a file system double that reports locking as unsupported, that assert for validate, review list, evaluate with a Proof_Report, review approve, review reject and the recovery of an existing journal the exact code, the exit code, that no file was read, that the files are byte-identical to their state before the command, and that no lock file was created, and that a lock held by another process keeps RT-APR-0212 for writers and RT-APR-0214 for readers.

### Requirement 27: Executables and Responsibilities

**User Story:** As a maintainer, I want exactly three runtime executables with separated responsibilities, so that no network or AI code can reach the deterministic workflow or the approval ledger, and so that the browser interface can reach neither the AI credential nor the approval ledger directly. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL deliver exactly three Runtime_Executables, ruletrace, ruletrace-ai (the AI_Tool), and ruletrace-lab (the Laboratory executable of Requirements 36 and 37), and no other Runtime_Executable, and SHALL build prize-check and bracket-check as library packages without a binary target (Requirement 31 criterion 3). A development or test utility that the workspace builds and does not deliver, for example rt-xtask, is not a Runtime_Executable and SHALL appear in a documented allow-list of such utilities. The responsibilities of ruletrace and ruletrace-ai are those of the following table, and those of ruletrace-lab are those of Requirements 36 and 37. This criterion supersedes the earlier statements of exactly two executables (UD-22) and of exactly two Demonstration_Executables (UD-33). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

| Responsibility | ruletrace | ruletrace-ai |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Commands | ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, review list, review approve, review reject | propose (with the option to propose a revision of a named Rule_Revision), models (Requirement 35 criterion 18) |
| Reads | Source_Documents, Source_Models, Rule_IR, Approval_Ledger, Proposal_Store, Competition_Data, Compiled_Rulebook | AI_Configuration, Source_Models, Candidate_Clauses, Rulebook type declarations, Proposal_Store, one named credential environment variable (models reads only the AI_Configuration and the credential variable) |
| Writes | Source_Models, Candidate_Clauses, Validation_Result, Compiled_Rulebook, Outcomes, Decision_Traces, Approval_Ledger (review only), Rulebook (review approve adoption only), Proposal_Store references (review only) | Proposal_Store, Transmission_Log |
| Network | None | Only through an AI_Integration_Crate to the configured endpoint |
| Credentials | None | One environment variable named in the AI_Configuration |
| Appends to the Approval_Ledger | Only through the Review_Group | Never |
| Calls an approval function | Only the Review_Group | Never, enforced by crate dependency |
| Provider libraries in its dependency tree | None | Allowed in AI_Integration_Crates only |
| Determinism | Identical inputs give identical output | Not deterministic across provider calls, with auditable request and response hashes |

2. THE ruletrace executable SHALL provide exactly the commands ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, and the Review_Group subcommands list, approve, and reject, and THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL provide exactly the commands propose and models.
3. THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL NOT create, modify, or delete the Rulebook, the Approval_Ledger, or any Compiled_Rulebook, whether a run succeeds or fails, and SHALL write no file other than the Proposal_Store and the Transmission_Log.
4. THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL contain no dependency, direct or transitive, in any build profile and with all features enabled, on the review library crate, on the crate that contains the Approval_Status operations, or on any crate that contains the Approval_Ledger append function, so that it can never write an Approval_Record or call an approval function.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that resolves the full dependency tree of the ruletrace binary (normal, build, and dev dependencies, all features enabled) and fails with the offending crate name and its dependency path when the tree contains a network client library, an AI provider library, an AI_Integration_Crate, an AI_Adapter crate, or the AI_Tool crate.
6. THE ruletrace executable SHALL load no provider code at run time, SHALL use no dynamic library loading, no plugin mechanism, and no subprocess, and SHALL NOT start ruletrace-ai.
7. THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL NOT start the ruletrace executable and SHALL NOT open or read the Approval_Ledger file.
8. IF a ruletrace-ai run ends with any exit code other than 0, including termination by a signal, THEN THE RuleTrace SHALL leave every Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, and Proposal_Store byte-identical to its state before the run (except that the Proposal_Store retains each batch that an earlier Provider_Request of the same run wrote, under Requirement 23 criteria 35 to 37, and the failed-run store state of criterion 35 for a failed directory sync), SHALL leave as the only change a Transmission_Log entry that was written before the send (every earlier Transmission_Log entry unchanged), and SHALL leave every ruletrace command operable with byte-identical standard output, standard error, output files, and exit code for the same inputs as before the run.
9. THE ruletrace executable SHALL produce byte-identical standard output, standard error, and output files, and the same exit code, for the same inputs, in each combination of these three conditions: the ruletrace-ai executable is present or absent; an AI_Configuration file is present or absent; the credential environment variable named in an AI_Configuration is set (to a canary value) or unset.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of criteria 3, 4, 5, 7, and 8, and a test that the set of exit codes each executable can return, per command, equals the tables of Requirement 16 criteria 15 and 16 (ruletrace: 0, 2, 3, 4, 70; ruletrace-ai: 0, 2, 3, 5, 6, 70), with no other code occurring in any test run.
11. IF the ruletrace executable is invoked with a command or Review_Group subcommand outside the set of criterion 2, or the ruletrace-ai executable is invoked with a command other than propose and models, THEN THE executable SHALL exit with code 2, SHALL write an Error Diagnostic whose Location is the offending command-line argument to standard error, and SHALL read and write no other file.
12. THE ruletrace executable SHALL write the Approval_Ledger, the Rulebook, and Proposal_Store references only in review approve and review reject, SHALL write the Rulebook only in review approve adoption, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger, the Rulebook, and the Proposal_Store byte-identical after every run of ingest, validate, compile, evaluate, and review list.
13. THE ruletrace executable SHALL open no network connection and SHALL read no environment variable named as a credential in an AI_Configuration, and THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL open network connections only through an AI_Integration_Crate, only to the endpoint of the AI_Configuration, and SHALL read exactly one credential environment variable, the one that the AI_Configuration names.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of criteria 2, 6, 9, 11, 12, and 13 that assert the exact exit code and the byte-level file state before and after each run, and a source scan that fails with the file and line when the ruletrace sources contain a subprocess, dynamic library loading, or network call.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that the set of executables that the workspace builds equals exactly ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, and ruletrace-lab, together with the development and test utilities of the allow-list of criterion 1, and that fails with the name of any other executable (including a binary target of prize-check or bracket-check), and criteria 5 to 7 and 9 to 13 SHALL apply to ruletrace and ruletrace-ai unchanged, so that ruletrace-lab is not a command of ruletrace, is not started by ruletrace or ruletrace-ai, and does not appear in the dependency tree of ruletrace or ruletrace-ai. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

### Requirement 28: AI Configuration, Credentials, and Transmission Consent

**User Story:** As a security-conscious operator, I want AI to run only under an explicit configuration, a named credential, and my consent for each invocation, so that no document or secret leaves my machine unexpectedly.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE AI_Tool SHALL read its AI_Configuration only from the single TOML file that a command argument names, SHALL use no default path, no working directory file, and no environment variable to find it, and IF no file is named or the file does not set the enabling field to true, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL treat AI as disabled, SHALL return AI_DISABLED, SHALL read no environment variable, SHALL make no network call, SHALL send nothing, and SHALL exit with code 2.
2. THE AI_Configuration SHALL contain these fields and no others: a schema version (1 or 2, Requirement 35 criterion 9); the enabling field; the provider identifier and the protocol (the single provider kind of version 1 maps to both); the model identifier; the endpoint URL; credential_env_var, which is the name of the environment variable that holds the credential; the optional reasoning effort, verify_model, and allow_provider_training of Requirement 35; the optional capability declarations of Requirement 35 criterion 4; the limits of Requirement 29 (total deadline, per-attempt timeout, maximum retries, maximum response bytes) and the maximum Candidate_Clauses per Provider_Request, with an accepted range of 1 to 100; allow_document_text, which permits the transmission of Source_Document text; the list of AI identities; and the path of the Transmission_Log. Every field except the enabling field, the four limits of Requirement 29 (which take the defaults of Requirement 29 criterion 1), and the maximum Candidate_Clauses (default 20) SHALL be required WHILE the enabling field is true, and the absent enabling field SHALL mean false.
3. IF the AI_Configuration is not valid TOML, lacks a required field, contains an unknown field, or has a field of the wrong type, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INVALID with the Input_Location of the offending field (file, 1-based line, and 1-based column) and exit with code 2, and IF a limit is outside the range of Requirement 29 criterion 1 or the maximum Candidate_Clauses is outside 1 to 100, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_LIMIT_INVALID with the Input_Location of that field and exit with code 2.
4. IF the AI_Configuration contains a field whose name contains key, token, secret, password, or authorization (compared case-insensitively) instead of credential_env_var, or a value in any field that begins with sk-, sk_, Bearer, ghp_, or AKIA, or a credential_env_var value that is not a valid environment variable name made of upper-case letters, digits, and underscores that does not begin with a digit, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INLINE_SECRET with the Input_Location of the offending field, SHALL echo no part of the offending value, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2.
5. THE AI_Tool SHALL read no environment variable other than the one that credential_env_var names, SHALL read it only after the checks of criteria 3, 4, and 7 have passed, and SHALL NOT detect the provider, the model, or the enabling state from the environment, and IF that variable is unset or has an empty value, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CREDENTIAL_MISSING with the Input_Location of the credential_env_var field and without any value, SHALL make no network call, SHALL send nothing, and SHALL exit with code 2.
6. IF the endpoint scheme (compared case-insensitively) is not https and the host is not a loopback address (127.0.0.0/8, ::1, or localhost), THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_INSECURE with the Input_Location of the endpoint field, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2, and THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL verify the server certificate chain and the host name for every https connection, SHALL offer no option, configuration field, or environment setting that disables certificate or host name verification, and SHALL report a verification failure as AI_PROVIDER_TLS_FAILED.
7. THE AI_Tool SHALL send Source_Document text only WHILE allow_document_text is true and the invocation carries a Transmission_Consent argument whose list of document identifiers equals, by exact string comparison, the set of identifiers of the Source_Documents whose text the Provider_Request contains (no missing identifier and no additional identifier), and otherwise (including WHILE allow_document_text is false) SHALL return AI_CONSENT_MISSING with the Location of the Transmission_Consent argument or, when that argument is absent, of the command line, SHALL send nothing, and SHALL exit with code 2.
8. BEFORE each transmission attempt, including each retry, THE AI_Tool SHALL append one entry to the Transmission_Log that contains the provider identifier, the protocol, the model identifier, the endpoint host, the document identifiers with their SHA-256 hashes, the number of Candidate_Clauses, the byte size of the Provider_Request, the Request_Identifier, the attempt number (starting at 1), and a caller-supplied time, and SHALL record no document text, no response text, and no credential, and IF the append fails, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_TRANSMISSION_LOG_FAILED, SHALL send nothing, and SHALL exit with code 70.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL replace every occurrence of the credential value with [REDACTED] in every Diagnostic, log line, Transmission_Log entry, standard output, standard error, Proposal_Store entry, Decision_Trace, Fixture, and Origin metadata before the text is written, including text that a provider error message or response echoes and including a form of the value that has the prefix Bearer and a space, and THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT store a Provider_Response payload that contains the credential value or any run of at least 8 consecutive characters of it, and THE RuleTrace SHALL write the credential value to no file.
10. WHERE the platform reports file permissions, WHEN the AI_Configuration file has a read permission bit set for the group or for other users, THE AI_Tool SHALL return the Warning AI_CONFIG_PERMISSIONS with the Input_Location of the file and continue with an unchanged exit code.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL use only publicly published competition documents in the initial demonstration, in every Fixture, and in every live test, and SHALL record the source and the permission of use of each such document in the repository.
12. THE AI_Tool SHALL select the AI_Provider by the provider identifier and the protocol and the model by the model identifier of the AI_Configuration, so that replacing the provider identifier, the protocol, or the model needs no source change and no rebuild, WHERE the AI_Provider is built into the executable, and IF no built-in AI_Provider implements the pair of provider identifier and protocol, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN with the Input_Location of the provider identifier or protocol field, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2. The endpoint host, the model guard, and the capability check of Requirement 35 apply under the order of criterion 17.
13. WHERE the provider API offers a parameter that disables provider-side retention of the request, THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL set that parameter to disable retention in every Provider_Request that it sends.
14. THE AI_Tool SHALL send in a Provider_Request only the Clause_Context of Requirement 23 criterion 2 and the task, and no credential, no environment value, and no text of a document that Transmission_Consent does not name, and THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL place the credential only in the authentication field of the request to the endpoint. IF a built Provider_Request contains the credential value or a run of at least 8 consecutive characters of it in its plain, JSON-escaped, or control-escaped form, whether from a clause text, the feedback, or the revised rule content, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL refuse the run with AI_REQUEST_CONTAINS_CREDENTIAL (RT-AI-0308) before the Transmission_Log entry and any send, SHALL write nothing, and SHALL NOT include the matched text in any output. A --dry-run reads no credential and is not subject to this check.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL fail a test when a credential-looking string (a value that matches a pattern of criterion 4) appears in the source, a Fixture, an AI_Configuration example, or a golden file, and SHALL include the canary credential test of Requirement 23 criterion 23 for the Transmission_Log and the Proposal_Store.
16. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of criteria 1 to 10, 12, 14, and 17 to 19, each asserting the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity, the Location, the exit code, and that no network call and no transmission occurred.
17. WHEN the AI_Tool loads an AI_Configuration, THE AI_Tool SHALL run the checks in this order and SHALL return only the Diagnostics of the earliest failing check: TOML syntax (criterion 3), inline secret (criterion 4), the remaining field checks (criterion 3), limits (criterion 3), endpoint scheme (criterion 6), endpoint host (Requirement 35 criterion 6), disabled state (criterion 1), provider identifier and protocol (criterion 12), model guard (Requirement 35 criterion 8), capability negotiation (Requirement 35 criterion 3), consent (criterion 7), credential (criterion 5), the model verification of Requirement 35 criterion 19 WHERE verify_model is true, Transmission_Log append (criterion 8), and transmission.
18. IF the endpoint URL contains a user name, a password, or a query component, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INLINE_SECRET with the Input_Location of the endpoint field, SHALL echo no part of the URL component, and SHALL exit with code 2, and THE AI_Tool SHALL offer no command argument and no AI_Configuration field that carries the credential value.
19. IF a provider response redirects the request to a different scheme or a different host, THEN THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL NOT follow the redirect, SHALL NOT send the credential or any Source_Document text to the redirect target, and SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_ERROR without retry and exit with code 5.
20. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests, using a canary credential and a loopback stub server, that assert for criteria 9 and 13 that the canary value appears in none of the outputs listed in criterion 9 and that the retention parameter is present in the request that the stub receives.

21. THE RuleTrace SHALL apply criterion 11 to every document of the Demo_Corpus, and WHERE a demonstration document is transmitted to the AI_Provider in the live-provider step of Requirement 33 criterion 10, THE AI_Tool SHALL require the Transmission_Consent of criterion 7 for that document, so that no demonstration shortcut bypasses the consent, the configuration, or the Transmission_Log.

### Requirement 29: Provider Call Policy: Deadline, Retry, and Idempotency

**User Story:** As a rules author, I want a bounded, deterministic retry policy that cannot create duplicate or inconsistent proposals, so that a flaky provider never corrupts my proposal store.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE AI_Tool SHALL apply a total request deadline of 60 seconds, a per-attempt timeout of 20 seconds, at most 2 retries, and a response limit of 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes) as defaults, and WHERE the AI_Configuration sets a value, THE AI_Tool SHALL accept a total deadline from 1 to 300 seconds, a per-attempt timeout from 1 to 120 seconds that is not above the total deadline, a retry count from 0 to 2, and a response limit from 1,024 to 16,777,216 bytes (UD-19). The total deadline SHALL start when the first attempt starts, SHALL include every waiting time, and SHALL NOT restart. The per-attempt timeout SHALL start when that attempt starts and SHALL end only when the complete response body has been received. A value outside these ranges is rejected under AI_CONFIG_LIMIT_INVALID before any transmission.
2. THE AI_Tool SHALL retry only a Transient_Failure, which is a per-attempt timeout, a connection refusal, a connection reset or other unavailability (including a connection reset before the response body is complete), an HTTP 429 response, or an HTTP 5xx response other than 504, and SHALL NOT retry an HTTP 504 response (a gateway timeout, a final failure for every provider adapter, decided by the user on 2026-11-16 as option B of OD-37-1), an authentication failure, a TLS failure, any other HTTP 4xx response, an oversize response, a malformed response, a schema violation, a forbidden field, an incomplete response, or a provenance failure.
3. WHEN an HTTP 429 response carries a Retry-After header whose value is a decimal integer of 1 to 9 digits (whole seconds), THE AI_Tool SHALL wait the larger of that value and the scheduled waiting time of criterion 4, and SHALL treat a Retry-After header in date form, a header with any other syntax, and a header on any response other than HTTP 429 as absent, because ruletrace-ai reads no wall clock. A Retry-After value of 0 SHALL leave the scheduled waiting time unchanged.
4. THE AI_Tool SHALL wait 2 seconds before the first retry and 8 seconds before the second retry, SHALL use no jitter and no random value, and SHALL measure every wait and every deadline with a monotonic timer only.
5. WHEN a retry is still permitted by the retry count and the remaining total deadline is less than the waiting time of criteria 3 and 4 plus the per-attempt timeout, THE AI_Tool SHALL start no further attempt, SHALL wait no longer, and SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED with the number of attempts (a decimal integer from 1 to 3) and the code name of the per-attempt Warning of the last failure in the message. A remaining deadline equal to the waiting time plus the per-attempt timeout SHALL permit the attempt.
6. WHEN the last permitted attempt (attempt number 1 plus the configured retry count) ended in a Transient_Failure, THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED with the number of attempts (a decimal integer from 1 to 3) and the code name of the per-attempt Warning of the last failure in the message, and SHALL emit the per-attempt Warning of Requirement 23 criterion 17 for each failed attempt, so that the number of per-attempt Warnings equals the number of attempts. AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED and AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED SHALL be mutually exclusive for one invocation, and each SHALL exit with code 5.
7. THE AI_Tool SHALL derive the Request_Identifier as the SHA-256 hash of the canonical bytes of the Provider_Request, encoded as 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters, so that equal requests have equal Request_Identifiers and any difference in the Clause_Context, the task, the model, or the limits changes it.
8. THE AI_Tool SHALL run the attempts of one Provider_Request one after another with no two attempts in flight at the same time, SHALL parse the Provider_Response of at most one attempt, namely the first complete HTTP 2xx response that was received within the limits, and SHALL discard without parsing, storing, or logging its content the response of every failed or timed-out attempt, including a response that arrives after the timeout and a partially received response body.
9. THE AI_Tool SHALL derive the identifier of each Proposal as the SHA-256 hash of the Request_Identifier, a colon, and the zero-based index of the Proposal in the Provider_Response, encoded as 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.
10. THE AI_Tool SHALL accept at most one batch for a Request_Identifier, and WHEN the Proposal_Store already holds a batch with that Request_Identifier, THE AI_Tool SHALL make no provider call, SHALL send nothing, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store byte-identical, SHALL return the Warning AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED, and SHALL exit with code 0.
11. THE AI_Tool SHALL store a batch in one atomic and idempotent write keyed by the Request_Identifier, so that WHEN two invocations with the same Request_Identifier run at the same time, exactly one batch exists afterwards, the Proposal_Store contains the Proposals of that batch exactly once, and the other invocation returns the Warning AI_BATCH_ALREADY_STORED, stores nothing, and exits with code 0.
12. THE AI_Tool SHALL perform no retry after a Provider_Response was received completely with a 2xx status, so that a malformed, schema-violating, or provenance-failing Provider_Response is rejected once with its own Diagnostic code and exit code 6, after exactly one attempt that received that response, and is never replaced by a later attempt.
13. THE AI_Tool SHALL implement the retry decision as a pure function of the sequence of attempt outcomes and the elapsed monotonic time, so that a test with a simulated timer reproduces the attempts, the waits, and the final Diagnostic exactly.
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests with the Mock_Provider and a simulated timer, and with the loopback stub server, for each Transient_Failure, for each non-retried failure, for a Retry-After value that fits and one that does not fit the deadline, for retry exhaustion, for deadline exhaustion, for a late response from a timed-out attempt that is never stored, for success after one and after two retries with exactly one stored batch, and for the repeated run and the concurrent run of criteria 10 and 11, each asserting the exact Diagnostic code, the attempt count, the exit code, and the Proposal_Store bytes. These tests SHALL also cover criteria 15 to 19.
15. IF a failure that is not a Transient_Failure ends an attempt, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL start no further attempt and SHALL return the Diagnostic of that failure (AI_PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED, AI_PROVIDER_TLS_FAILED, or AI_PROVIDER_ERROR for any HTTP status other than 2xx, 429, and 5xx), and not AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED or AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, even when an earlier attempt ended in a Transient_Failure. The Error SHALL exit with code 5, and SHALL be accompanied by one per-attempt Warning for each earlier failed Transient_Failure attempt.
16. THE AI_Tool SHALL send, in every attempt of one invocation, byte-identical Provider_Request bytes with the same Request_Identifier, and SHALL compute the Request_Identifier once, before the first attempt.
17. IF the invocation ends with any Error of criteria 5, 6, 12, or 15, or with an Error exit code of 5 or 6, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL leave the Proposal_Store, the Rulebook, and the Approval_Ledger byte-identical to their state before the failing Provider_Request (which keeps a batch of an earlier Provider_Request of the same run under Requirement 23 criteria 35 to 37), and SHALL store no Proposal and no partial batch.
18. WHEN an attempt succeeds under criterion 8 after zero, one, or two failed Transient_Failure attempts, THE AI_Tool SHALL produce the same stored batch bytes, the same Proposal identifiers, and the same exit code 0 regardless of the number of earlier failed attempts, and SHALL emit one per-attempt Warning for each earlier failed attempt.
19. IF the existing Proposal_Store is malformed at the start of an invocation, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_STORE_INVALID with exit code 2 before any provider call, and IF the atomic write of criterion 11 fails, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED with exit code 70 and leave the Proposal_Store byte-identical, except that IF only the directory sync after the hard link fails, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED with exit code 70, SHALL keep the complete batch file at its final name because a concurrent run may already have reported it as stored, and SHALL NOT leave a partial batch file. The directory sync has three outcomes: synced, skipped by the platform, and failed. On Windows the AI_Tool SHALL skip the directory sync because the standard library cannot open a directory for a flush there, so RuleTrace does not attempt one, SHALL still flush the batch file content before the hard link, and SHALL report the skip as a successful store with exit code 0, the status of Requirement 23 criterion 36 and the Warning AI_STORE_DIRECTORY_SYNC_SKIPPED (RT-AI-0309), and SHALL NOT return AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED for it. AI_STORE_WRITE_FAILED is reserved for a real failure. On Windows, a power loss shortly after a store write may lose the directory entry of the batch, and RuleTrace does not claim otherwise.

### Requirement 30: Proposal Batch Acceptance and Protocol Failures

**User Story:** As a reviewer, I want a malformed or unverifiable provider batch rejected completely and a well-formed batch stored with its validation outcomes, so that I never review a partially trusted batch.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. IF a Protocol_Failure of the transport (RT-AI-0104 to RT-AI-0108), of the response schema (RT-AI-0201 to RT-AI-0205), of the credential check of the response (RT-AI-0206), of the revision target (RT-AI-0207), or of the provenance check (RT-PRV-0101 to RT-PRV-0104) occurs for any one element of a Provider_Response, even when every other element is valid, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the complete batch, SHALL store no Proposal and no Rule_Revision of it, SHALL leave the Proposal_Store byte-identical to its state before the failing Provider_Request (Requirement 23 criteria 35 to 37), SHALL return the Diagnostic with the specific code and the primary Location of the failure, SHALL exit with the code that the Diagnostic_Catalogue gives for the earliest failing stage under Requirement 16 criterion 17, and SHALL NOT accept the Proposals that passed. A rejected Provider_Response is never stored in any form. WHERE the batch of a Provider_Response is stored, the Proposal_Store SHALL retain the accepted response payload of that batch (accepted meaning that it passed the protocol, schema, and provenance checks, and not that a reviewer approved it), and SHALL NOT retain a credential, an authorization header, or other transport data (UD-36).
2. WHEN a Proposal is well-formed and its provenance is verified, THE AI_Tool SHALL store it as a Rule_Revision with Origin AI_Proposal, with the Semantic_Outcome that the Validator produced, including each Ambiguity, Unsupported, or Error Diagnostic with its Severity, code, and Location, and with a derived Approval_Status Proposed that is not executable, and SHALL NOT treat the Semantic_Outcome as a Protocol_Failure. This applies equally to a Proposal whose Semantic_Outcome has no Diagnostic.
3. WHEN a stored batch contains at least one Proposal with a Blocking_Diagnostic, THE AI_Tool SHALL emit the Warning AI_BATCH_HAS_BLOCKING_PROPOSALS (RT-AI-0305) once for the batch, SHALL exit with code 0, and SHALL count no Semantic_Outcome toward the exit codes 3, 5, or 6.
4. THE AI_Tool SHALL verify the provenance of every Proposal by recomputing from the Source_Models that the caller supplied, without using any verification result of the AI_Provider. The document hash comes from the Source_Model. The span is checked against the Source_Model bytes. The quoted text is compared byte for byte with the bytes of the span, without normalization. Any hash, line number, column number, quote, or validation claim that the AI_Provider made is compared with the recomputed value and never trusted. A claim that differs from the recomputed value gives RT-PRV-0102 for a document hash, RT-PRV-0104 for a quote, and RT-PRV-0101 for a span, line, or column.
5. THE AI_Tool SHALL derive the has_errors flag and every other validation status of a stored Proposal from the Diagnostic_State of the Validator under Requirement 22 and SHALL store no flag that the AI_Provider supplied.
6. THE AI_Tool SHALL store each Semantic_Outcome Diagnostic with its Severity, code, and Location, and the Review_Group SHALL refuse to approve a Rule_Revision with a Blocking_Diagnostic under Requirement 26 criterion 16.
7. THE AI_Tool SHALL create no Rule_Revision from a Proposal of the kinds clause relationship, conflict highlight, or candidate test, which are advisory under Requirement 18 criterion 18, so that the number of Rule_Revisions of a batch equals the number of its structured rule suggestions.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL include, for each failure mode of Requirement 16 criterion 16 and for each Semantic_Outcome kind, a Mock_Provider test that asserts the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity, the primary Location, the exit code, and either that the Proposal_Store is byte-identical to its prior state (protocol failure) or that it holds the batch with the Diagnostics (Semantic_Outcome). THE RuleTrace SHALL also include a prompt injection test with a Source_Document that instructs approval, which asserts that the Approval_Ledger gains no record, that every stored Rule_Revision has the derived Approval_Status Proposed, and that no Diagnostic code changes.
9. IF a Provider_Response supplies a field named approval, reviewer, validation status, has_errors, or no_errors, whether it appears alone, with a value that agrees with the Validator, or with contradictory values such as has_errors and no_errors both true, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the complete batch with AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD (RT-AI-0204), SHALL treat the contradictory case as invalid input and not accommodate it, and SHALL exit with code 6.
10. WHEN a Provider_Response contains Proposals, THE AI_Tool SHALL complete the provenance verification of criterion 4 for every Proposal of every kind, including the advisory kinds, before it writes any file, and IF the caller supplied no Source_Model for the document identifier of a Proposal, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL reject the complete batch with AI_PROPOSAL_SPAN_UNRESOLVED (RT-PRV-0101) and exit with code 3.
11. IF a Protocol_Failure occurs, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger and the Rulebook byte-identical to their state before the invocation, and THE AI_Tool SHALL, in every outcome, append no record to the Approval_Ledger and store no Reviewer identity.

### Requirement 31: Thin Demonstration Application Layers over the Shared Engine

**User Story:** As a project owner, I want Prize_Check and Bracket_Check to run as thin layers over the one shared Rules_Engine, so that I can demonstrate that one engine powers two different applications without a second implementation of any competition logic.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL implement Prize_Check and Bracket_Check each as a separate Application_Layer crate that depends only on the library interface of the Rules_Engine and on the crates that Requirement 24 criterion 12 allows, and that depends on neither the ruletrace binary crate nor any Auxiliary_Crate.
2. WHEN an Application_Layer receives a Compiled_Rulebook, an Application_Input_Format file of participants and values, the names of the Targets to evaluate, and optionally a published-result file and Source_Models, THE Application_Layer SHALL map the Application_Input_Format file to Competition_Data, build one Question per requested Target and participant or participant set, call the Rules_Engine once for the complete Question list, and render a report from the returned Outcomes and Decision_Traces, so that the Application_Layer contains input mapping, Question construction, and report layout only.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL deliver each Application_Layer as a library crate named prize-check or bracket-check with no binary target, and THE RuleTrace_Laboratory (ruletrace-lab) SHALL perform the reading of its named input files and the writing of its named report files within the roots of Requirement 37 criterion 13 and SHALL call the library of the Application_Layer for input mapping, Question construction, and report presentation (UD-33, UD-38). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
4. THE Application_Layer SHALL read every eligibility condition, weight, threshold, rounding mode, tie-breaking criterion, and precedence only from the Compiled_Rulebook, as verified by a metamorphic test that changes exactly one weight, one threshold, and the order of two tie-breaking criteria in the Rulebook, compiles each variant with a test Approval_Ledger that approves the variant, runs the unchanged Application_Layer, and asserts that the Outcomes equal those of `ruletrace evaluate` for the same variant and differ from the Outcomes of the unchanged Rulebook where the change is observable.
5. WHEN an Application_Layer evaluates a Compiled_Rulebook, THE Application_Layer SHALL write Outcomes and Decision_Traces whose serialized bytes are identical to the bytes that `ruletrace evaluate` writes for the same Compiled_Rulebook, the same Competition_Data (serialized by the canonical serializer from the Competition_Data that the Application_Layer mapped), the same Questions, the same Published_Result, and the same Source_Models, as verified for every Fixture of the Demo_Corpus and by a property test of at least 256 generated Competition_Data sets (Requirement 15 criterion 7).
6. THE Prize_Check Application_Layer SHALL report for each participant the eligibility Outcome, the weighted score as an exact value, the Rank_Position or the Tie_Group, and each Indeterminate Outcome with the name of the missing input, and SHALL report the result of the published-result consistency check of Requirement 13 criterion 12 with each differing value, and the Prize_Check report SHALL contain no field other than values that the Outcomes and Decision_Traces provide, so that the Prize_Check report contains no prize, award, amount, or allocation of any prize to a participant.
7. WHEN a published-result file is supplied, THE Application_Layer SHALL map the file to a Published_Result, SHALL pass the Published_Result to the Rules_Engine, and SHALL render each difference that the Rules_Engine reports with the published value, the computed value, and the reference to the Decision_Trace entry, and SHALL apply no comparison, tolerance, or rounding of its own.
8. THE Application_Layer SHALL map a missing value in the Application_Input_Format file to a Missing_Value and SHALL map an explicit zero, false, or text value to that value, where an empty CSV cell, an absent JSON member, and a JSON null denote a Missing_Value, so that no Missing_Value becomes zero, false, or an empty text value (Requirement 11 criterion 5).
9. IF an Application_Input_Format file is not well formed, lacks a required column or member, contains two records with the same participant identifier, or contains a value that is not valid for the declared type of its field (including a numeric literal that has an exponent, is not a decimal literal under Requirement 8 criterion 27, or is read by a floating-point conversion), THEN THE Application_Layer SHALL return one Error Diagnostic per violation with the code APP_INPUT_MALFORMED, APP_INPUT_FIELD_MISSING, APP_INPUT_DUPLICATE_PARTICIPANT, or APP_INPUT_VALUE_INVALID respectively and an Input_Location with the file, the 1-based line and column, and the field name, SHALL call the Rules_Engine for no Question, and SHALL exit with code 2. IF a requested Target is not declared in the Compiled_Rulebook, or is declared with a Rule kind that the Application_Layer cannot report (for Prize_Check and Bracket_Check, a Target that is not an eligibility predicate, a derived numeric value, a weighted score, or a ranking), THEN THE Application_Layer SHALL return one Error Diagnostic with the code APP_TARGET_NOT_DECLARED or APP_TARGET_KIND_MISMATCH with a Cli_Location of the Target argument, SHALL call the Rules_Engine for no Question, and SHALL exit with code 2.
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL include an automated source scan of the Application_Layer crates and of the ruletrace-lab crate that fails with the crate, file, and line WHEN a source file contains a call or an operator that adds, subtracts, multiplies, divides, rounds, compares, or aggregates Exact_Number values, a sorting, ordering, minimum, maximum, or ranking function applied to participants, scores, or Outcomes, a tie-breaking rule, a literal weight, threshold, or rounding mode, or a field name of a specific competition, and SHALL include a negative test fixture for each of these violations that makes the scan fail with the expected file and line, so that no ranking, scoring, eligibility, or tie-breaking logic exists in an Application_Layer (Requirement 15 criterion 6). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
11. THE Application_Layer library crates SHALL perform no file, network, clock, random, or environment access and start no subprocess, and SHALL return byte-identical reports and the same result classification of criterion 12 whether or not ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, an AI_Configuration file, or a credential environment variable is present, as verified by the scan of criterion 10, the dependency check of Requirement 24 criterion 12, and a test under at least two values of the clock, the working directory, and the locale. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
12. THE Application_Layer SHALL return a result classification that ruletrace-lab maps to exactly these exit codes: 0 WHEN the report is complete and no Outcome is Indeterminate, 4 WHEN at least one Outcome is Indeterminate, 2 for invalid input (criterion 9, a contradictory diagnostic or lifecycle state under Requirement 22, a missing or unreadable input file, an unknown argument, a Source_Model that is missing for a document or whose hash differs, as Requirement 16 criterion 8 defines it), and 70 for an internal error, and each result classification SHALL equal the exit code that `ruletrace evaluate` returns for the same Compiled_Rulebook, Competition_Data, Questions, Published_Result, and Source_Models, and THE Application_Layer SHALL write no partial report as if the run had succeeded (Requirement 16 criteria 9 and 12). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
13. THE Application_Layer SHALL render each report deterministically with no timestamp, no random value, no absolute file path, and no environment-dependent value, in a human-readable form and in a machine-readable form whose field order and element order are identical across runs, and SHALL write the report entries in the order of the Outcomes that the Rules_Engine returned, SHALL order the members of a Tie_Group in ascending participant identifier order for serialization only, and SHALL mark that order as carrying no ranking meaning (Requirement 12 criterion 2).
14. WHEN Source_Models are supplied, THE Application_Layer SHALL call the Explainer for each Decision_Trace and SHALL render the verification status and the source text that the Explainer returns, and WHEN no Source_Model is supplied, THE Application_Layer SHALL state in the report that provenance was not verified and SHALL carry the Warning PROV_NOT_VERIFIED_NO_SOURCES, so that the Application_Layer never presents an Outcome as verified when the Explainer did not verify the Decision_Trace.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL include for each Application_Layer unit tests of the mapping (each error of criterion 9 with the exact code and Location, and each Missing_Value case of criterion 8), Fixtures with golden reports for eligibility, a weighted score, a ranking with a Tie_Group, an Indeterminate Outcome from a Missing_Value, a published result that equals the computed result, and a published result that differs, and an isolation test that runs Prize_Check and Bracket_Check in the same process over two different Compiled_Rulebooks in any order and asserts that the results of each equal its results when run alone (Requirement 20 criterion 6).

### Requirement 32: Bracket_Check Standings and Qualification

**User Story:** As a competition administrator, I want Bracket_Check to show standings, tie groups, and per-participant qualification conditions computed by the shared engine, so that I can see which participants meet a stated condition and why, without any bracket or seeding logic.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL report Standings for each requested ranking Target, listing each participant with its Rank_Position or its Tie_Group, listing each Tie_Group as one entry with its members, listing each Indeterminate position with the missing input, and listing for each ranking comparison the tie-breaking criteria applied in order and the compared values that the Decision_Trace records (Requirement 12 criterion 7), and THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL name this workflow Tournament standings and SHALL describe it as standings and tie groups only, with no bracket, seeding, or progression (Requirement 36 criterion 18). (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
2. THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL define qualification only as the Outcome of a Qualification_Predicate (Requirement 14 criterion 11) and SHALL report for each requested Qualification_Predicate and participant the Outcome true, false, or Indeterminate, with the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Source_Span, and the inputs read, so that a qualification Outcome is a per-participant Boolean condition and is not a place, slot, seed, or advancement.
3. WHERE the UD-32 option Rank_Reference is selected, THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL report the Outcome of a Qualification_Predicate that reads a Rank_Position exactly as the Rules_Engine returns it, and WHEN a Tie_Group straddles the threshold, THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL report every member of the group with the same Outcome and SHALL state that the number of participants with the Outcome true can exceed the numeric threshold N of the Rule.
4. WHERE the UD-32 option Threshold_Only is selected, THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL support Qualification_Predicates over weighted scores, derived numeric values, and fields only, and THE Bracket_Check demonstration SHALL report each rank-based qualification clause of the demonstration document as Unsupported under Requirement 14 criterion 13.
5. THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL write a report whose set of fields equals exactly the set that criteria 1 and 2 and Requirement 31 criteria 7, 13, and 14 define, and SHALL contain no pairing, round, bracket position, seed, slot, advancement, or elimination field, as verified by a test that compares the field set of every machine-readable report with the documented field set and fails on any additional field.
6. WHEN a published-standings file is supplied, THE Bracket_Check Application_Layer SHALL map each published participant position and each published tie to a Published_Result and SHALL report each difference that the Rules_Engine returns, including a published ordering that gives different positions to participants that the computed Outcome places in one Tie_Group (so that a tie that the published standings broke without a declared criterion is reported as a difference, and the Application_Layer applies no tie-breaking of its own).
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL include Fixtures with golden reports for Standings with a Tie_Group, Standings with an Indeterminate position, a Qualification_Predicate over a weighted score, a Tie_Group at the threshold of a Qualification_Predicate (WHERE the option Rank_Reference is selected, over the Rank_Position), a published standings file that equals the computed Standings, and a published standings file that breaks a computed tie.

### Requirement 33: Reproducible End-to-End Demonstration over Real Public Documents

**User Story:** As a project owner, I want a documented, reproducible demonstration that takes a real public competition rulebook through AI-assisted proposal, human approval, compilation, evaluation, and proof of every decision, so that anyone can verify that the result is correct without trusting an AI statement.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit a Demo_Corpus in the repository that contains at least one real, publicly available competition rulebook as a plain-text or Markdown Source_Document for the Prize_Check demonstration and at least one real, publicly available tournament regulation with standings and tie-breaking rules for the Bracket_Check demonstration (the same Source_Document MAY serve both demonstrations when it contains all required kinds of clauses), and SHALL contain no document that the user did not confirm under UD-34.
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit with each demonstration Source_Document a manifest entry that records the title, the publisher, the public address, the retrieval date as text supplied by the user, the SHA-256 hash of the committed file, the license or permission of use that allows the committed use and the transmission under Requirement 28 criterion 21, the UD-34 confirmation reference, and a coverage note that states which clauses of the document contain eligibility conditions, weighted criteria, and tie-breaking rules, and a test SHALL fail with the document identifier and the missing field WHEN any of these fields is missing, WHEN the recorded hash differs from the hash of the committed file, or WHEN no license or permission is recorded, in which case the document SHALL NOT be committed or sent to an AI_Provider (Requirement 28 criterion 11).
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL accept as a demonstration document only a document that meets all of these selection criteria, which the user confirms under UD-34: the document is publicly available; the document is available as, or manually convertible to, plain text or Markdown; the document contains eligibility conditions, weighted scoring criteria, and tie-breaking rules (for a Bracket_Check document, standings and tie-breaking rules); and the document has a clear license or permission of use that allows redistribution in the repository.
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL NOT assert in any requirement, Fixture, runbook, or report that a named document is suitable until the user has confirmed the document under UD-34, and SHALL NOT invent, extend, or paraphrase the text of a demonstration document, so that the committed Source_Document differs from the published text only by the conversion that criterion 5 records.
5.
