WHERE a demonstration document is published only as PDF or in another format outside Requirement 1, THE RuleTrace SHALL accept as the Source_Document only a plain-text or Markdown file that the user converted manually outside RuleTrace, and SHALL record in the manifest the original format, the SHA-256 hash of the original file, the conversion method, the person who converted the file, the conversion date as supplied text, and every known conversion difference such as dropped tables or figures, and SHALL treat the converted file as the only Source_Document, so that every Source_Span refers to the converted file and no statement claims that a Source_Span refers to the original PDF.
6. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit a Coverage_Statement for the Demo_Corpus that lists every Candidate_Clause that the ingestion produced from each demonstration document, in ascending order of start byte offset, and classifies each as exactly one of: Rule approved (with the Rule identifier and Revision_Number), Proposal rejected (with the reason text of the rejection record), Unsupported (with the Diagnostic code and the capability name), Ambiguity (with the Diagnostic code and the recorded interpretations), or Not a rule (with the reason), and a test SHALL fail with the clause identifier WHEN a Candidate_Clause is missing from the Coverage_Statement, appears twice, or is classified differently from the Diagnostics of the validation run of the Demo_Corpus, so that clauses that the First_Version_Support_Boundary does not support are shown in the demonstration output and are never hidden, removed, reworded, or approximated.
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL document the demonstration flow as these ordered steps, each with its command, its inputs, and its expected exit code: (1) ingest the demonstration Source_Documents with the --candidates option; (2) run ruletrace-ai propose on the Candidate_Clauses; (3) list and review the Proposals with ruletrace review list, approve, and reject using a caller-supplied Reviewer and time; (4) validate the Rulebook with the Approval_Ledger; (5) compile; (6) evaluate on the demonstration Competition_Data with the Source_Models and a Published_Result; (7) render the Decision_Traces and the Proof_Report through the Explainer; (8) run the Prize workflow and the Tournament standings workflow through the RuleTrace_Laboratory (Requirement 36 criterion 18; the machine-readable block gives the Laboratory command or test-harness command) on the same Compiled_Rulebook or on the Compiled_Rulebook of each demonstration document (the Demo_Runbook states which), and compare their Outcomes and Decision_Traces with the output of step 6 under Requirement 31 criterion 5. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit a Recorded_Provider_Script that holds, for every Provider_Request of step 2, the unmodified Provider_Response bytes that a real AI_Provider returned (captured by the mechanism that UD-36 selects), keyed by the Request_Identifier, together with the provider identifier, the protocol, the model identifier, the capture date as supplied text, and the SHA-256 hash of each response, and SHALL label a script entry that a person wrote or edited as authored and SHALL NOT describe an authored entry as provider output in any document. A test SHALL fail with the file and the Request_Identifier WHEN the hash of a script entry differs from the recorded hash, WHEN a credential-looking string appears in the script (Requirement 28 criterion 15), or WHEN the Mock_Provider is asked for a Request_Identifier that the script does not contain, in which case ruletrace-ai reports the Provider_Failure under Requirement 16 criterion 16.
9. WHEN the offline flow runs on the committed Demo_Corpus with the Mock_Provider that replays the Recorded_Provider_Script, THE RuleTrace SHALL produce every artifact of the flow (Source_Models, Candidate_Clauses, Proposal_Store, Rulebook, Approval_Ledger, Validation_Result, Compiled_Rulebook, Outcomes, Decision_Traces, Proof_Report, and the reports of Prize_Check and Bracket_Check) byte-identically to the committed golden files on every platform of Requirement 21 criterion 10, with no network access, no credential, no AI_Configuration of a real provider, and no clock value other than the times that the Demo_Runbook supplies as command arguments.
10. WHERE the user runs the live-provider step, THE RuleTrace SHALL run ruletrace-ai with an explicit AI_Configuration for a real Provider_Profile of the Responses protocol (the first is the Meta Model API, UD-37) and a Transmission_Consent that names each transmitted demonstration document, SHALL write the stored proposals only to a location outside the committed golden files, SHALL NOT replace the Recorded_Provider_Script or any golden file automatically, and SHALL require a deliberate, documented commit to change the Recorded_Provider_Script, with the new response hashes, so that the non-determinism of a live provider (R-12) never changes the offline reproduction.
11. THE RuleTrace SHALL generate every Approval_Record of the Demo_Corpus by running `ruletrace review approve` (and every rejection record by `ruletrace review reject`) with a Reviewer identity that is not an AI identity, and SHALL NOT write an Approval_Ledger by hand or by any other code path, and the Demo_Runbook SHALL state that the offline flow replays the recorded decisions of the demonstration Reviewer and does not make a new human decision.
12. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit a Demo_Runbook that states the prerequisites, the offline commands in a machine-readable block with their arguments and expected exit codes, the optional live-provider commands, the location of every golden file, which inputs are real and which are synthetic or scripted, and a statement of the limits of Requirement 34, and THE demonstration acceptance test SHALL execute the offline commands of the machine-readable block as written, so that the Demo_Runbook cannot differ from the executed flow.
13. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit realistic Competition_Data for the demonstration that has a manifest entry stating for each file whether the values are real published values or synthetic, SHALL label synthetic data as synthetic in the manifest, the Demo_Runbook, and the Proof_Report header, and SHALL include in the data at least one eligible participant, one ineligible participant, one participant with a Missing_Value that gives an Indeterminate Outcome, one Tie_Group, one Published_Result that equals the computed result, and one Published_Result with a deliberately seeded difference that the manifest labels as seeded (UD-35).
14. THE RuleTrace SHALL produce a Proof_Report with one section per decision that shows, in this order: the Question and the Outcome; each applied Rule with its identifier and Revision_Number; the Approval_Record of each Rule (Reviewer, approval time, Rule_Content_Hash); each Source_Span with the document identifier, the byte offsets, the line and column, and the exact source text that the Explainer rendered; each input value read with its name and exact value; each calculation step with its operation, operands, and exact result; each tie-breaking criterion applied with the compared values; each missing input; each considered Rule that did not apply with its reason; the verification status of the Decision_Trace; and the result of the published-result comparison, and THE Proof_Report SHALL be built only from the Decision_Trace, the Approval_Ledger, and the Explainer output, SHALL contain no text that an AI_Provider generated, and SHALL be byte-identical across runs.
15. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a demonstration acceptance test that runs the offline flow of criterion 9 from the committed Demo_Corpus, compares every produced artifact byte-for-byte with its golden file, asserts the expected exit code of every step, asserts that each Decision_Trace of the demonstration is marked as verified by the Explainer, asserts that the Proof_Report replays (each calculation step recomputed from its recorded operation and operands equals the recorded result, as Requirement 13 criterion 11 requires), asserts that the demonstration data contains the participants and results of criterion 13, asserts the Coverage_Statement of criterion 6 and the manifest checks of criteria 2 and 8, asserts that the dependency tree of the ruletrace binary still has no network client, provider library, or AI_Crate (Requirement 27 criterion 5), and on a failure SHALL report the name of the failing step and the golden file with the first differing byte offset.
16. THE demonstration acceptance test SHALL be a required test of the continuous integration pipeline and a condition for tagging a release (Requirement 20 criterion 26), and SHALL run with no network access and no credential.
17. THE RuleTrace SHALL include in the demonstration acceptance test a mutation check that changes, in turn, one byte of one demonstration Source_Document, one value of the demonstration Competition_Data, and one approved Rule_Content in the Rulebook, and asserts that the first change makes the Explainer report PROV_HASH_MISMATCH and mark the affected Decision_Traces as not verified, that the second change alters only the Outcomes that depend on the changed value and leaves every Outcome that does not depend on it byte-identical, and that the third change makes the Approval_Record non-matching so that validate reports APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED and the compile command exits with code 3 and writes no Compiled_Rulebook.
18. THE RuleTrace SHALL run the demonstration of criteria 7 and 9 through the RuleTrace_Laboratory in demo mode (Requirement 36 criterion 20) with the Recorded_Provider_Script, so that every artifact that the Laboratory writes equals the golden file of criterion 9, and THE Demo_Runbook SHALL state the commands that build and start the Laboratory, the steps that a user performs in the browser, and which Ledger_Records are replayed recorded decisions. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
19. THE demonstration acceptance test of criterion 15 SHALL include a run that drives the HTTP interface of ruletrace-lab (Requirement 36 criterion 24) and SHALL compare the artifacts of that run with the same golden files. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

### Requirement 34: Honest Claims and Stated Limits of Demonstration Material

**User Story:** As a project owner, I want every demonstration document and report to claim only what RuleTrace supports and to state the limits of its guarantees, so that no reader mistakes the demonstration for universal rulebook support or for proof of fidelity to the regulation.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in every demonstration document, in the Demo_Runbook, in the user-facing documentation, and in the header of the Proof_Report and of each application report only capabilities that lie within the First_Version_Support_Boundary, and SHALL NOT claim support for arbitrary or universal rulebooks, PDF ingestion, prize allocation, slot-based qualification, seeding, bracket generation, tournament progression, or multi-stage competitions, and SHALL NOT describe the Prize_Check and Bracket_Check Workflow_Modules as the full Prize Check and Bracket Check applications, and SHALL NOT describe the RuleTrace_Laboratory as the source of any decision or approval. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
2. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook and in the header of the Proof_Report that every Rule is executable only after a human Reviewer approved the exact Rule_Revision through `ruletrace review`, that a Proposal of an AI_Provider is untrusted until approval, and that the offline demonstration replays recorded Reviewer decisions.
3. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook and in the header of the Proof_Report that validation checks the internal consistency of the Rulebook and does not check that the Rulebook matches the meaning of the regulation, that human approval is the only establishment of fidelity, and which classes of Conflict validation cannot detect (Requirement 6 criterion 22, R-3, R-5).
4. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook and in the header of the Proof_Report that the term verified means that every Source_Span resolves against a Source_Model whose hash equals the recorded hash, and that verified does not mean that the Rule is a correct interpretation of the source text.
5. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook that a live AI_Provider can return different Proposals for the same request (R-12), that the Recorded_Provider_Script is one recording with the stated provider identifier, protocol and model identifier, and that the Outcomes of the demonstration do not depend on any AI-generated explanation.
6. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook and in the Proof_Report header the number and the identifiers of the clauses of the demonstration documents that the Coverage_Statement classifies as Unsupported or Ambiguity, and SHALL state the number zero when none exist, and SHALL state that synthetic data is synthetic (Requirement 33 criterion 13).
7. THE RuleTrace SHALL keep, in the repository, a list of required statements with an identifier for each statement of criteria 2 to 6 and a list of prohibited claim phrases, and a documentation scan SHALL fail with the file, the line, and the statement identifier or phrase WHEN a demonstration document, the Demo_Runbook, the Proof_Report header, or an application report header lacks a required statement or contains a prohibited claim phrase.
8. THE RuleTrace SHALL include in the user-facing document of Requirement 14 criterion 8 the limits of the Application_Layers stated in Requirement 31 and Requirement 32 and the UD-32 option that applies, and SHALL update that document before each release is tagged.
9. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook, in the user-facing documentation, and on the first screen of the RuleTrace_Laboratory that the Laboratory decides nothing and approves nothing by itself, that every Outcome is the result of `ruletrace evaluate`, that every approval is a recorded human act through `ruletrace review`, and that every Proposal shown is untrusted AI output. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
10. THE RuleTrace SHALL state in the Demo_Runbook and in the user-facing documentation that the stage names Brain, Spine, Heart, Lightning, Ghost, and Autopsy name the components of Requirement 36 criterion 17, that the Prize workflow reports eligibility and ranking as separate Outcomes of separate Rules and that eligibility does not filter rankings, that Tournament standings are not brackets, seeding, or progression, and that a demo-mode run replays recorded provider output and recorded Reviewer decisions, and THE required-statement list and the prohibited-phrase list of criterion 7 SHALL include these statements and the phrases "AI approved", "AI decided", "automatically approved", and "bracket generator". (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)

### Requirement 35: Provider Profiles, Capability Negotiation, Model Catalogue, and Proposal_Store Format Version 2

**User Story:** As an operator, I want each real provider described by a documented profile and by negotiated capabilities, with the credential sent only to its official host, so that a provider is used only for what it is known to support and no setting can silently send my credential or my documents elsewhere.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE AI_Adapter SHALL return, with every Provider_Response, provider metadata that consists of the Provider_Identity, the optional Reported_Metadata (model name and response identifier), and the optional usage (input, output, and total token counts, each optional), as a typed value that is built only through a constructor and that is marked non-exhaustive, so that a later field does not break an implementation, and the AI_Adapter crate SHALL contain no network code and no provider-specific type (Requirement 18 criterion 12).
2. THE AI_Adapter crate SHALL define the Capabilities of a provider, a three-valued answer for each Capability (unknown, yes, no), the StructuredOutput kinds, and a pure function `negotiate` that takes the required Capabilities and the available Capabilities and returns every unmet requirement in the ascending byte order of the Capability name, with no clock, no file, no environment value, and no network access.
3. THE AI_Tool SHALL require, for propose, the Capability of strict JSON Schema structured output and, WHERE a reasoning effort is configured, the Capability of a reasoning effort control, and IF `negotiate` returns an unmet requirement (the value no or the value unknown), THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED (RT-AI-0011) naming the Capability and its value, with the Input_Location of the field that caused the requirement, SHALL read no credential, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2, so that an unknown required Capability fails closed.
4. THE AI_Tool SHALL take the available Capabilities from the Provider_Profile, and the optional capability declarations of the AI_Configuration SHALL only restate a value or lower it from yes to no (a change from unknown to no, and a change from yes to unknown, are refused like a raise), and IF a declaration raises a value that the Provider_Profile gives as no or unknown, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INVALID with the Input_Location of the declaration and exit with code 2.
5. THE AI_Tool crate SHALL hold each Provider_Profile as documented constants and data, and each Provider_Profile SHALL state the provider identifier, the protocol, the official endpoint host, the default Capabilities, the accepted reasoning effort values, the `max_output_tokens` constant, and the documentation URLs with the date on which each fact was read, and no Provider_Profile SHALL contain network code, credential code, or a model name that the code selects. The provider identifiers meta and openai and the mock identifier mock-replay are the only identifiers of this version.
6. WHEN the provider identifier is meta or openai, THE AI_Tool SHALL require the host of the endpoint to be exactly `https://` followed by the official host of the Provider_Profile (api.meta.ai for meta, api.openai.com for openai), compared after ASCII lower-casing, with no port (the default port 443 included, because the endpoint parser does not normalize it), no path other than empty or a single `/`, and no fragment, and a lookalike host, a host with a different suffix or a trailing dot, a port, a path, a fragment and any other host SHALL return AI_CONFIG_ENDPOINT_HOST_NOT_PERMITTED (RT-AI-0013) with the Input_Location of the endpoint field, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2, and the loopback exception of Requirement 28 criterion 6 SHALL remain the only other accepted host, so that the loopback stub server of the tests works (a loopback host may carry a port and a path) and the credential is never sent to an arbitrary host. An endpoint with a user name, a password or a query is refused earlier, by the inline-secret check, with AI_CONFIG_INLINE_SECRET (RT-AI-0003), in the order of Requirement 28 criterion 18.
7. THE AI_Tool SHALL offer no field, option, or environment setting that allows another endpoint host for the provider identifiers meta and openai, and a provider identifier that is not in criterion 5 (including an endpoint that claims to be compatible) SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN, because a custom compatible endpoint needs its own explicit trust decision that this version does not contain.
8. IF the model identifier contains the text `contributor` in any letter case and the AI_Configuration does not set allow_provider_training to true, THEN THE AI_Tool SHALL return AI_MODEL_TRAINING_MODE_REFUSED (RT-AI-0012) with the Input_Location of the model field, SHALL make no network call, and SHALL exit with code 2, for every provider identifier, and the AI_Configuration of the initial experiment (criterion 22) SHALL NOT set allow_provider_training.
9. THE AI_Tool SHALL accept an AI_Configuration of schema version 2, which holds a provider table (id, protocol, model, endpoint, credential_env_var, optional reasoning_effort, optional verify_model, optional allow_provider_training), a limits table, the shared fields of Requirement 28 criterion 2, and an optional capabilities table, and SHALL accept an AI_Configuration of schema version 1 unchanged, mapping the provider kind `openai-responses` to the provider identifier openai with the protocol `openai-responses` and the provider kind `mock-replay` to the provider identifier `mock-replay` with the protocol `mock-replay`, and the mapping SHALL be made in memory only, so that a run SHALL NOT create, change, rename, or delete the configuration file or any file beside it. Any other schema version SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INVALID.
10. THE AI_Tool SHALL validate the provider identifier, the protocol, the model identifier, and the credential environment variable name each by its own rule, and the pair of provider identifier and protocol SHALL be one of (meta, openai-responses), (openai, openai-responses), and (mock-replay, mock-replay), and any other pair SHALL return AI_PROVIDER_UNKNOWN, and the model identifier SHALL be 1 to 200 Unicode scalar values without control characters, and an invalid model identifier SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INVALID.
11. WHERE the AI_Configuration sets a reasoning effort, THE AI_Tool SHALL accept for the provider identifier meta only the values minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, and max, and the value max only WHEN the model identifier is one that the Provider_Profile lists for it (muse-spark-1.3, muse-ember-1.0, and muse-wonder-1.0), SHALL return AI_CONFIG_INVALID for the value none and for any other value, and SHALL return AI_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED for the provider identifier openai until a task verifies its Capability, and the reasoning effort SHALL be part of the canonical bytes of the Provider_Request only WHEN it is configured, so that a different effort gives a different Request_Identifier and a Provider_Request without an effort keeps the Request_Identifier of earlier versions. WHERE no reasoning effort is configured, the request SHALL omit the field and the tool SHALL NOT state which effort the provider applied.
12. THE AI_Integration_Crate for the Responses protocol SHALL send `POST {endpoint}/v1/responses` with the fields model, input, `text.format` of type json_schema with the name, the schema of Requirement 23 criterion 39, and strict true, `store` false (Requirement 28 criterion 13), no tools, and the `max_output_tokens` constant of the Provider_Profile, which SHALL be at least 16 and SHALL be documented with the statement that reasoning tokens count toward it, and `reasoning.effort` only WHERE criterion 11 configures it, and SHALL send no other field, and the credential SHALL be sent only in the Bearer authorization header.
13. THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL map a response with `status` completed, ignoring reasoning items, exactly one message item, and exactly one `output_text` to the payload, SHALL return AI_RESPONSE_MALFORMED (RT-AI-0201) for an envelope that is not valid JSON or has any other shape (including a tool call item, a second message, or a second `output_text`), and SHALL map a refusal, an incomplete status, or any status other than completed to the completion kinds Refused and Incomplete of the AI_Adapter (AI_RESPONSE_INCOMPLETE, RT-AI-0205).
14. THE AI_Integration_Crate SHALL classify an HTTP error response by its status and not by the provider's error `type` or `code`, with 401 and 403 as an authentication failure, 429 as rate-limited (with the Retry-After handling of Requirement 29 criterion 3), every 5xx status other than 504 as unavailable, HTTP 504 as a gateway timeout, and 3xx, 400, 402, and any other 4xx status as another provider error, so that HTTP 504 is not a Transient_Failure (Requirement 29 criterion 2) and a Retry-After header of an HTTP 503 response is treated as absent (Requirement 29 criterion 3). An HTTP 504 ends the request after the attempt that received it, with no waiting time and no second attempt however many retries are configured, with AI_PROVIDER_ERROR (RT-AI-0105) whose message names the HTTP status and none of the provider's text. The decision is OD-37-1 of `tools/decisions/UD-37.md`, resolved by the user on 2026-11-16.
15. THE AI_Tool SHALL treat Reported_Metadata and usage as untrusted audit data, SHALL record them only in their own members, SHALL NOT use them for the Request_Identifier, the AI identities, the Reviewer check, any approval, or any Diagnostic decision, SHALL keep the configured model identifier separate from the reported model name, SHALL record a usage count only WHEN the provider reports it as a non-negative integer and otherwise record it as absent, SHALL NOT derive, sum, or estimate a count, and SHALL compute and state no cost. A reported value of more than 256 Unicode scalar values or with a control or format character (Unicode category Cc or Cf, which includes the bidi controls) SHALL be omitted. The redaction of Requirement 28 criterion 9 SHALL apply to every reported value.
16. THE AI_Tool SHALL write every new batch of the Proposal_Store as format version 2, whose members are those of version 1 (with `provider` the provider identifier, `model` the configured model identifier, and the Provider_Identity adapter version) and the optional members `protocol`, `reported_model`, `response_id`, and `usage` (an object whose members `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, and `total_tokens` are each optional), and an optional member SHALL be omitted, never written as null, and the canonical serialization of the Proposal_Store batch SHALL apply.
17. THE Proposal_Store reader of the AI_Tool, of the review library, and of the script export SHALL accept batches of format version 1 and 2 in one Proposal_Store, SHALL read a version 1 batch with the optional members absent and its `provider` member as written (in version 1 it holds the provider kind), SHALL NOT rewrite any existing batch, SHALL count a batch of either version for the idempotency of Requirement 29 criterion 10, and SHALL treat any other version, an unknown member, and a member of the wrong type as a malformed Proposal_Store (AI_STORE_INVALID, RT-AI-0302, or REVIEW_PROPOSAL_STORE_INVALID, RT-APR-0213).
18. THE ruletrace-ai executable SHALL provide the command `models --config <file>`, which SHALL load the AI_Configuration under the checks of Requirement 28 criterion 17 up to the credential (except the capability negotiation and the consent, which concern a Provider_Request), send `GET {endpoint}/v1/models` with the credential in the authorization header only, include no Source_Document text and no Candidate_Clause, need no Transmission_Consent, append one Transmission_Log entry before the send that names the provider identifier, the protocol, the configured model, the endpoint host, the request kind `models`, and no document, write the model identifiers to standard output one per line in ascending byte order through the control-character escape, write no other file, and SHALL NOT select, change, or write a model into any file. The call policy of Requirement 29 criteria 1 to 6 and 8 SHALL apply, and a failure SHALL return AI_MODEL_DISCOVERY_FAILED (RT-AI-0109) naming the failure kind and exit with code 5.
19. WHERE verify_model is true, THE AI_Tool SHALL query the Model_Catalogue once per propose run, after the credential check and before the first Provider_Request, SHALL return AI_MODEL_DISCOVERY_FAILED for a failed query and AI_MODEL_NOT_IN_CATALOGUE (RT-AI-0110) WHEN the configured model is not in the catalogue, in both cases with exit code 5, no Provider_Request sent, and the Proposal_Store unchanged, and WHERE verify_model is false or absent, SHALL make no catalogue query.
20. THE AI_Tool SHALL send every Provider_Request of a run with the configured model identifier, SHALL NOT replace the model after a failure, SHALL NOT keep a list of fallback models, and SHALL NOT choose a model from a Model_Catalogue.
21. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests that assert the exact Diagnostic code, the Severity, the Location, the exit code, and that no network call occurred, for: every row of the truth table of `negotiate` (including unknown), criterion 3, the declaration rule of criterion 4, a table of hosts for criterion 6 (the official host in two letter cases, a lookalike suffix, a host with a user name, a trailing dot, an IP literal, and loopback), criterion 7, the model guard of criterion 8 (letter cases, the Contributor model identifier, and allow_provider_training true), the version 1 mapping of criterion 9 with the byte-level state of the configuration file and its directory, the pair table of criterion 10, the effort values of criterion 11 and the Request_Identifier with and without an effort, the request body of criterion 12 against the stub server, the response and error mapping of criteria 13 and 14 (including 401, 402, 429, 503 with Retry-After, 504, and 400), criteria 15 to 17 (a version 1 batch read, a version 2 batch written, an unknown version, usage absent and present, and the canary credential in a reported value), and criteria 18 to 20 (the request bytes of `models` contain no document text, no consent is needed, the Transmission_Log entry has no document, and the output order).
22. WHERE the initial experiment with the Meta Model API runs, THE RuleTrace SHALL use only public competition documents and synthetic data (Requirement 28 criterion 11), the provider identifier meta, the model identifier muse-spark-1.3, an explicit low reasoning effort, and no allow_provider_training, and MAY compare the model identifier muse-ember-1.0 on one identical, small benchmark of public clauses, and no agent SHALL request, print, or log the credential of the user, who runs the `models` command and the live run locally.
23. THE AI_Integration_Crate for the Responses protocol SHALL report the adapter version `responses-1/prompt-N`, where N is the prompt profile, the number of the current wording of the constant text that precedes the canonical request in `input` and of the `description` members of the Proposal schema, and SHALL change N whenever either text changes, so that the adapter version recorded in a stored batch (criterion 16) shows which wording produced it. Every sentence of that constant text and of those descriptions SHALL state a rule that the local parser, the provenance check or the semantic validator enforces (for the type sentences of profile 3: the Rule_IR type names, RT-VAL-0001 and RT-VAL-0003), and a test SHALL assert the behavior of each sentence through the real acceptance path. Profile 3 (`responses-1/prompt-3`) adds the rules that a rule type is the type its kind yields (eligibility boolean, derived and weighted_score number, ranking ranking, default the type of its target input), that a type is written only as number, boolean, text, date, ranking or enum:<Name>, that the kind and not the expression decides the type, and that a clause whose kind and type cannot be decided is stored as kind unrecognized. A stored batch keeps the adapter version that produced it and is never relabelled. Neither text SHALL be part of the canonical bytes of the Provider_Request, so a change of wording SHALL NOT change a Request_Identifier, and the constant text SHALL contain no text derived from the request.

### Requirement 36: RuleTrace Laboratory

**User Story:** As a competition administrator or reviewer, I want one local application that shows AI proposals, human approval, exact deterministic execution, and verified proof in one workflow, so that I can use RuleTrace without the command line and without trusting any AI statement.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE RuleTrace SHALL deliver the RuleTrace_Laboratory as the executable ruletrace-lab, which serves a browser interface on the loopback interface only (Requirement 37), and THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL be the only user-interface executable of the RuleTrace (UD-38).
2. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL perform every authoritative operation (ingestion, candidate extraction, proposal generation, validation, compilation, evaluation, review listing, review approval, review rejection, and provenance verification) only by starting a ruletrace or ruletrace-ai subprocess, and SHALL perform none of these operations in its own process, as verified by a test that replaces each of the two executables with a recording stand-in and asserts that each such operation requested through the interface reaches exactly one stand-in invocation and no other invocation.
3. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL start each subprocess with an argument vector that contains one element per argument, SHALL construct no shell command string, and SHALL start no executable other than ruletrace and ruletrace-ai, as verified by a source scan that fails with the file and line on a shell invocation and by a test that records every started executable path.
4. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL write neither the Approval_Ledger nor the Rulebook from its own process, and SHALL change the bytes of either file only through a ruletrace review approve or ruletrace review reject subprocess, as verified by a test that hashes both files before and after every Laboratory operation and asserts that a change occurs only after an approval or rejection request that passed the checks of criterion 6 and Requirement 37 criterion 19.
5. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL compute no Outcome, score, Rank_Position, Tie_Group, eligibility result, difference to a Published_Result, or comparison of Exact_Number values, and SHALL display each of these values as the bytes that `ruletrace evaluate` returned, as verified by a source scan of the ruletrace-lab crate (Requirement 31 criterion 10 applied to ruletrace-lab) and by a test that asserts the machine-readable result of the Laboratory run equals, byte for byte, the output of `ruletrace evaluate` for the same Compiled_Rulebook, Competition_Data, Questions, Published_Result, and Source_Models.
6. WHEN a user requests the approval of a Rule_Revision, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL display the Rule identifier, the Revision_Number, the Rule_Content_Hash, the Source_Spans, and the Diagnostics of that Rule_Revision, SHALL require a non-empty Reviewer identity entered by the user, and SHALL start `ruletrace review approve` only after the user submitted a confirmation that names the same Rule identifier, Revision_Number, and Rule_Content_Hash that the Laboratory displayed, and SHALL pass the Reviewer identity and these three values to the subprocess as four separate arguments, starting exactly one subprocess per submitted confirmation.
7. IF the Reviewer identity is empty, matches an AI identity under the check of UD-23, or the confirmation names a Rule identifier, Revision_Number, or Rule_Content_Hash that differs from the displayed values or from the values that ruletrace review reads, THEN THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL display the Diagnostic that the refusal produced (its own input Diagnostic when it refused before starting a subprocess, otherwise the one that `ruletrace review` returned), SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger byte-identical, and SHALL present the Rule_Revision as not approved.
8. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL start `ruletrace review approve` and `ruletrace review reject` only in response to a request that a human initiated in the browser interface, and SHALL NOT approve or reject a Rule_Revision upon the arrival, storage, or validation of a Proposal, upon a timer, at the end of a proposal run, upon loading a Demo_Corpus, in demo mode, or upon any other event, as verified by a test that runs a complete proposal run in demo mode and asserts that the Approval_Ledger is byte-identical to its state before the run.
9. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL exchange data between ruletrace and ruletrace-ai subprocesses only as files in the working directory of the Laboratory_Session, SHALL record the SHA-256 hash of each Hand_Off_Artifact when a subprocess produced it, and SHALL verify that hash immediately before it passes the Hand_Off_Artifact to the next subprocess, and IF the hash differs, THEN THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL start no further subprocess for the workflow, SHALL display the file and both hashes, and SHALL change no Approval_Ledger.
10. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL present every Proposal and every field that originates from a Provider_Response as untrusted, SHALL NOT treat a valid hash of a Hand_Off_Artifact as approval or as verification of its content, and SHALL pass no Proposal to compilation or evaluation except as a Rule_Revision that the Approval_Ledger approves under Requirement 26.
11. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show in the Brain stage each Pending_Proposal with the label untrusted, with its Origin metadata (provider identifier, protocol, model identifier, and token usage where the Proposal_Store records them), and with each validation Diagnostic (code, Severity, message, and Location) exactly as `ruletrace validate` and `ruletrace-ai` returned it, and SHALL show no Rule_Revision as approved in the Brain stage.
12. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show in the Spine stage the Approval_Status of each Rule_Revision, each Ledger_Record with its Reviewer, time, and Rule_Content_Hash, and the hash-chain verification result, each exactly as `ruletrace review list` and `ruletrace validate` report it, and SHALL derive no Approval_Status in its own process.
13. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show in the Heart stage the result of `ruletrace compile` (the Compiled_Rulebook identifier, or each blocking Diagnostic) and each numeric value as the canonical exact decimal or rational text that the Rules_Engine returned, and SHALL convert no Exact_Number to a floating-point value, as verified by a source scan of the ruletrace-lab crate that fails on any floating-point type or parse.
14. WHILE no `ruletrace evaluate` subprocess has completed with exit code 0 or 4 for the displayed Compiled_Rulebook, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show the Lightning stage as inactive, and WHEN such a subprocess completes, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show the Lightning stage as active, bound to the identifier of that Compiled_Rulebook and that evaluation's result, and SHALL return it to inactive when a different Compiled_Rulebook is displayed, so that an AI response, a Proposal storage, an approval, a validation, and a compilation never activate the Lightning stage, as verified by a test for each of these events.
15. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show in the Ghost stage each Missing_Value input name and each Indeterminate Outcome with the missing input exactly as the Decision_Trace records it, and SHALL substitute no zero, false, empty text, or other default for a Missing_Value in any display.
16. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL show in the Autopsy stage only the source text, the Source_Spans, the calculation steps, the Approval_Records, and the verification status that the Explainer and the Proof_Report of `ruletrace evaluate` returned, SHALL show an Outcome whose Decision_Trace is not verified as not verified together with the Diagnostic code, and SHALL show no text that an AI_Provider generated in the Autopsy stage.
17. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL label each of the six stages with a caption that names the RuleTrace component and the data that the stage displays (Brain: untrusted Proposals and validation findings; Spine: human approval and the Approval_Ledger; Heart: compilation and exact deterministic arithmetic; Lightning: execution of the approved Compiled_Rulebook; Ghost: missing data and Indeterminate Outcomes; Autopsy: Proof_Report, calculation steps, and verified source provenance), SHALL bind each stage to the result of the subprocess that the caption names, and SHALL contain no animation, symbol, or label that is not bound to such a result, as verified by a test that asserts the caption text and the binding of each of the six stages.
18. WHEN a user selects the Prize workflow or the Tournament standings workflow, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL use the Prize_Check or the Bracket_Check Workflow_Module respectively for input mapping, Question construction, and report presentation over one Compiled_Rulebook, SHALL pass the mapped inputs and Questions unchanged to a `ruletrace evaluate` subprocess, SHALL obtain every Outcome from that subprocess, SHALL name the workflows Prize workflow and Tournament standings workflow in the interface, and SHALL show no pairing, round, bracket position, seed, slot, advancement, elimination, or prize allocation (Requirement 32 criterion 5).
19. WHERE no AI_Configuration is supplied and the ruletrace-ai executable is absent, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL provide the workflow from a Rule_IR file and an Approval_Ledger file through validation, compilation, evaluation, and proof, and SHALL produce the same Outcomes, Decision_Traces, and Proof_Report bytes as the corresponding ruletrace commands, so that the manual-rules path needs no AI component, no credential, and no network access.
20. WHERE demo mode is selected, THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL run the proposal step through `ruletrace-ai` with the Mock_Provider that replays the Recorded_Provider_Script, SHALL need no network access, no credential, and no AI_Configuration of a real provider, SHALL label every demo-mode result as a recorded replay with the provider identifier, the protocol, the model identifier, and the capture date of the script, and SHALL present each Ledger_Record loaded from the Demo_Corpus as a replayed recorded Reviewer decision and not as a new human decision (Requirement 33 criterion 11).
21. IF a subprocess exits with a nonzero exit code, exceeds a limit of Requirement 37 criterion 14, or produces output that is not well formed for its documented format, THEN THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL display the exit code and each Diagnostic that the subprocess wrote to standard error, escaped under Requirement 37 criterion 16, SHALL display no success state for the affected stage, SHALL start no further subprocess for the affected workflow step, and SHALL treat an exit code outside the set of the executable (Requirement 27 criterion 10) as an internal error.
22. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL return exit code 0 after an orderly shutdown, 2 for an invalid command-line argument, an invalid configuration, or a refused listener address (Requirement 37 criteria 1 and 21), and 70 for an internal error, with no other exit code in any test run, and WHEN ruletrace-lab runs or is absent, THE ruletrace and ruletrace-ai executables SHALL produce byte-identical standard output, standard error, output files, and exit codes for the same inputs, and THE dependency trees of ruletrace and ruletrace-ai SHALL contain no ruletrace-lab crate.
23. THE RuleTrace SHALL commit reproducible build and run instructions for the RuleTrace_Laboratory (the pinned toolchain, the build command, the command that starts demo mode without a credential, the manner in which the user obtains the Launch_Secret, and the loopback address and port that the user opens in a browser), SHALL NOT rely on a hosted address, and SHALL include a test that executes each command of the instructions as written in a clean checkout and fails with the command and the first differing result.
24. THE RuleTrace SHALL include a Laboratory acceptance test that drives the HTTP interface of a running ruletrace-lab against the real ruletrace and ruletrace-ai executables in demo mode through proposal, review approval by a human-initiated request, validation, compilation, evaluation, and proof for both workflows, and that asserts the exit code of each step, that each artifact equals the corresponding golden file (Requirement 33 criterion 19), and that the Approval_Ledger changed only by the approval request.

### Requirement 37: Laboratory Threat Model and Local Security

**User Story:** As a user who runs the Laboratory on a personal computer, I want the Laboratory to be unreachable and unusable by anything except me and to keep the AI credential and the approval ledger out of its reach, so that a web page, another local program, or a malicious document cannot approve a rule or steal a credential.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL bind its listener only to the IPv4 address 127.0.0.1 or the IPv6 address ::1, and IF a command-line argument or a configuration value names any other address (including 0.0.0.0, ::, a hostname, and a non-loopback interface address), THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL open no listener, SHALL write an Error Diagnostic with a Cli_Location of the argument, and SHALL exit with code 2.
2. WHILE the ruletrace-lab executable is running, THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL, as defence in depth in addition to the loopback bind of criterion 1, close without a response each accepted connection whose peer address is not a loopback address, as verified by a unit test of the peer-address check with a non-loopback address, because a real non-loopback connection cannot reach a loopback listener.
3. IF the Host header of a request is absent or is not byte-for-byte one of the literal forms `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `[::1]:<port>` (the form that matches the bound address, with the actual listener port, and the brackets for IPv6), THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 400 or 403, SHALL start no subprocess, and SHALL write no file, as verified by a table of Host values that includes a foreign name, the name `localhost`, a name that resolves to a loopback address, a different port, an absent port, a trailing dot, differing letter case, an unbracketed IPv6 form, and a duplicated Host header.
4. IF a request carries an Origin header whose value is not exactly `http://` followed by the Host form of criterion 3 of the listener, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 403, SHALL start no subprocess, and SHALL write no file. IF a state-changing request (criterion 9) carries no Origin header and no Sec-Fetch-Site header with the value `same-origin`, or carries a Sec-Fetch-Site header with another value, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse it with HTTP status 403, SHALL start no subprocess, and SHALL write no file. A GET, HEAD, or OPTIONS request without an Origin header SHALL NOT be refused on that ground.
5. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL generate a Launch_Secret of at least 128 bits from the operating-system source of cryptographic randomness at each start, SHALL print it once to the terminal (standard error or standard output) of the process that started ruletrace-lab, SHALL place it in no URL, query string, fragment, command-line argument, referrer, response body, file, or log, and SHALL accept it once, in the body of a POST request to the exchange endpoint, in exchange for a session cookie. The user SHALL enter the Launch_Secret by copying it from that terminal and pasting it into a form field on the Laboratory start page, which the executable serves without a session and which carries no secret.
6. WHEN a valid Launch_Secret is exchanged, THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL consume it, SHALL create a Laboratory_Session with a session identifier of at least 128 bits from the same source, and SHALL set the session identifier in a cookie with the attributes HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, and Path=/, and with no Domain attribute. The cookie SHALL NOT carry the Secure attribute, because the listener serves plain http on loopback and a browser would not return a Secure cookie. IF the Launch_Secret is presented a second time or is invalid, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse with HTTP status 401 and SHALL create no session. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL hold at most one Laboratory_Session at a time. Several browser tabs that carry the same valid session cookie belong to that one Laboratory_Session and SHALL be served, and THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL create no second, independent Laboratory_Session while one exists. (Clarified at the requirements checkpoint, 2026-11-18.)
7. IF a request other than the Launch_Secret exchange and the request for the start page of criterion 5 carries no valid session cookie, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 401, SHALL start no subprocess, SHALL read no workspace file, and SHALL write no file.
8. WHEN a Laboratory_Session has had no request for 30 minutes or the session is ended by the user, THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL invalidate the session identifier so that every later request that carries it receives HTTP status 401. A test with a simulated timer SHALL verify the 30-minute boundary (a request at 29 minutes 59 seconds succeeds, a request at 30 minutes 0 seconds receives 401).
9. IF a request that can change state (any request with the method POST, PUT, PATCH, or DELETE, and any request that starts a subprocess or writes a file) lacks a CSRF token bound to its Laboratory_Session, carries a token of another session, or carries the token in the URL, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 403, SHALL start no subprocess, and SHALL write no file. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL accept a CSRF token only in a request header or in the request body. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL start no subprocess and write no file in response to a request with the method GET, HEAD, or OPTIONS. The Launch_Secret exchange of criterion 5 is exempt from the CSRF token because no session exists yet, and remains subject to criteria 3 and 4.
10. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL send no Access-Control-Allow-* header or other CORS response header in any response, SHALL answer a preflight request (OPTIONS) with HTTP status 405, and SHALL send the headers Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, and X-Frame-Options: DENY, as verified by an HTTP-client test that reads the exact response header values. Browser-side enforcement is not part of this criterion.
11. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL send with every HTML response a Content-Security-Policy that sets default-src to 'none', sets script-src, style-src, img-src, font-src, and connect-src each to 'self' only, sets object-src to 'none', base-uri to 'none', form-action to 'self', and frame-ancestors to 'none', and contains neither 'unsafe-inline', 'unsafe-eval', a nonce, nor a hash source, so that no inline script, no inline style (including the style attribute and the style element), and no eval is permitted. A test that parses the header SHALL assert each directive and its exact value list, and a scan of every served HTML asset SHALL find no inline script, no inline style, and no inline event-handler attribute.
12. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL serve every script, style sheet, font, and image from assets embedded in the ruletrace-lab executable, SHALL reference no resource whose address has another origin than the listener (including scheme-relative addresses), and SHALL open no outbound network connection, as verified by a scan of every served asset and by a test in a network namespace that has only a loopback interface and records every connection attempt. The loopback connections of ruletrace-lab to its own listener are the only permitted connections.
13. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL read and write files only inside the workspace root named at start and the temporary directory of the Laboratory_Session, SHALL canonicalize each path from a request before use, and IF a path is outside these roots (by an absolute path, a parent-directory component, or a symbolic link whose target is outside these roots), THEN THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 400 and a Diagnostic, SHALL open no file at that path, and SHALL start no subprocess.
14. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL start each subprocess through an argument vector with no shell, SHALL pass each value from a request as exactly one argument element that follows an explicit end-of-options marker or that is part of an option of the form name=value, SHALL resolve the ruletrace and ruletrace-ai executable paths only from the directory of the ruletrace-lab executable or from a path named at start (never from the PATH variable or a request), SHALL pass an explicit allow-list of environment variables, SHALL limit the request body to 1 MiB (an oversized request is refused with HTTP status 413), the captured output of a subprocess to 16 MiB, and the run time of a subprocess to 300 seconds, and SHALL terminate the subprocess and display an error WHEN a limit is exceeded, as verified by tests with values that contain shell metacharacters, a leading hyphen, a newline, and a NUL byte (a value with a NUL byte is refused before any subprocess starts). WHEN a subprocess exceeds the output limit or the run-time limit, THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL terminate it, SHALL wait for its exit so that no child process remains, SHALL display a Diagnostic that names the limit and the workflow step, SHALL display none of the captured output as if it were complete (no silent truncation), SHALL use no output file of that step, and SHALL leave no temporary file of that step in the session directory after the session ends. The 1 MiB limit applies to ordinary control requests; file uploads have the separate limits of criterion 26. (Clarified at the requirements checkpoint, 2026-11-18.)
15. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL validate every request parameter against a declared type, length, and character set before use, SHALL refuse an unknown parameter and a malformed body with HTTP status 400 without a side effect, and SHALL accept a Reviewer identity only as text of 1 to 128 characters without control characters (Unicode general category Cc).
16. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL escape for its output context every value that originates from a subprocess, a Source_Document, a Proposal, a file name, or a request parameter before it inserts the value into a response, SHALL serve data responses with a declared content type, and SHALL insert no such value as markup or script, as verified by test inputs that contain script elements, attribute-breaking quotes, closing tags, and javascript addresses in each displayed field.
17. WHEN the user ends the session, WHEN the ruletrace-lab process receives an interrupt or terminate signal, or WHEN the process starts an orderly shutdown, THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL close the listener, SHALL invalidate every session, SHALL terminate each running subprocess, SHALL delete the temporary directory of the Laboratory_Session, and SHALL exit with code 0.
18. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL create the temporary directory of a Laboratory_Session with an unpredictable name of at least 128 bits and with permissions that allow only the owning user (mode 0700 on a POSIX system), SHALL write into it no credential, Launch_Secret, session identifier, or CSRF token, and WHEN it starts, SHALL delete a temporary directory of an earlier Laboratory_Session that a crash left behind and that carries the marker of the Laboratory, and SHALL delete no other directory.
19. IF an approval or rejection request lacks a valid session cookie, lacks a valid CSRF token, fails the Host or Origin check, lacks a Reviewer identity, or lacks the confirmation of the exact Rule identifier, Revision_Number, and Rule_Content_Hash (Requirement 36 criterion 6), THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request, SHALL start no ruletrace review subprocess, and SHALL leave the Approval_Ledger byte-identical, as verified by a test that omits each of these elements in turn.
20. THE RuleTrace_Laboratory SHALL NOT contain the AI provider credential in its process environment, in any argument vector, in any file that it writes, in any log line, or in any HTTP response, and THE RuleTrace SHALL include a test that supplies a canary credential through the mechanism of criterion 21, runs a complete proposal workflow against a loopback stub provider that records the Authorization header, and asserts that the stub received the canary from ruletrace-ai and that the canary appears nowhere in the environment of the ruletrace-lab process, the files of the workspace and the session directory, the standard output and standard error of ruletrace-lab, and the bodies and headers of every HTTP response.
21. THE RuleTrace SHALL record in a Laboratory threat model, before the implementation of ruletrace-lab starts, the mechanism by which ruletrace-ai receives its credential when the RuleTrace_Laboratory starts it, and SHALL select only a mechanism under which the credential is not reachable by the ruletrace-lab process. Whether that mechanism requires a change to Requirement 28 (credential only from the environment variable named in the AI_Configuration) is OPEN for the L1 threat model and needs an explicit user decision before any change to Requirement 28. IF the AI_Configuration that a user names names a credential environment variable that is present in the environment of the ruletrace-lab process, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL start no subprocess, SHALL write an Error Diagnostic that names the variable and not its value, and SHALL exit with code 2.
22. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL log for each request only the method, the path without the query string, the status, and for each subprocess the executable name, the argument names without values that originate from a document, and the exit code, and SHALL log no document text, Proposal text, credential, Launch_Secret, session identifier, or CSRF token.
23. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of criteria 1 to 22 and 24 to 26 that assert the exact HTTP status, the exact header values, the exit code, and the byte-level state of the Approval_Ledger and of the workspace files, and SHALL include for each of criteria 3, 4, 7, 9, 13, 14, 19, and 20 at least one negative test that fails when the check is removed, demonstrated by a mutant that removes the check.
24. IF a request carries a Cookie header with more than one session cookie, or a session identifier that does not match the single current Laboratory_Session, THEN THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL refuse the request with HTTP status 401, SHALL start no subprocess, and SHALL write no file.
25. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL run at most one state-changing request (criterion 9) of a Laboratory_Session at a time, whichever tab sent it, SHALL refuse a state-changing request that arrives while another runs, or that was prepared from a state of the session older than the current one, without starting a subprocess, and WHEN it starts `ruletrace review approve` or `ruletrace review reject`, SHALL pass the Rule identifier, Revision_Number, and Rule_Content_Hash of the confirmation of Requirement 36 criterion 6, so that `ruletrace review` rechecks the exact revision and hash for every request regardless of the tab. THE RuleTrace SHALL include tests of two tabs that use the same session, of concurrent state-changing requests, of a stale Rule_Content_Hash, of a second decision on an already decided revision, and of an attempt to establish a second independent Laboratory_Session, each asserting the HTTP status, the Diagnostic, and the byte state of the Approval_Ledger. (Added at the requirements checkpoint, 2026-11-18.)
26. THE ruletrace-lab executable SHALL accept a file upload only through an upload request that names the kind of the file, SHALL apply to each upload an explicit maximum size for that kind that the design states and that is not greater than the size limit of that kind of input in Requirement 19 and the design limits, SHALL apply an explicit maximum to the total size of the uploads of one Laboratory_Session, SHALL refuse an upload above a maximum with HTTP status 413 before it reads more of the body than the maximum allows, SHALL accept an upload whose size equals the maximum, and SHALL accept no request body, upload or other, without a maximum size. (Added at the requirements checkpoint, 2026-11-18.)


## Risks and Unresolved Decisions


These notes are inputs to the design phase. Each item must be resolved or explicitly deferred in the design document.

### Unresolved Decisions

These notes are inputs to the design phase. Each entry has a status: Resolved-by-user, Resolved-at-requirements, Recommended-pending (a recommendation that awaits user confirmation), Open (needs a user decision), or Left-to-design (the design phase decides within the stated constraints). The date of every Resolved-by-user entry is 2026-11-14.

- **UD-1 Rule language form** (Left-to-design; decided by the design phase with user review; affects 3.2, 3.7, 14.2): The first version proposes a declarative, data-format rule language. The design must compare it with an expression sub-language embedded in the data format, and must record the decision. A general-purpose language remains excluded unless a compelling reason is recorded.
- **UD-2 Serialization format** (Left-to-design; decided by the design phase; affects 3.10, 3.11, 4.5, 8.16, 26.1, 26.20): The design must choose the data format of the Rule_IR and of the Approval_Ledger based on canonical output, exact-decimal support, and dependency cost. TOML is fixed only for the AI_Configuration (UD-18).
- **UD-3 Exact_Number representation** (preferred: bounded rational, pending design documentation; decided by the user; affects 3.6, 4.4, 8.4, 8.5, 8.7, 8.11, 8.13, 8.16, 8.19 to 8.27, 13.11, 13.12, 20.22, the Glossary entries Exact_Number and Exact_Number_Limits, and R-7): Preferred: bounded rational, pending design documentation. The user prefers a bounded rational with decimal Rule_IR literals and canonical serialization. The choice is not final until the design documents the complete comparison, the integer-width selection, the normalization, the overflow behavior, the rounding, and the serialization (8.19). The requirements keep both Option D (bounded decimal) and Option R (bounded rational) consistent and tag every dependent criterion with UD-3-dependent. See the Exact_Number decision note and the UD-3 impact table below. No final choice is made.
- **UD-4 Three-valued logic** (Resolved-at-requirements): Requirement 11 criterion 4 fixes the Kleene semantics. The design only documents the truth tables.
- **UD-5 Ambiguity and incomplete markers** (Left-to-design; affects 6.5, 6.6): The first version has no automatic ambiguity detection. The design must define how an author marks a Rule as ambiguous or incomplete in the Rule_IR.
- **UD-6 Approval_Record storage** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 5.x, 9.5, 25.x, 26.x): Approval_Records live in a separate, append-only, hash-chained Approval_Ledger (Requirement 26). The Approval_Status is derived from the ledger and stored nowhere else. The file format of the ledger remains with UD-2 and UD-27. The first version does not authenticate Reviewers (R-8).
- **UD-7 Conflict detection scope** (Resolved-at-requirements): Requirement 6 criteria 16 to 23 define the decidable subset. Undetectable classes are listed in Requirement 6 criterion 22 and in R-5.
- **UD-8 Ordering field and collection semantics** (Left-to-design; affects 10.4, 14.2).
- **UD-9 Size and depth limits** (Left-to-design; affects 19.3, 19.4, 19.6): The design must set default values for the limits in Requirement 19.
- **UD-10 Source format coverage** (Left-to-design; affects 1.1): The design must decide how Markdown elements map to Source_Model elements, including nested lists and tables.
- **UD-11 Contradictory diagnostic state handling** (Left-to-design; affects 22.7 to 22.12): The design must decide how derived flags in an input are detected, whether a single derived flag that agrees with the Diagnostic list is accepted and discarded or also rejected (22.9 rejects only disagreement, and 22.10 rejects both flags in all cases), how the API test of 22.8 works, and the location reported per input kind. The inputs now include the Approval_Ledger and the Proposal_Store.
- **UD-12 Defects in non-Approved Rules** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14 through approval decision 4; affects 5.5, 6.25, 25.13, 26.11, 26.12): A Rule_Revision in an Unresolved_Approval_State blocks compilation, and a Blocking_Diagnostic on a Proposed Rule_Revision blocks compilation. Rejected Rule_Revisions receive structural checks only and never block. The alternative of ignoring Proposed Rules is rejected.
- **UD-13 Execution-time conflict check** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 12.5, 12.11, 12.12, 16.23): Requirement 12 criterion 5 is retained as a defensive check. A runtime conflict that yields an Indeterminate Outcome uses the Indeterminate exit code 4 (Requirement 16 criterion 11) and never exit code 3. The Diagnostic is EXECUTION_CONFLICT_DETECTED (RT-DGN-0004).
- **UD-14 Exit-code classification** (Recommended-pending; decided by the user; affects 16.8, 16.10, 16.15 to 16.17, 16.19): Parse-level failures, an unrecognized IR_Version, Source_Model mismatches, and Approval_Ledger integrity failures give exit code 2. Blocking_Diagnostics of validate and compile, including APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, and refused review actions give exit code 3. ruletrace-ai uses the codes 0, 2, 3, 5, 6, and 70 with the stage order of criterion 17.
- **UD-15 CI pins** (Left-to-design; affects 21.2, 21.10).
- **UD-16 Rulebook document references** (Left-to-design; affects 3.12, 26.7).
- **UD-17 Real AI provider choice** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14, first provider amended by UD-37; affects 23.29, 28.x, 29.x): The OpenAI Responses protocol is the integration (the first real provider is the Meta Model API since UD-37, with OpenAI as the second profile), behind the provider-independent AI_Adapter, using structured JSON Schema outputs. The provider kind and the model are configuration values and not code. The initial demonstration uses public competition documents only (28.11). Document transmission is explicit and opt-in through a per-invocation Transmission_Consent (28.7) and a Transmission_Log (28.8).
- **UD-37 First real provider, crate, schema, store, configuration and capability decisions** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11; affects 23.27 to 23.31, 23.39, 28.2, 28.8, 28.12, 28.17, 29.2, 33.8, 33.10, 35.x; record `tools/decisions/UD-37.md`): amends UD-17. The first real provider is the Meta Model API over the Responses protocol, in one crate `rt-ai-responses` that also serves the OpenAI profile. Modal is deferred. Requirement 29 criterion 2 was first left unchanged. On 2026-11-16 the user resolved OD-37-1 with option B: HTTP 504 gateway_timeout is a final failure for every adapter.
- **UD-18 AI_Configuration mechanism** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 23.19, 23.21, 23.22, 28.x): An explicit TOML file named by an argument. The credential is referenced only by environment variable name. An inline credential or a key field is rejected as invalid input (28.4). AI is disabled by default and needs an explicit invocation of ruletrace-ai, with no auto-detection. This supersedes the earlier option of a credential file path.
- **UD-19 Timeout, retry, and size defaults** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14, confirmed; affects 23.17, 23.18, 29.x): Total deadline 60 seconds, per-attempt timeout 20 seconds, at most 2 retries, response limit 1 MiB, the fixed waiting times of 2 and 8 seconds, and the configurable ranges of 29.1 are confirmed as documented.
- **UD-20 Atomic or per-item rejection of a Provider_Response** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 23.10, 30.x): Protocol failures reject the complete batch. Semantic outcomes of well-formed proposals are stored with their Diagnostics.
- **UD-21 Proposal_Store and Proposal file layout** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 23.9, 23.13, 26.19, 26.28 to 26.31): Approval of a Proposal atomically adopts it into the active Rulebook as a new Approved Rule_Revision together with its Approval_Record, all or nothing (23.13, 26.19, 26.30). Pending_Proposals remain in the Proposal_Store, are separate from the Rulebook, and do not block compilation of the existing Rulebook (26.28). A Proposed Rule_Revision inside the Rulebook still blocks compilation with APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED (26.29, 5.5, 25.13). The alternative of appending Proposed revisions to the Rulebook at proposal time is rejected.
- **UD-22 Packaging** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; amended by UD-33; affects 5.12, 17.1, 18.13, 24.5, 27.x): Two RuleTrace executables, ruletrace and ruletrace-ai, plus (UD-33, Recommended-pending) two Demonstration_Executables. Superseded by UD-38: the runtime executable set is ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, and ruletrace-lab. Human approval is an explicit operation in the main workflow through the Review_Group, which supersedes the earlier separate Review_Tool. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **UD-23 Reviewer identity check** (Recommended-pending; affects 23.15, 26.17): A string comparison of the Reviewer with the identities recorded in the Origin metadata and the Proposal_Store. It does not authenticate a person (R-8).
- **UD-24 Proposal_Schema data format and version** (Left-to-design; affects 23.4).
- **UD-25 Clause_Context size policy** (Left-to-design; affects 23.32). The maximum number of Candidate_Clauses per Provider_Request, with the default 20 and the range 1 to 100 of 28.2, is confirmed by the user on 2026-11-14 (UD-28). The size policy of the Clause_Context itself remains with the design.
- **UD-26 Exit code of a stored batch that carries blocking Semantic_Outcomes** (Resolved-by-user, confirmed; affects 16.16, 30.3): A structurally valid and provenance-verified batch that is successfully stored, but has blocking semantic findings, exits 0 with the Warning AI_BATCH_HAS_BLOCKING_PROPOSALS (RT-AI-0305). Such Proposals remain Proposed and cannot be approved or compiled while blocking findings remain. Malformed responses, invalid provenance and Provider_Failures remain failures. Partial multi-request runs remain explicitly incomplete (RT-AI-0306). The alternative of a dedicated nonzero exit code is rejected. Record: tools/decisions/UD-26.md.
- **UD-27 Approval_Ledger file format and two-file atomic adoption** (Left-to-design; affects 26.1, 26.6, 26.19): The design chooses the format (with UD-2), the exact atomic mechanism for changing the Rulebook and the Approval_Ledger together, and the behavior of --create-ledger, within the constraints of Requirement 26.
- **UD-28 Numeric bounds** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14, confirmed; affects 25.1, 28.2, 28.3): The Revision_Number range 1 to 4,294,967,295, the maximum Candidate_Clauses per Provider_Request with the default 20, and its range 1 to 100 are confirmed.
- **UD-29 Incomplete multi-request run** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 23.35, 23.36, 23.37, 16.24, 18.17, 27.8, 29.17, 30.1, 30.11): Batches that earlier Provider_Requests of a run stored are kept when a later Provider_Request fails, and the run is marked incomplete in a Run_Summary with the Warning AI_RUN_INCOMPLETE (RT-AI-0306). The exit code is the code of the failing Provider_Request. The overall operation never claims that all requested Candidate_Clauses were processed.
- **UD-30 Empty-collection aggregation** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 8.15, 8.29 to 8.31): The count and the sum of an empty collection are 0. The minimum and the maximum of an empty collection are undefined and give an Indeterminate Outcome with the Error AGGREGATE_EMPTY_COLLECTION (RT-DGN-0005) when the result is required.
- **UD-31 Decision_Trace with no resolvable source references** (Resolved-by-user 2026-11-14; affects 13.17, 13.22, 13.23): A Decision_Trace with no Source_Spans, or whose every Source_Span fails to resolve, is marked not verified. The zero-Source_Span case carries the Warning PROV_TRACE_NO_SOURCE_SPANS (RT-PRV-0007).

- **UD-32 Rank-based qualification** (Confirmed by the user: Rank_Reference, with the constraints recorded in `tools/decisions/UD-32.md` and in the Supersession Record; originally decided by the user; affects 14.2, 14.5, 14.11 to 14.14, 31.x, 32.3, 32.4, 33.6): The five Rule kinds and the operations of 14.2 express qualification only as a Qualification_Predicate over fields, derived numeric values, and weighted scores (for example, a score at or above a threshold). They cannot express a condition on a Rank_Position (for example, the top four qualify), because a Rank_Position belongs to the Outcome of a ranking Rule and is not an operand. Option Threshold_Only: add nothing; a rank-based clause is Unsupported (14.13) and the demonstration says so. Option Rank_Reference (smallest addition, recommended if the chosen tournament document uses rank-based qualification): add exactly one expression operation, rank reference, which reads the Rank_Position of the participant under a declared ranking Target, with Tie_Group members sharing the position, an Indeterminate position giving an Indeterminate operand, a static cycle check (RANK_REFERENCE_CYCLE), and no truncation of a Tie_Group at the threshold (14.12). Recommendation: decide after the user confirms the tournament document (UD-34), because the need for the operation depends on that document. Both options keep every other criterion unchanged, and every dependent criterion is tagged UD-32-dependent. The confirmation adds two constraints: a rank reference is used only where an applicable competition rule declares it (14.12 g), and an External_Ranking_Value is a separate Competition_Data input with authority, edition and provenance (14.15 to 14.25). Open sub-point: the carrying form of that metadata in Competition_Data (14.25, RN-30).
- **UD-33 Packaging of the Application_Layers** (Recommended-pending; decided by the user; affects 27.1, 27.15, 31.3): Compared options: (A) a third RuleTrace executable with prize-check and bracket-check subcommands; (B) two separate small Demonstration_Executables, prize-check and bracket-check, each a thin binary over its library crate; (C) library crates only, with Cargo example binaries and tests as the only entry points. Option A couples both applications into one binary and one dependency tree, which weakens the demonstration that two different applications use one engine, and adds a third entry point to the RuleTrace executables. Option C leaves 27.1 unchanged but offers no ordinary command for a demonstration and for a reviewer. Option B keeps the applications independent, keeps ruletrace and ruletrace-ai unchanged (their dependency trees contain no Application_Layer, 24.12, 27.15), and costs one amendment of 27.1. Recommendation: Option B, applied in this revision: 27.1 now says exactly two RuleTrace executables plus exactly two Demonstration_Executables. If the user chooses Option C, 27.1 reverts to its earlier wording and 31.3 and 27.15 change accordingly. Superseded by UD-38 (2026-11-18): option B is replaced by library Workflow_Modules and the executable ruletrace-lab; the four binding constraints stay for the libraries. (Amended by UD-38, 2026-11-18.)
- **UD-34 Demonstration documents** (Confirmed by the user, see `tools/decisions/UD-34.md`, which names the two documents, their official URLs and the scope; snapshots, retrieval dates and hashes follow at snapshot creation; UD-32 is not decided by it and stays Open for gate 1.11, Rank_Reference recommended; affects 33.1 to 33.6): The user selects the real public competition rulebook and the public tournament regulation. Selection criteria: publicly available; plain text or Markdown, or manually convertible to it (PDF ingestion is out of scope, so a PDF needs a recorded manual conversion, 33.5); contains eligibility conditions, weighted scoring criteria, and tie-breaking rules (for the tournament document, standings and tie-breaking rules); clear license or permission of use that allows redistribution in the repository. Apart from the record `tools/decisions/UD-34.md`, no document is named or assumed suitable in this specification, and no document text is invented. The same document MAY serve both demonstrations.
- **UD-35 Demonstration Competition_Data and Published_Results** (Open; decided by the user; affects 33.13): Real competition results may not be public in a form that fits the Rulebook. Recommendation: use real published values where they exist and are licensed for use, and otherwise use synthetic data that the manifest, the Demo_Runbook, and the Proof_Report header label as synthetic, with one equal Published_Result and one Published_Result whose seeded difference is labeled as seeded. Synthetic data is never presented as real results (34.6).
- **UD-36 Capture of the recorded provider response** (Confirmed, option 1, see `tools/decisions/UD-36.md`; decided by the user; affects 27.3, 33.8, 33.10): The Proposal_Store holds Proposals and response hashes, and a rejected Provider_Response is never stored (30.1), so the unmodified bytes of an accepted real response are not available today. Options: (1) the Proposal_Store retains the accepted Provider_Response bytes of each stored batch, and a deterministic export writes them to a Recorded_Provider_Script; (2) ruletrace-ai gains an optional recording output file, which needs an amendment of 27.3 (the files that ruletrace-ai writes); (3) a recording step in the test-support code of the AI_Integration_Crate, outside the executables. Recommendation: option 1, because it adds no write target to ruletrace-ai, supports audit (R-12), and keeps the credential redaction of 28.9. The design records the choice. Until then, 33.8 requires the capture mechanism without fixing it.
- **UD-38 RuleTrace Laboratory, category Frankenstein** (Confirmed by the user 2026-11-18, see `tools/decisions/UD-38.md`; affects the Introduction, Scope, Glossary, 7.23, 24.12, 24.13, 27, 31.3, 31.10 to 31.12, 32.1, 33.7, 33.18, 33.19, 34.1, 34.9, 34.10, 36, 37): One integrated local application served by ruletrace-lab; runtime executables ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, ruletrace-lab; prize-check and bracket-check as library Workflow_Modules; process separation, credential isolation, loopback-only local web interface, and metaphors that name real behaviour. The credential mechanism, the session mechanism, and the HTTP server library are decided by the Laboratory threat model (task L1), not by this specification.

### Exact_Number decision note (UD-3)

| Property | Bounded decimal (recommended) | Rational |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Decimal literal such as 0.1 | Exact, stored as written | Exact, stored as 1/10 |
| Result of 1 / 3 | Needs a declared scale and rounding mode, so the result is a rounded decimal | Exact 1/3, no rounding |
| Printing a value | Always a finite decimal string | Needs a rounding rule for non-terminating values |
| Round trip through the data format | Lossless as a decimal string | Needs a fraction syntax in the format |
| Comparison | Exact, by value | Exact, by cross multiplication |
| Fit with money and scores | Direct, because published scores are decimals | Indirect |
| Growth of size | Bounded by the 38-digit coefficient and scale 28 | Unbounded numerator and denominator unless a limit is set |
| Overflow rule | Checked against 38 digits and scale 28 | Checked against a chosen limit on numerator and denominator |
| Rounding | Only where a Rule declares it (Requirement 8 criteria 13 and 14) | Needed at every output of a non-terminating value |

The following table compares the bounded decimal, the rational, and a hybrid on the six dimensions that the decision must weigh. The hybrid is a bounded decimal at the Rule_IR, serialization, and Outcome boundary, with exact rational evaluation inside one expression and a single rounding at the declared point.
