| Dimension | Bounded decimal (recommended) | Rational | Hybrid |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exact addition, subtraction, multiplication, division | Addition, subtraction, and multiplication are exact or Indeterminate. Division is exact only when the quotient terminates, so every division declares a scale and a rounding mode | All four operations are exact, and 1 / 3 is exact | All four operations are exact inside one expression, and the result converts to a decimal at the boundary with a declared rounding |
| Numeric range and scale | Fixed and documented: 38 digits, scale 0 to 28 | No scale. A limit on numerator and denominator size must be chosen, and 1/3 is representable | Two limits, one for the internal rational and one for the boundary decimal |
| Rounding modes | Applied at each declared point, so each rounding is visible in the Rule and in the Decision_Trace | Needed only where a value leaves the rational form, and an explicit round operation is still needed for rulebooks that round intermediate steps | Both: explicit round operations in expressions and a conversion rule at the boundary |
| Overflow protection | Checked per operation against the fixed limit. Multiplication needs an intermediate of up to 76 digits | Growth is unbounded across a chain of operations on unrelated denominators, so the check follows each normalization | Both checks, and a rule for the conversion between the two |
| Serialization and canonical representation | One canonical decimal string without exponent (Requirement 8 criterion 16) that is lossless in common data formats | Canonical form needs lowest terms, a positive denominator, and a fraction syntax. Many values have no finite decimal form | Decimal in files. Decision_Traces need both forms for exact intermediate values |
| Dependency and performance costs | Small. A hand-rolled scaled integer with wide multiplication or one decimal crate. Fixed-size and fast | A big-integer crate or hand-rolled code with a greatest-common-divisor step per operation. Allocation and slower | The costs of both, and the largest test surface |

Earlier recommendation (superseded as the leading proposal by the reassessment below and kept for comparison): use the bounded decimal. The reasons were these. Published competition scores and money amounts are decimals. Regulations that state a rounding usually state it for a specific step, so declared per-step rounding reproduces the published procedure and makes each rounding auditable in the Decision_Trace, which the success definition requires. The fixed range gives a checked, testable overflow rule. The single canonical string gives lossless serialization and stable hashes (Requirement 25). The dependency and test costs are the lowest of the three. The accepted costs are these: 1/3 is not exact, a chain with several divisions needs a declared rounding at each division, and (a / b) * b is not always equal to a. Range: coefficient up to 38 decimal digits, scale 0 to 28. Addition, subtraction, and multiplication are exact or Indeterminate. Division always carries a declared scale and rounding mode. Rounding modes are half-even, half-away-from-zero, and toward-zero. Overflow is checked and never wraps.

Remaining open questions for the user: whether any target rulebook needs exact fractions across several divisions without an intermediate rounding (in which case the hybrid becomes preferable and Requirements 3, 4, 8, and 14 change), whether the limits of 38 digits and scale 28 are acceptable, and, in the design phase, whether the implementation is a decimal crate or a hand-rolled scaled integer. The design must justify that last choice by the six dimensions and the differential test of Requirement 20 criterion 22, and not by convenience. The choice must be confirmed before Rule_IR is finalized.

### Exact_Number reassessment (UD-3, 2026-11-14)

Status: the reassessment is documented, and the choice is pending user confirmation. The user's preferred direction is Option R: bounded rational internal arithmetic with decimal Rule_IR literals and canonical serialization. Nothing in this section is final.

Bounds under Option R, stated for both sub-options:

| Item | Option R-128 (hand-rolled signed 128-bit integers) | Option R-Big (bounded big integers) | Option D (bounded decimal) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Range | Numerator magnitude and denominator each at most 2^127 - 1 (about 1.7 x 10^38) | Numerator magnitude and denominator each at most 2^256 - 1 (about 1.2 x 10^77) | Coefficient of 38 digits, scale 0 to 28 |
| Decimal literal capacity | A 38-digit literal with scale 28 fits, because 10^38 - 1 is below 2^127 | Fits with large margin | Fits by definition |
| Intermediate width needed | 256 bits for products and sums of two bounded values (criterion 8.23) | 513 bits or more | 76 digits for products |
| Normalization | Lowest terms by greatest common divisor, positive denominator, zero as 0/1 | Same | Trailing zeros removed, zero as 0 |
| Division | Exact. Division by zero gives Indeterminate | Exact. Division by zero gives Indeterminate | Needs a declared scale and rounding mode. Division by zero gives Indeterminate |
| Overflow | Checked on every step, including gcd normalization and cross-multiplication. Indeterminate, never wrap | Same, with bounds checked on the normalized result | Checked per operation against 38 digits and scale 28 |
| Rounding | Only at an explicit rounding point with scale 0 to 28 and a declared mode. Comparisons use exact rationals | Same | Only at declared points, and every division is one |
| Canonical output | Decimal for a terminating value, canonical fraction n/d otherwise | Same | Decimal always |
| Hand-rolled cost | A 256-bit multiply and gcd to write and test, no dependency | Not applicable | A 76-digit multiply to write and test, no dependency |
| Library cost | None | The num-bigint and num-rational crates (Rust num project) bring a dependency tree and allocation. Their determinism is integer arithmetic, and no_std support and maintenance status must be verified at design time and justified under Requirement 21 criterion 5 | The rust_decimal crate has a 96-bit coefficient and a scale of 0 to 28, which is below the 38-digit bound and rounds on some scale overflow unless every checked operation is used, so it needs a written justification and checked-operation wrappers. Arbitrary-precision decimal crates are the alternative |

Normalization, division, overflow, rounding, and display for Option R follow criteria 8.21 to 8.25. A non-terminating rational such as 1/3 is displayed and serialized as the canonical fraction 1/3 in Outcomes, Decision_Traces, and the Compiled_Rulebook, and it is never written as a rounded decimal unless a Rule declares the rounding.

Published result with a non-terminating computed value: when a weighted score is mathematically 1/3 and the Published_Result states 0.33, the comparison is exact (criterion 8.26). Under Option R the values differ and the report states that the computed value is non-terminating and that the Rule declares no rounding or a rounding that yields another value. The rulebook author makes them equal only by declaring the rounding that the publishing body used, at a declared scale and mode, in the Rule_IR. No stage rounds to match a published value. Under Option D the Rule necessarily declares the rounding at each division, so the computed value is already a decimal, and the comparison reports a difference only when the declared rounding differs from the published procedure.

Proposed recommendation (proposed, pending user confirmation): adopt Option R-128 as the starting point because it follows the user's preferred direction, keeps division and weighted scores exact without intermediate rounding, and needs no dependency, and then widen to R-Big only if the target rulebooks need larger denominators. Residual risks: an author must declare a final rounding wherever a published decimal must be reproduced, chains of unrelated denominators can reach the bound, the fraction form adds a syntax to the trace serializers and parsers, and the hand-rolled 256-bit arithmetic needs the differential test of Requirement 20 criterion 22. Option D remains a valid fallback and is fully specified.

UD-3 impact table (criteria that change with the final choice):

| Criterion | Option D | Option R |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Glossary Exact_Number, Exact_Number_Limits | Coefficient 38 digits, scale 0 to 28 | Numerator and denominator bounds |
| 3.6, 4.4, 8.7, 8.27 | Literal syntax and limits (38 digits, scale 28) | Identical |
| 8.4, 8.20 | A division needs a declared scale and mode | Only a rounding operation needs a declaration |
| 8.5, 8.11 | Range and scale limits | Numerator and denominator bounds |
| 8.13, 8.21 | Round the quotient once at the declared scale | Exact quotient, no rounding |
| 8.16, 8.24, 8.25 | One decimal serialization | Decimal or canonical fraction, and fraction parsing only in serialized artifacts |
| 8.22, 8.23 | Not applicable | Normalization and overflow of the rational |
| 8.26, 13.12 | Exact comparison with a published decimal | Same, with the non-terminating statement |
| 13.11 | Replay parses decimals | Replay parses decimals and fractions |
| 20.22 | Differential test at 38 digits and scale 28 | Differential test at the numerator and denominator bounds |
| R-7 | Digit and scale limits may reject large values | Denominator growth may reject chains of fractions |

### Risks

- **R-1 Interpretation risk**: Humans may encode a rule differently from the source clause. Mitigation: mandatory Source_Spans, Reviewer approval, and displaying source text beside each decision.
- **R-2 Scope creep**: Requests to support more rulebook styles, or prize allocation and bracket features, may erode the support boundary. Mitigation: Requirements 14, 15, 31, and 32, the source scan of 31.10, the field-set test of 32.5, and Unsupported Diagnostics.
- **R-3 False confidence**: A passing validation may be read as proof that the Rulebook matches the regulations. Mitigation: documentation that validation checks internal consistency only, that human approval establishes fidelity, and the list of checked Conflict classes in the Validation_Result (Requirement 6 criterion 23).
- **R-4 Extraction gap**: Rule authoring effort is high without automatic extraction. Mitigation: the Candidate_Clause list and the optional AI-assisted proposal pipeline of Requirement 23, whose Proposals need human approval.
- **R-5 Undetected conflicts**: Conflicts outside the decidable subset (UD-7) are not reported by validation. These are Rules with different Targets that contradict each other in meaning, expression overlaps that need satisfiability reasoning, and Rules that diverge from their source clause. Only the case in which two Rules produce incompatible results for one Target is reported at execution, as Indeterminate under Requirement 12 criterion 5. Mitigation: Reviewer approval and the documented list in Requirement 6 criterion 22.
- **R-6 Determinism drift**: Dependency upgrades or platform differences may change output. Mitigation: locked dependencies, Fixtures with golden outputs, and the platform matrix of Requirement 21 criterion 10.
- **R-7 Exact_Number limits**: The limits of the selected UD-3 option (38 digits and scale 28 under Option D, numerator and denominator bounds under Option R) may reject legitimate values. Mitigation: rejection is explicit and reported as a Diagnostic, and UD-3 awaits user confirmation.
- **R-8 Unauthenticated approval**: An Approval_Record proves that content matches a hash, not who approved it. A person who can write the Approval_Ledger can append a record that names any Reviewer, including a record that approves an AI-origin Rule_Revision. The refusal of a Reviewer identity that equals an AI identity (Requirement 23 criterion 15) is a string comparison. Mitigation: no AI_Crate can call the approval function or write the Approval_Ledger (Requirement 18 criterion 13, Requirement 27 criterion 4). Authentication is deferred, and users must control access to Rulebook files by other means.
- **R-9 Caller-supplied sources**: Provenance verification depends on the caller supplying the correct Source_Models. Mitigation: the Explainer compares each Source_Model hash with the hash recorded in the Compiled_Rulebook and reports a mismatch.
- **R-10 Prompt injection and hallucinated provenance**: A Source_Document may contain text that instructs an AI, and a model may invent spans or quotes. Mitigation: the Proposal_Schema excludes status and approval fields, every span and quote is verified against the Source_Models, nothing is repaired, and a human Reviewer approves (Requirements 18 and 23).
- **R-11 Disclosure of rulebook text**: Sending Clause_Context to a hosted AI_Provider transmits rulebook text to a third party. Mitigation: AI is disabled by default, transmission needs explicit configuration and a per-invocation consent, and a disclosure and a Transmission_Log entry are written before each request (Requirement 23 criteria 21 and 24, Requirement 28 criteria 7 and 8).
- **R-12 Non-reproducible proposals**: A real AI_Provider may return different Proposals for the same request. Mitigation: only the stored Proposal_Store entries, with request and response hashes, matter for audit, and the deterministic core never reads an AI_Provider (Requirement 18).
- **R-13 Reviewer fatigue**: A Reviewer may approve a plausible Proposal without checking the source. Mitigation: the Review_Group shows the source text beside each Proposal and approves one Rule_Revision per invocation (Requirement 23 criteria 12 and 13). The risk remains.
- **R-14 Ledger rewrite**: The hash chain of the Approval_Ledger detects the edit of an earlier Ledger_Record but not a complete rewrite of the file by a person with write access. Mitigation: access control on the ledger by other means, and R-8 stays open.
- **R-15 Third-party provider**: Sending clause text to the provider (the Meta Model API or the OpenAI service) transmits rulebook text to a third party. Mitigation: public documents for the demonstration, an explicit per-invocation Transmission_Consent naming the documents, a Transmission_Log without document text, and the provider retention parameter of Requirement 28 criterion 13.
- **R-16 Published decimals under Option R**: A non-terminating computed score never equals a published decimal unless the Rule declares the rounding. Mitigation: criterion 8.26 reports the difference explicitly.
- **R-17 Demonstration overclaim**: A successful demonstration over one real rulebook may be read as support for arbitrary rulebooks, as proof that the Rulebook matches the regulation (R-3), or as the full Prize Check and Bracket Check applications. Mitigation: Requirement 34 (required statements, prohibited phrases, documentation scan), the Coverage_Statement (33.6), and the unchanged First_Version_Support_Boundary.
- **R-18 Hidden unsupported clauses**: A demonstration could look complete because unsupported or ambiguous clauses were omitted or reworded. Mitigation: the Coverage_Statement test (33.6), the ban on altering demonstration text (33.4), and the recorded conversion (33.5).
- **R-19 Demonstration drift**: A runbook, a recorded response, or a golden file can drift from the code. Mitigation: the Demo_Runbook commands are executed by the acceptance test (33.12), the hashes of the recorded responses are checked (33.8), and the acceptance test is a required release criterion (20.26, 33.16).
- **R-20 Rank-based qualification gap**: The existing operations cannot express a condition on a Rank_Position (UD-32). Mitigation: the Unsupported Diagnostic under Threshold_Only (14.13), or the single added operation under Rank_Reference (14.12). Rank_Reference selected (UD-32).
- **R-21 Re-sent request after a gateway timeout (UD-37)**: a retry of an HTTP 504 response would re-send byte-identical request bytes (criterion 16) while a reasoning provider may still be generating or billing the first answer. Mitigation: OD-37-1 is resolved by the user's decision of 2026-11-16 (option B): Requirement 29 criterion 2 does not retry a 504 for any adapter, and the tests of Requirement 35 criterion 14 and Requirement 29 criterion 14 assert one attempt, no waiting time and exit code 5. A provider that returns another status for the same condition (for example a 503) is still retried, which this decision does not change.

### Supersession Record

This record lists every earlier statement that the AI-pipeline, lifecycle, contract, provenance, and approval-ledger revisions changed, and the reason. Each entry names the criterion that changed and the criterion or requirement that replaces its effect.

- **Introduction (AI as interface only), Scope (out of scope: any AI implementation, provider integration, network call; in scope: AI_Boundary as interface only), Deferred list (Proposal storage, review workflow, revisions from Proposals, approval command)**: superseded by the user's instruction that the AI pipeline is part of the initial product. Replaced by Requirements 18, 23, and 25. The prohibitions on AI approving, compiling, or executing rules and on AI in the deterministic core are kept.
- **Requirement 18 criterion 10 (no provider implementation, no network call)**: superseded because a real provider integration is required. The core prohibition is kept and narrowed to Core_Crates and the CLI, and the network call is permitted only in an AI_Integration_Crate while AI is explicitly enabled.
- **Requirement 18 criterion 1 (output identical with and without an AI component)**: kept and extended to the case where an AI_Configuration or credential exists.
- **Requirement 18 criterion 11 and Requirement 5 criterion 6 (in-place content change of an Approved Rule)**: kept, and made precise by Requirement 25 criteria 8 and 10: a content change makes the Approval_Record non-matching, and the lawful change path is a new Proposed Rule_Revision.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 2 and Requirement 6 criteria 7 and 16 to 21 (Approved Rules)**: narrowed to Effective_Revisions by Requirement 5 criterion 13 and Requirement 25 criterion 15, so that a superseded Approved Rule_Revision is not compiled and does not create a false Conflict. This is a clarification of the earlier text, not a weakening: every Approved Rule_Revision that was compiled before is still compiled unless a higher Approved Rule_Revision supersedes it.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 12 (no CLI command performs a transition)**: kept for the CLI. A transition is performed only by the Review_Tool, which is a separate executable, and no AI_Crate can call the transition function. The Review_Tool is required because human review is a first-version feature. (Earlier entry, superseded by the 2026-11-14 entries below.)
- **Requirements 3 criterion 3, 3 criterion 9, and 4 criteria 1 and 7 (unique Rule identifier)**: changed to uniqueness of the pair of Rule identifier and Revision_Number, because revisions share an identifier.
- **Requirement 9 criterion 5 (Rulebook content hash)**: made precise so that the hash excludes Origin, Approval_Records, and non-effective Rule_Revisions, which keeps the Compiled_Rulebook independent of Origin.
- **Requirement 16 criterion 15 (exactly these exit codes)**: kept for the CLI only. Criterion 16 gives the codes 5 and 6 and the other codes of the AI_Tool and Review_Tool. Exit code 2 for AI_DISABLED and AI_CONFIG_INVALID extends invalid input to AI command usage errors, as criterion 16 states. (Earlier entry, superseded by the 2026-11-14 entries below.)
- **Requirement 16 criterion 9 and Requirement 17 criterion 4 (write no partial result on failure)**: extended by one exception, Requirement 13 criterion 18, which writes complete Outcomes marked not verified when only provenance verification fails. The exception does not present any Outcome as verified.
- **Requirement 17 criterion 1 (exactly four commands)**: kept. The Review_Tool and the AI_Tool are separate executables and are not commands of the CLI. (Earlier entry, superseded by the 2026-11-14 entries below.)
- **Requirement 21 criterion 8 (no crate opens a network listener)**: one exception for loopback stub servers in AI_Integration_Crate test code, so that the real provider integration is tested without external network access.
- **Introduction (each stage consumes the previous stage's output only; no shared mutable state)**: replaced by the Pipeline table and Requirement 24, which declare every typed input and output of every stage. The prohibition on undocumented shared state, mutable globals, and implicit environmental inputs is kept and made verifiable.
- **Requirement 13 criterion 13 (no Source_Model supplied for rendering)**: kept, and tied to the Warning PROV_NOT_VERIFIED_NO_SOURCES and exit code 0. Requirement 16 criterion 8 (a supplied Source_Model that is missing for a document or whose hash differs gives exit code 2) is kept and tied to the Errors of Requirement 13 criterion 16.
- **UD-3 (resolved with recommendation)**: changed to recommended, pending user confirmation, as the user requested. Requirement 8 is unchanged in substance and gains criteria 17 to 19.
- **Requirement 6 criterion 25 (validate Approved and Proposed Rules in full)**: narrowed by Requirement 25 criterion 12 so that a superseded Approved Rule_Revision gets only the structural checks of Requirement 3 criteria 4 and 9, and kept in full for every Effective_Revision and every Proposed Rule_Revision.

The following entries record the revision of 2026-11-14 (decisions UD-3 reassessment, UD-6, UD-12, UD-17 to UD-22, and approval items 1 to 8). Each entry names the earlier criterion, the new criterion, the corrected wording, and the reason. The mapping of earlier mnemonics to catalogue identifiers is in Requirement 7 criterion 17.

- **Requirement 17 criterion 1 (exactly four pipeline commands) and Requirement 5 criterion 12 (no CLI command performs a transition; the Review_Tool is the only executable that does)**: superseded by UD-22. New wording: exactly four pipeline commands plus the non-pipeline review command group with the subcommands list, approve, and reject (17.1), and the Review_Group is the only code path that appends to the Approval_Ledger (5.12). Reason: human approval must be an explicit operation of the main workflow. The earlier prohibition that no AI_Crate can approve is kept and is enforced by crate dependency (27.4).
- **Review_Tool (Introduction, Scope, Glossary, Requirements 5.12, 16.16, 16.17, 16.18, 19.8, 23.12 to 23.16, 24.1, 24.4, 24.5, 24.8)**: retired. Replaced by Review_Group. The review library crate is a Core_Crate, not an Auxiliary_Crate. Reason: UD-22.
- **Requirement 23 criterion 16 (the Review_Tool creates a revision)**: changed. The Review_Group has no revise subcommand, and a revision is created by editing the Rulebook (25.10) or by ruletrace-ai propose. Reason: the user fixed the review group as list, approve, reject.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 1 (the Rule_IR records an Approval_Status for every Rule) and Requirement 25 criterion 16 (a stored status disagrees with a matching record)**: changed to a derived Approval_Status (5.1, 26.10) with the contradiction rule moved to ledger and lifecycle-flag checks (22.17, 25.16). Reason: UD-6 puts the records in a separate ledger, and a stored status next to a derived one would be an independent lifecycle flag.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 4 (an Approved Rule with no matching Approval_Record is an Error) and Requirement 25 criterion 11 (the Error of 5.4 for the Effective_Revision)**: changed. A Rule_Revision whose approval became non-matching is derived Proposed and is in an Unresolved_Approval_State with the Error APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED, and the stale record gets the Warning APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE. Both still block compilation and both still forbid a fallback to a lower revision, so the protection is not weakened. Reason: the derived status cannot be Approved without a matching record.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 5 (Warning for each Proposed or Rejected Rule)**: superseded by approval decision 4. New wording: Error APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED for an Unresolved_Approval_State (5.5), and the Warning APPROVAL_REVISION_REJECTED for a Rejected Rule_Revision (5.14), which keeps the earlier visibility of rejected Rules.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 7 (an empty Compiled_Rulebook with a Warning when no Rule is Approved)**: superseded by approval decision 7. New wording: Error APPROVAL_NO_EXECUTABLE_RULES and no Compiled_Rulebook (5.7, 9.13). Reason: an empty successful artifact could be mistaken for a complete one.
- **Requirement 25 criterion 13 (a Proposed higher revision keeps the Effective_Revision executable, with a Warning)**: superseded by approval decision 4 and UD-12. New wording: the unresolved higher revision blocks compilation (25.13). Reason: a Rulebook is not complete while a revision awaits a decision.
- **Requirement 5 criterion 8 (no transition out of Approved) and Requirement 25 criterion 8**: kept. A content, source binding, or IR_Version change is not a transition. It makes the old Approval_Records non-matching and the derived status Proposed (5.15). The binding of the IR_Version and the document hashes is explicit in 25.6, 25.8, and 26.7 to 26.9 (approval items 2 and 3).
- **Requirement 25 criteria 6 and 7 (Approval_Record naming the Rule_Content_Hash only)**: extended to bind the IR_Version and the document hashes. Reason: approval item 2.
- **Requirement 9 criterion 11 (Compiler accepts only a Validation_Result)**: kept, with the Validation_Result now carrying the Approval_Ledger and the Compiler re-deriving approvals (9.12). Reason: atomic compilation must not trust the Diagnostic_State alone.
- **Requirement 9 criterion 10 and Requirement 7 criteria 6 and 7 (atomic compilation)**: kept and extended to subsets that omit unresolved revisions.
- **Requirement 22 criterion 2 (blocking set)**: kept in substance and made explicit that APPROVAL_UNRESOLVED and the other approval Errors are in the set, because they have Severity Error (22.2, 22.18). No new Severity was created.
- **Requirement 22 criterion 16 (reject a Provider_Response under 22.9 and 22.10, exit 2)**: changed. A Provider_Response that supplies a forbidden field is rejected as a whole with AI_RESPONSE_FORBIDDEN_FIELD and exit 6, while the other inputs of 22.16 keep exit 2. Reason: a Provider_Response is untrusted data of a protocol, not an input file, and the stage order of 16.17 puts it at response validation.
- **Requirement 23 criterion 10 (reject the batch when any check of criteria 5 to 9 fails, which included semantic validation)**: narrowed by UD-20 to criteria 5 to 8. A Semantic_Outcome of criterion 9 is stored (30.2). Reason: the user separates protocol failures from semantic outcomes. A malformed batch is still never partially accepted.
- **Requirement 23 criteria 17 and 18 (Provider_Failure Diagnostics, retry with waiting times, last failure returned)**: superseded by Requirement 29 and UD-19. New wording: retry only Transient_Failures, a fixed 2 and 8 second schedule, a 60 second deadline, a 20 second attempt timeout, and the distinct final codes AI_PROVIDER_RETRY_EXHAUSTED and AI_PROVIDER_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED. The earlier Error for each transient kind became a per-attempt Warning. Reason: exhaustion must be distinguishable.
- **Requirement 23 criterion 21 and UD-18 (credential named by an environment variable or a file path)**: narrowed to an environment variable name only (28.2, 28.4). Reason: UD-18.
- **Requirement 16 criterion 16 (one exit-code table for the AI_Tool and the Review_Tool)**: replaced by the ruletrace-ai table (16.16), the review rows of the table of 16.15, and the stage order of 16.17. Exit code 3 for a semantic validation rejection was removed, because semantic outcomes are not failures (UD-26).
- **Requirement 16 criterion 17 (earliest step)**: extended to the stage orders of all executables, with configuration load, provider call, response parse, and provenance check placed explicitly.
- **Requirement 18 criteria 12, 13, 15, 17 and Requirement 24 criterion 5 (crate dependencies)**: extended to the Approval_Ledger append function, to the dependency tree of the ruletrace binary (27.5), and to Transmission_Consent.
- **Requirement 8 criteria 4, 5, 7, 11, 13, 16, 19 and Requirement 4 criterion 4, Requirement 3 criterion 6, Requirement 20 criterion 22**: reworded so that they hold under both UD-3 options, with the option-specific content moved to criteria 8.20 to 8.27 and tagged UD-3-dependent. The old limits are kept as Option D. Reason: UD-3 is pending.
- **UD-3 (recommended bounded decimal)**: changed to a documented reassessment, pending confirmation, with Option R as the proposed starting point.
- **UD-12, UD-6, UD-17, UD-18, UD-19, UD-20, UD-22**: resolved by the user on 2026-11-14. UD-21 and UD-23 stay recommended with updated text, and UD-26 and UD-27 are new.
- **Diagnostic mnemonics (STALE_APPROVAL_RECORD, PROV_*, AI_*, REVIEW_*)**: kept as mnemonics and bound to the identifiers RT-DGN, RT-PRV, RT-APR, and RT-AI in the catalogue (7.16, 7.17). STALE_APPROVAL_RECORD is renamed APPROVAL_RECORD_STALE.

The following entries record the confirmation round of 2026-11-14 (user-confirmed decisions on UD-21, UD-13, UD-3, the numeric bounds, the incomplete multi-request run, the Decision_Trace with no source references, and the empty-collection aggregation). Each entry names the earlier criterion, the new criterion, and the reason.

- **UD-21 (Recommended-pending) and Requirement 26 criterion 19 with Requirement 23 criterion 13 (adoption on approve)**: UD-21 resolved. The adoption rule of 26.19 and 23.13 is kept and made explicit and testable by the new criteria 26.28 (a Pending_Proposal is separate from the Rulebook and never blocks compilation), 26.29 (a Proposed Rule_Revision inside the Rulebook still blocks under 5.5 and 25.13), 26.30 (adoption yields an Approved Rule_Revision with its Approval_Record and no observable intermediate state), and 26.31 (tests). Reason: the user confirmed adoption on approve and the distinction between the Proposal_Store and the Rulebook.
- **UD-13 (Open) and Requirement 12 criterion 5 (defensive execution-time conflict check)**: UD-13 resolved, criterion 5 retained unchanged. New criteria 12.11 (Diagnostic EXECUTION_CONFLICT_DETECTED, RT-DGN-0004), 12.12 (test with a test-only Compiled_Rulebook), and 16.23 (exit code 4 and never exit code 3) state it. Reason: the user decided to retain the check and to classify its Indeterminate Outcome with exit code 4 of Requirement 16 criterion 11.
- **UD-3 text ("pending user confirmation" with no preference recorded as a status) and Requirement 8 criterion 19, Glossary entries Exact_Number and Exact_Number_Limits**: UD-3 now reads "preferred: bounded rational, pending design documentation". Criterion 8.19 is reworded to require the integer-width selection, normalization, overflow behavior, rounding, and serialization in the design before the choice is final. All UD-3-dependent tags are kept. Reason: the user prefers bounded rational but does not make the choice final before the design documents it.
- **UD-19 (waiting times and ranges Recommended-pending), Requirement 29 criterion 1, Requirement 25 criterion 1, Requirement 28 criteria 2 and 3**: no criterion text changed. The values are marked confirmed in UD-19 and in the new UD-28 (Revision_Number 1 to 4,294,967,295; maximum Candidate_Clauses default 20, range 1 to 100). Reason: user acceptance of the documented assumptions.
- **Requirement 23 criterion 35 (keep earlier batches, stop at first failure) and Requirement 27 criterion 8, Requirement 29 criterion 17, Requirement 30 criteria 1 and 11, Requirement 18 criterion 17 (Proposal_Store byte-identical to its state before the run or invocation)**: 23.35 is kept and extended by 23.36 (Run_Summary with processed, stored, and unprocessed identifiers and the incomplete state), 23.37 (Warning AI_RUN_INCOMPLETE, RT-AI-0306, no completeness claim, exit code of the failing request), 23.38 (tests), and 16.24 (exit code policy). The byte-identical rules of 27.8, 29.17, 30.1, 30.11, and 18.17 now refer to the state before the failing Provider_Request and make the exception for batches that earlier Provider_Requests of the same run kept. 23.35 defines the failed-run store state once, with one further exception: the whole batch file of a request whose directory sync failed after the link (RT-AI-0303, 23.36). 18.17, 23.20, 23.26, 23.33, 23.37 and 27.8 refer to that definition; 29.17, 30.1, 30.8 and 30.11 are unchanged. Reason: the user allowed earlier successful batches to be preserved but requires the run to be marked incomplete, and the earlier whole-run rules contradicted that.
- **Requirement 13 criterion 17 (a Decision_Trace with zero Source_Spans is not verified)**: confirmed unchanged and extended by 13.22 (a Decision_Trace with no Source_Span or whose every Source_Span fails to resolve is not verified, with the Warning PROV_TRACE_NO_SOURCE_SPANS, RT-PRV-0007, for the zero-Source_Span case) and 13.23 (tests). Reason: the user confirmed the intent and asked for the all-failed case to be explicit.
- **Requirement 8 criterion 15 (aggregation result over an empty collection undefined)**: reworded to refer to the new criteria 8.29 (count and sum of an empty collection are 0), 8.30 (minimum and maximum of an empty collection give an Indeterminate Outcome with AGGREGATE_EMPTY_COLLECTION, RT-DGN-0005, when required), and 8.31 (tests). Reason: the user fixed the semantics of the empty collection, and an absent collection stays missing information under Requirement 11.

The following entries record the scope amendment of 2026-11-14 (two thin demonstration Application_Layers and a reproducible end-to-end demonstration, decided by the user). Each entry names the earlier statement, the new criterion, the corrected wording, and the reason. Every safety criterion is kept.

- **Introduction (Future application vision: Prize Check and Bracket Check are future applications; this specification delivers only the shared foundation) and Scope (out of scope: Prize Check and Bracket Check applications)**: superseded. New wording: the first version contains two thin demonstration Application_Layers with input mapping and report layout only (Requirements 31 and 32), and the full applications, prize allocation, slot-based qualification, seeding, bracket generation, progression, and multi-stage competitions stay out of scope. Reason: the user wants to demonstrate that one engine powers two applications.
- **Requirement 14 criterion 5 (the first version does not support qualification)**: changed to "slot-based qualification (the allocation of a limited number of places, slots, or stages)". Qualification as a per-participant condition is supported only as a Qualification_Predicate (14.11), with an optional rank reference (14.12, UD-32). Reason: "qualification" was undefined, and a threshold condition is an eligibility predicate already inside the five Rule kinds. No capability beyond the five Rule kinds is added unless the user selects Rank_Reference.
- **Requirement 14 criterion 2 (exactly these expression operations)**: unchanged. WHERE Rank_Reference is selected, 14.12 extends the list by exactly one operation, and 14.6 and 14.8 name criterion 12 so that the extension is explicit. Reason: the user asked to flag the gap and not to add the operation silently.
- **Requirement 15 criterion 8 (no Prize_Check crate, no Bracket_Check crate, and no code for prize allocation, qualification, seeding, bracket generation, or progression)**: superseded. New wording: exactly two Application_Layer crates and no code in any crate for prize allocation, slot-based qualification, seeding, bracket generation, or progression. Reason: the user decided to add the two thin layers. Criteria 15.1, 15.5, 15.6, and 15.7 are kept unchanged and become testable through 31.4, 31.5, 31.10, 31.11, and 24.12. Criterion 15.10 is new.
- **Requirement 27 criterion 1 and the Requirement 27 user story (exactly two executables, and no third)**: superseded. New wording: exactly two RuleTrace executables, ruletrace and ruletrace-ai, plus exactly two Demonstration_Executables, prize-check and bracket-check, and no other executable (27.1, 27.15). Reason: UD-33 (recommended: separate small executables, which keeps the applications independent and leaves the dependency trees of ruletrace and ruletrace-ai unchanged). All other criteria of Requirement 27 apply to ruletrace and ruletrace-ai unchanged.
- **UD-22 (two executables)**: amended by UD-33 as stated in the register.
- **Requirement 28 criterion 11 (public documents only in the initial demonstration)**: kept and extended by 28.21 and 33.2 to 33.5 (manifest, license, conversion record, consent). Reason: the demonstration now uses real documents and needs recorded permission of use.
- **Glossary entries Prize_Check and Bracket_Check (future application, not part of the first version)**: changed to the thin first-version Application_Layers, with the full applications remaining future. New entries: Application_Layer, Application_Input_Format, Demonstration_Executable, Qualification_Predicate, Standings, Demo_Corpus, Coverage_Statement, Recorded_Provider_Script, Demo_Runbook, Proof_Report. Reason: terms of Requirements 31 to 34.
- **Requirement 20 (the required tests)**: kept without removal. New criteria 20.26 and 20.27 make the demonstration acceptance test a first-class release criterion next to the required safety tests (determinism, isolation, provenance, approval gating, diagnostic state, no network in the core, exact numeric). Reason: the user's reprioritisation. The number of property tests is not itself a goal, and no criterion is weakened.
- **Requirement 22 (contradictory diagnostic states)**: kept unchanged. New criterion 22.22 applies it to the Application_Input_Format files, the published-result files, the Recorded_Provider_Script, the Demo_Corpus manifest, and the Proof_Report. Reason: the steering rule that contradictory flags are rejected as invalid input applies to every new input kind.
- **Requirement 24 criterion 5 (allowed-dependency check)**: kept. New criterion 24.12 adds the allowed edges of the Application_Layers and forbids their dependency on the CLI binary crate, the review and ledger crates, the Validator, Compiler, Ingestor, Extractor, any Auxiliary_Crate, and any network client.
- **Requirement 7 (Diagnostic_Catalogue)**: new criterion 7.23 adds the codes RANK_REFERENCE_CYCLE and APP_* to the catalogue.
- **Requirement 16 criterion 9 and Requirement 17 criterion 4 (a failed command creates no file at its output location), RN-29**: two further exceptions, both for the validate command. The validate command writes the Validation_Result when only blocking Diagnostics prevent success, so that the compile command can return those Diagnostics unchanged (Requirement 9 criterion 2) and exit with code 3 (Requirement 33 criterion 17). The artifact is not a Compiled_Rulebook and is not executable, and the compile command refuses it. Reason: user decision RN-29, option (a). Record: `tools/decisions/RN-29.md`.

- **Requirement 16 criterion 12 (a failure to write the output location exits with code 70 and never with 2, 3, or 4)**: narrowed to the case that no earlier stage failed, and the prohibition on codes 2, 3, and 4 now applies to that failure alone. Reason: criteria 16.14 and 16.17 make the code of the earliest failing stage decide and place the output write last, so a blocking validation result (exit 3) with a failed write exits 3, and the write failure is still written as an additional Diagnostic to standard error with no partial file left. Found by the property test `exit_code_stage_order` (task 5.26). A failed write on an otherwise successful run still exits 70.

- **Requirement 13 criterion 18 (a provenance Error exits with code 2)**: narrowed to the case that no earlier stage of Requirement 16 criterion 17 failed. An Indeterminate Outcome (stage 6, exit code 4) outranks a stage 7 provenance Error, so a run with both exits 4. New criterion 13.24 requires a test of the simultaneous case. Design §3.5, §6.1 and §8.4 (application exit derivation) are corrected to match. Reason: 13.18 contradicted the earliest-stage rule of 16.17 and Property 30. Nothing is presented as verified in either case, and the complete Outcomes and Decision_Traces are still written.

- **Requirement 23 criterion 25 and Requirement 30 criterion 1 (no raw Provider_Response is kept; a rejected batch stores nothing)**: narrowed by UD-36 (`tools/decisions/UD-36.md`, RN-27). No raw response is kept except the accepted response payload that the Proposal_Store retains. Accepted means accepted by the protocol, schema, and provenance checks and not approved by a human reviewer. A rejected response is never stored. Credentials, authorization headers, and unnecessary transport data are never stored. Reason: the confirmed option 1 lets `rt-xtask export-script` write the Recorded_Provider_Script from the stored responses without another provider request.
- **UD-34 (Open)**: Confirmed by the user, with the two documents, scope and snapshot rules recorded in `tools/decisions/UD-34.md`. UD-32 is not decided by it (gate 1.11, Rank_Reference recommended). The statement of the register that no document is named is narrowed to refer to that record.
- **Requirement 26 criterion 19 (every file is byte-identical to its earlier state when any write fails)**: amended by the user decision of 2026-11-14 after the second review of task 7.3. A rollback can itself fail, and no implementation can then promise byte identity. The criterion now distinguishes (a) a successful rollback, (b) a rollback failure after both new files are in place, and (c) a rollback failure in an intermediate state, and forbids describing an intermediate pair as consistent. New criteria 26.32 to 26.36 add the refusal of every reader while a journal exists, recovery before any load, the exit-0 cleanup case, the fault-injection tests, and the documentation of a torn marker. Criterion 26.30 is unchanged and stays absolute. Reason: the user decision; a journal-aware reader refusal makes the intermediate pair unobservable without weakening 26.30.
- **Requirement 26 criterion 32 (reader protocol, third review of task 7.3)**: amended after the third review, which showed that a journal check before the read does not close the window of a reader that straddles a failed commit and its rollback: the reader then holds the adopted Rulebook and the old ledger. The user decision keeps criterion 26.30 absolute, so the criterion now defines the reader protocol (shared lock, journal check, read, journal check, second read, byte comparison) and the one file-system condition it relies on (design §3.8.3 (g)). Criterion 26.35 adds the tests of that window. The first sentence no longer says that a reader reads no file other than the journal in every case, because a reader that passes the first check must read the files. Reason: the user decision of 2026-11-14 that 26.30 is not narrowed.
- **RT-APR-0211 as the warning of a reader that finds a journal (design §3.8.3, task 7.3)**: superseded for readers by RT-APR-0214 (Error, exit code 2). RT-APR-0211 remains a Warning with exit code 0 for a completed commit whose cleanup failed and for a report of a finished recovery. Exit code 2 follows UD-14 (the files named on the command line are not usable input) and not 3, which UD-14 reserves for Blocking_Diagnostics of the Rulebook and for refused review actions, and not 70, which Requirement 16 criterion 12 reserves for failures that are not invalid input. The catalogue changed without a released baseline, so no renumbering or removal occurred (Requirement 7 criterion 11 and 12).
- **Requirement 26 criteria 32 and 37 (fail closed without advisory locks, fourth review of task 7.3)**: amended by the user decision of 2026-11-14. Criterion 32 had taken the shared review lock only WHEN the file system has one, which left a residual case on a file system without advisory locks: a reader suspended across two further failed commits of identical bytes could read a mixed pair. That residual risk is removed, not accepted. A reader or review command that cannot take its lock because locking is unsupported now refuses with the new Error REVIEW_LOCKING_UNSUPPORTED (RT-APR-0215, exit code 70) and reads and changes nothing. The earlier `create_new` lock-file fallback (design §3.8.3 step 1, UD-15 section 3, risk R-D4) is removed for writers and readers. Criterion 26.30 therefore no longer has a lock-free residual case: a command either holds the lock or refuses. Exit code 70 follows UD-14, which classifies lock failure as 70, and not 2, which stays with the content-related refusals RT-APR-0214 of readers. RT-APR-0214 now covers a journal, a lock held by a writer and a pair that changed between the two reads. The catalogue changed without a released baseline, so no renumbering or removal occurred (Requirement 7 criterion 11 and 12).
- **UD-32 (Confirmed by the user, constraints added)**: the user confirmed Rank_Reference (`tools/decisions/UD-32.md`, section "User constraints (confirmed)") with two constraints. Requirement 14 criterion 12 gains clause (g), so a rank reference is used only where an applicable competition rule explicitly declares it, with the Source_Spans of the declaring Rule as provenance. Requirement 14 criteria 15 to 25 and the Glossary entry External_Ranking_Value are new and keep an outside ranking separate from the rank reference. No criterion was renumbered, removed or weakened; the earlier entry that Rank_Reference was applied under the gate rule and awaited confirmation is superseded by the confirmation. Reason: the user decision. The new codes EXTERNAL_RANKING_DECLARATION_INVALID and EXTERNAL_RANKING_POSITION_INVALID are named by mnemonic and get their numbers in the Diagnostic_Catalogue under Requirement 7 criteria 11 and 12.
- **Requirement 23 criterion 29, Requirement 28 criteria 2, 8, 12, and 17, Requirement 27 criteria 1, 2, and 11, Requirement 33 criteria 8 and 10, and the Scope line on provider integration (UD-37)**: reworded. The provider kind of the AI_Configuration becomes a provider identifier and a protocol (version 1 files are mapped in memory, Requirement 35 criterion 9). The first real provider is the Meta Model API over the Responses protocol, in one crate that also serves the OpenAI profile. ruletrace-ai gains the command `models`. Nothing is weakened: every earlier check keeps its code and exit code, and Requirement 29 criterion 2 is unchanged. Task 10.4 (the flat Proposal schema, Requirement 23 criterion 39) is no longer optional.

The following entry records the scope amendment of 2026-11-18 (UD-38, decided by the user). Every safety criterion is kept. The old and new wording of every amended criterion is in `traceability.md`, section "UD-38 amendments (2026-11-18)".

- **Introduction, Scope, Glossary, Requirements 7, 24, 27, 31, 32, 33, and 34, and new Requirements 36 and 37 (UD-38)**: the RuleTrace_Laboratory is added as the one permitted local user interface; the Runtime_Executables become ruletrace, ruletrace-ai, and ruletrace-lab; prize-check and bracket-check become library Workflow_Modules; the Demonstration_Executable term is superseded. Reason: the user changed the submission category to Frankenstein and decided one integrated local application. Kept: every no-business-logic, byte-identity, parameter-from-compiled-rulebook, trust-boundary, credential, ledger, and deferral criterion, including the unsatisfied 9.3-dependent criteria of Requirement 32.
